E^{K}_{\ell_{z}}(\rho,z)=E^{K}_{\ell_{z}}(0,z)+\rho\partial_{\rho}E^{K}_{\ell_{z}}(0,z)+O(\rho^{2}).

We compute:

E^{K}_{\ell_{z}}(0,z)=2d\ell_{z}\frac{r_{H}}{\tilde{\Pi}(0,z)}=\frac{d\ell_{z}}{2r_{H}}

and

\partial_{\rho}E^{K}_{\ell_{z}}(0,z)=\frac{1}{X_{\mathscr{H}}(0,z))}\sqrt{\ell_{z}^{2}+X_{\mathscr{H}}(0,z)}+2d\ell_{z}\frac{\partial_{\rho}r(0,z)\tilde{\Pi}(0,z)-r_{H}\partial_{\rho}\tilde{\Pi}(0,z)}{16r_{H}^{4}}.

We have

\partial_{\rho}r(0,z)=0\quad\text{and}\quad\partial_{\rho}\tilde{\Pi}(0,z)=8r_{H}^{2}.

Hence

\partial_{\rho}r(0,z)\tilde{\Pi}(0,z)-r_{H}\partial_{\rho}\tilde{\Pi}(0,z)=-8r_{H}^{2}

and

\partial_{\rho}E^{K}_{\ell_{z}}(0,z)>0.

Now, we choose
\rho>0
such that

\frac{d\ell_{z}}{2r_{H}}+\rho\partial_{\rho}E^{K}_{\ell_{z}}(0,z)<0.

∎

Figure 10:
Shape of
E^{K}_{\ell_{z}}
with
d=0.8
. On the left:
\ell_{z}=1
. On the right:
\ell_{z}=4

4.2.2
Trapped timelike future-directed geodesics

A full classification of timelike future-directed geodesics in Kerr can be achieved using the integrability of the geodesics equation in BL coordinates (see Proposition
13
). A priori, the nature of the orbits then depends also on the Carter constant
Q
. In stationary and axisymmetric spacetimes, the geodesic motion does not necessarily form an integrable Hamiltonian system since there is no generalisation of
Q
and there are only three constants of motion
E
,
L_{z}
and
m
.

However, since only an open set of trapped non-spherical orbits is relevant to our work, we will determine sufficient conditions on
(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
which are independent of
Q
and on the precise initial position
(\rho(0),z(0))
for a trajectory to be trapped. This will allow us to construct an open subset of parameters
(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
on which the distribution function
f
will be supported.

We begin by recalling from Proposition
13
the necessary and sufficient conditions for a timelike future-directed orbit to be trapped and non-spherical.

Let
\gamma:I\ni 0\to\mathcal{O}
be a timelike future-directed geodesic with constants of motion
(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
and let
\tilde{\gamma}
its projection in
\mathscr{B}
. Then
\gamma
is trapped if and only if one of the following cases occur:

1.

\displaystyle\varepsilon\in]\varepsilon^{+}_{min},\varepsilon^{-}_{min}]
,
\displaystyle\ell_{z}\in]\tilde{\ell}_{min}(\varepsilon),\ell^{+}_{lb}(\varepsilon)[
and

\displaystyle\tilde{\gamma}(0)

\displaystyle\in\left(A^{K,abs}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\cap(]\tilde{\rho}^{+}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),\rho^{K}_{0}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})[\times]\gamma-\tilde{z}^{+}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),\gamma[)\right)

\displaystyle\cup\left(A^{K,abs}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\cap(]\tilde{\rho}^{+}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),\rho^{K}_{0}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})[\times]-\gamma,\gamma+\tilde{z}^{+}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})[)\right),

2.

\displaystyle\varepsilon\in]\varepsilon^{+}_{min},\varepsilon^{-}_{min}]
,
\displaystyle\ell_{z}\in]\ell^{+}_{lb}(\varepsilon),\ell^{+}_{ub}(\varepsilon)[
and
\displaystyle\tilde{\gamma}(0)\in A^{K,trapped}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
,

3.

\displaystyle\varepsilon\in]\varepsilon^{-}_{min},1[
,
\displaystyle\ell_{z}\in]\ell^{-}_{ub}(\varepsilon),\ell^{-}_{lb}(\varepsilon)\cup]\ell^{+}_{lb}(\varepsilon),\ell^{+}_{ub}(\varepsilon)[
and
\displaystyle\tilde{\gamma}(0)\in A^{K,trapped}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
,

4.

\displaystyle\varepsilon\in]\varepsilon^{-}_{min},1[
,
\displaystyle\ell_{z}\in]\ell^{-}_{lb}(\varepsilon),\ell^{+}_{lb}(\varepsilon)[
and

\displaystyle\tilde{\gamma}(0)

\displaystyle\in\left(A^{K,abs}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\cap(]\tilde{\rho}^{1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),\rho^{K}_{0}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})[\times]\gamma-\tilde{z}^{1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),\gamma[)\right)

\displaystyle\cup\left(A^{K,abs}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\cap(]\tilde{\rho}^{1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),\rho^{K}_{0}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})[\times]-\gamma,-\gamma+\tilde{z}^{1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),\pi[)\right),

where

(\tilde{\rho}^{+}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),\tilde{z}^{+}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})):=(\sqrt{\Delta(\tilde{r}^{+}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}))}\sin(\theta_{1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})),(\tilde{r}^{+}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})-1)\cos(\theta_{1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})))

and

(\tilde{\rho}^{1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),\tilde{z}^{1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})):=(\sqrt{\Delta(\tilde{r}^{1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}))}\sin(\tilde{\theta}_{1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})),(\tilde{r}^{1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})-1)\cos(\tilde{\theta}_{1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})))

In particular, if
\gamma
is a timelike future-directed geodesic with constants of motion
(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\in\mathcal{A}_{bound}
, where
\mathcal{A}_{bound}
is defined by (
4.125
) (see Figure
11
), then
\tilde{\gamma}
is either trapped or plunging. Moreover, the zero velocity curve associated to
\gamma
,
Z^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
, has two connected components. Indeed, by Lemma
12
, we have

Z^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})=Z^{K,abs}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\cup Z^{K,trapped}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})

where
Z^{K,abs}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
is diffeomorphic to
\mathbb{R}
and
Z^{K,trapped}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
is diffeomorphic to
\mathbb{S}^{1}
.

In the following section, we will reparameterize
18
18

18

For the sole purpose of the main theorem, we could have constructed an atlas only for
Z^{K,trapped}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
. We also do the analysis of
Z^{K,abs}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})

Z^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
for
(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\in\mathcal{A}_{bound}
.

4.2.3
Reparameterization of the zero velocity curves associated to trapped geodesics

Let
\gamma
be a timelike future-directed geodesic with constants of motion
(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\in\mathcal{A}_{bound}
and let
\tilde{\gamma}
be its projection in
\mathscr{B}
. We recall that the allowed region for
\tilde{\gamma}
has two connected components
A^{K,abs}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
and
A^{K,trapped}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
bounded respectively by
Z^{K,abs}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
and
Z^{K,trapped}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
.

Since
Z^{K,abs}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
and
Z^{K,trapped}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
are one-dimensional manifolds, we will construct atlases
(\Phi^{K,abs}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})},I_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}\subset\mathbb{R})
and
(\Phi^{K,trapped}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),i},I_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),i})_{i=1\cdots i_{0}}
for
Z^{K,abs}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
and
Z^{K,trapped}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
respectively. This will allow us to see locally the solutions of the equation

\varepsilon=E^{K}_{\ell_{z}}(\rho,z).

(4.148)

as the graph of smooth functions. As a consequence, the problem of finding solutions to Equation (
4.148
) on
\mathscr{B}
is equivalent to writing locally
\rho
as a function of
z
or
z
as a function of
\rho
. This representation of the solutions will help us to formulate the stability result for trapped non-spherical timelike future-directed geodesics in the following section.

Figure 11:
The set
\mathcal{A}_{bound}
with
d=0.8
as the union of the two set bounded by the red curve, the blue curve and the black curve. On the left:
\mathcal{A}_{bound}^{+}
. On the right:
\mathcal{A}_{bound}^{-}
.

To this end, we begin with the following lemma

Lemma 37
.

\forall(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\in\mathcal{A}_{bound}
, we have

Z^{K,trapped}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\subset[\rho_{1}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),\rho_{2}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})]\times[-z_{max}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),z_{max}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})]

where
\rho_{i}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
are the two largest roots of the equation (
4.148
) with
z=0
and
z_{max}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
is defined by

z_{max}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}):=\left(\tilde{r}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})-1\right)\cos\overline{\theta}^{<1}_{max}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})

(4.149)

where
\tilde{r}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
is defined in Lemma
33
and
\cos\overline{\theta}^{<1}_{max}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
is given by (
4.114
).

Moreover,
\forall(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\in\mathcal{A}_{bound}
, the equation (
4.148
) with
z=\pm z_{max}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
admits a unique solution
\rho_{max}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
in the region
[\rho_{1}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),\rho_{2}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})]
, given by

\rho_{max}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})=\sqrt{\Delta(\tilde{r}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}))}\sin\overline{\theta}^{<1}_{max}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}).

(4.150)

Proof.

We recall from Proposition
12
that

\displaystyle Z^{K,trapped}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})

\displaystyle=Graph\left(r^{1}_{tr}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},q(\cdot))\right)\cup Graph\left(r^{2}_{tr}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},q(\cdot))\right)

\displaystyle\cup Graph\left(\theta_{tr}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},q(\cdot))\right)\cup Graph\left(\pi-\theta_{tr}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},q(\cdot))\right),

where
r^{1}_{tr}
,
r^{2}_{tr}
and
\theta_{tr}
are given by (
4.120
), (
4.121
) and (
4.122
) respectively. Moreover, the latter functions verify

•

r^{1}_{tr}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},q(\cdot))
has a global minimum at
r_{1}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
and

r^{1}_{tr}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},q(\overline{\theta}_{max}^{<1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})))=r^{1}_{tr}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},q(\pi-\overline{\theta}_{max}^{<1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})))=\tilde{r}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}).

Therefore,

Graph\left(r^{1}_{tr}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},q(\cdot))\right)\subset\left[r_{1}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),\tilde{r}^{+}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\right]\times\left[\overline{\theta}_{max}^{<1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),\pi-\overline{\theta}_{max}^{<1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\right].

•

r^{2}_{tr}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},q(\cdot))
has a global maximum at
r_{2}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
and

r^{2}_{tr}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},q(\overline{\theta}_{max}^{<1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})))=r^{2}_{tr}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},q(\pi-\overline{\theta}_{max}^{<1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})))=\tilde{r}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),

Thus,

Graph\left(r^{2}_{tr}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},q(\cdot))\right)\subset\left[\tilde{r}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),r_{2}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\right]\times\left[\overline{\theta}_{max}^{<1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),\pi-\overline{\theta}_{max}^{<1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\right].

•

\displaystyle\theta_{tr}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},q(\cdot))
has a global maximum at
\displaystyle\overline{\theta}_{max}^{<1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
and

Graph\left(\theta_{tr}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},q(\cdot))\right)\subset\left[r_{1}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),r_{2}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\right]\times\left[0,\overline{\theta}_{max}^{<1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\right].

•

\displaystyle\pi-\theta_{tr}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},q(\cdot))
has a global minimum at
\displaystyle\pi-\overline{\theta}_{max}^{<1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
and

Graph\left(\theta_{tr}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},q(\cdot))\right)\subset\left[r_{1}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),r_{2}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\right]\times\left[0,\pi-\overline{\theta}_{max}^{<1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\right].

Therefore,

\displaystyle Z^{K,trapped}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})

\displaystyle\subset[r_{1}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),r_{2}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})]\times[\overline{\theta}_{max}^{<1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),\pi-\overline{\theta}_{max}^{<1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})],

or if we use the coordinates
(\rho,z)
, we have

Z^{K,trapped}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\subset[\rho_{1}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),\rho_{2}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})]\times[-z_{max}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),z_{max}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})].

∎

Now, let
\mathcal{B}_{bound}\subset\subset\mathcal{A}_{bound}
. We claim that
\mathcal{B}_{bound}
can be included in a finite union of products of closed intervals. More precisely, we have

Lemma 38
.

Let
\mathcal{B}_{bound}\subset\subset\mathcal{A}_{bound}
. Then, there exists a finite number
N
of products of closed intervals
[\varepsilon^{i,\pm}_{1},\varepsilon^{i,\pm}_{2}]\times[\ell^{i,\pm}_{1},\ell^{i,\pm}_{2}]
such that

[\varepsilon^{i,\pm}_{1},\varepsilon^{i,\pm}_{2}]\times[\ell^{i,\pm}_{1},\ell^{i,\pm}_{2}]\subset\mathcal{A}_{bound}^{\pm},

\mathcal{B}_{bound}^{\pm}\subset\bigcup_{i=1}^{N}[\varepsilon^{i,\pm}_{1},\varepsilon^{i,\pm}_{2}]\times[\ell^{i,\pm}_{1},\ell^{i,\pm}_{2}]

and
\displaystyle\varepsilon^{i,\pm}_{j}
and
\displaystyle\ell^{i,\pm}_{j}
satisfy

\varepsilon^{\pm}_{min}<\varepsilon^{i,\pm}_{1}<\varepsilon^{i,\pm}_{2}<1\quad\text{,}\quad\ell_{lb}(\varepsilon^{+}_{2})<\ell^{i,+}_{1}<\ell^{i,+}_{2}<\ell_{ub}(\varepsilon^{+}_{1})\quad\text{and}\quad\ell_{ub}(\varepsilon^{-}_{1})<\ell^{i,-}_{1}<\ell^{i,-}_{2}<\ell_{lb}(\varepsilon^{-}_{2}).

(4.151)

Proof.

By compactness of
\mathcal{B}_{bound}
, there exists a finite number
N
of product closed intervals
[\varepsilon^{i,\pm}_{1},\varepsilon^{i,\pm}_{2}]\times[\ell^{i,\pm}_{1},\ell^{i,\pm}_{2}]
such that

[\varepsilon^{i,\pm}_{1},\varepsilon^{i,\pm}_{2}]\times[\ell^{i,\pm}_{1},\ell^{i,\pm}_{2}]\subset\mathcal{A}_{bound}^{\pm},

(4.152)

\mathcal{B}_{bound}^{\pm}\subset\bigcup_{i=1}^{N}[\varepsilon^{i,\pm}_{1},\varepsilon^{i,\pm}_{2}]\times[\ell^{i,\pm}_{1},\ell^{i,\pm}_{2}].

We need to check that
\displaystyle\varepsilon^{i,\pm}_{j}
and
\displaystyle\ell^{i,\pm}_{j}
satisfy (
5.3
). Let
i\in\left\{1,\dots N\right\}
. Then,

[\varepsilon^{i,\pm}_{1},\varepsilon^{i,\pm}_{2}]\subset]\varepsilon^{+}_{min},1[

and

[\ell^{i,\pm}_{1},\ell^{i,\pm}_{2}]\subset]\ell_{lb}^{+}(\varepsilon),\ell_{ub}^{+}(\varepsilon)[\quad\text{for all}\;\varepsilon\in[\varepsilon^{i,\pm}_{1},\varepsilon^{i,\pm}_{2}].

By monotonicity properties of
\ell_{ub}^{+}
and
\ell_{lb}^{+}
(see Lemma
23
), we have

\displaystyle\ell_{lb}^{+}(\varepsilon^{i,+}_{1})<\ell_{lb}^{+}(\varepsilon)<\ell_{ub}^{+}(\varepsilon^{i,+}_{2})

\displaystyle\ell_{ub}^{+}(\varepsilon^{i,+}_{1})<\ell_{ub}^{+}(\varepsilon)<\ell_{ub}^{+}(\varepsilon^{i,+}_{2}).

Therefore,
\ell_{1}^{i,+}
and
\ell_{2}^{i,+}
verify

\ell_{lb}^{+}(\varepsilon^{i,+}_{1})<\ell_{lb}^{+}(\varepsilon_{2})<\ell_{1}^{i,+}<\ell_{2}^{i,+}<\ell_{lb}^{+}(\varepsilon)<\ell_{ub}^{+}(\varepsilon^{i,+}_{1}).

∎

Remark 20
.

In the remaining of our work, we will suppose that
\mathcal{B}_{bound}
is included in one of the product intervals
\displaystyle[\varepsilon^{i,\pm}_{1},\varepsilon^{i,\pm}_{2}]\times[\ell^{i,\pm}_{1},\ell^{i,\pm}_{2}]
. The general case can be dealt by a partition of unity argument.

We shall henceforth assume that
\mathcal{B}_{bound}
has the following form

\mathcal{B}_{bound}:=\mathcal{B}_{bound}^{-}\cup\mathcal{B}_{bound}^{+}

(4.153)

where
\mathcal{B}_{bound}^{-}
and
\mathcal{B}_{bound}^{+}
are defined by

\mathcal{B}_{bound}^{\pm}:=[\varepsilon_{1}^{\pm},\varepsilon_{2}^{\pm}]\times[\ell_{1}^{\pm},\ell_{2}^{\pm}]

where
\displaystyle\varepsilon^{i,\pm}_{j}
and
\displaystyle\ell^{i,\pm}_{j}
satisfy

\varepsilon^{\pm}_{min}(d)<\varepsilon^{\pm}_{1}<\varepsilon^{\pm}_{2}<1\quad\text{,}\quad\ell_{lb}(\varepsilon^{+}_{2})<\ell^{+}_{1}<\ell^{+}_{2}<\ell_{ub}(\varepsilon^{+}_{1})\quad\text{and}\quad\ell_{ub}(\varepsilon^{-}_{1})<\ell^{-}_{1}<\ell^{-}_{2}<\ell_{lb}(\varepsilon^{-}_{2}).

(4.154)

In the remaining of this section, we omit the
\pm
in order to lighten the expressions. We state the following lemma

Lemma 39
.

Let
(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\in\mathcal{B}_{bound}
and let
\rho^{K}_{i}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),i\in\left\{0,1,2\right\}
be the solutions of the equation

E^{K}_{\ell_{z}}(\rho,0)=\varepsilon

such that

\rho^{K}_{0}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})<\rho^{K}_{1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})<\rho^{K}_{2}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}).

Then,

1.

\forall\varepsilon\in[\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}]
,

•

\displaystyle\rho^{K}_{0}(\varepsilon,\cdot)
decreases monotonically on
[\ell_{1},\ell_{2}]
.

•

\displaystyle\rho^{K}_{1}(\varepsilon,\cdot)
increases monotonically on
[\ell_{1},\ell_{2}]
.

•

\displaystyle\rho^{K}_{2}(\varepsilon,\cdot)
decreases monotonically on
[\ell_{1},\ell_{2}]
.

2.

\forall\ell_{z}\in[\ell_{1},\ell_{2}]
,

•

\displaystyle\rho^{K}_{0}(\cdot,\ell_{z})
decreases monotonically on
[\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}]
.

•

\displaystyle\rho^{K}_{1}(\cdot,\ell_{z})
increases monotonically on
[\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}]
.

•

\displaystyle\rho^{K}_{2}(\cdot,\ell_{z})
decreases monotonically on
[\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}]
.

Proof.

First of all, we recall from Proposition
15
that
E^{K}_{\ell_{z}}(\cdot,0)
admits two critical points: a maximum at
\rho_{s}(\ell_{z})
and a minimum at
\rho_{s}(\ell_{z})
. It follows that

•

E^{K}_{\ell_{z}}(\cdot,0)
is monotonically increasing on
]0,\rho_{s}(\ell_{z})[
,

•

E^{K}_{\ell_{z}}(\cdot,0)
is monotonically decreasing on
]\rho_{s}(\ell_{z}),\rho_{m}(\ell_{z})[
,

•

E^{K}_{\ell_{z}}(\cdot,0)
is monotonically increasing on
]\rho_{m}(\ell_{z}),\infty[
.

Moreover,
\forall\rho>0
,
E^{K}_{\cdot}(\rho,0)
is monotonically increasing on
]\ell_{1}^{+},\ell_{2}^{+}[
. Furthermore, we have

\rho^{K}_{0}(\varepsilon,\cdot)<\rho_{s}(\ell_{z})<\rho^{K}_{1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})<\rho_{m}(\ell_{z})<\rho^{K}_{2}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})

and

\frac{\partial\rho^{K}_{i}}{\partial\varepsilon}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})=\frac{1}{\frac{\partial E^{K}_{\ell_{z}}}{\partial\rho}(\rho^{K}_{i}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),0)}\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{\partial\rho^{K}_{i}}{\partial\ell_{z}}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})=-\frac{\frac{\partial E^{K}_{\ell_{z}}}{\partial\ell_{z}}(\rho^{K}_{i}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),0)}{\frac{\partial E^{K}_{\ell_{z}}}{\partial\rho}(\rho^{K}_{i}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),0)}.

This yields the result.
∎

Now, we state the following lemma

Lemma 40
.

There exists
\eta>0
such that
\forall(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\in B_{bound}
, we have

\rho_{1}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})-\rho_{0}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})>2\eta.

Proof.

Let
(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\in\mathcal{B}_{bound}
. Then,
\varepsilon\in[\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}]
and
\ell_{z}\in[\ell_{1}(\varepsilon),\ell_{2}(\varepsilon)]
where
\varepsilon_{min}(d)<\varepsilon_{1}<\varepsilon_{2}<1
and
\ell_{lb}(\varepsilon)<\ell_{1}(\varepsilon)<\ell_{2}(\varepsilon)<\ell_{ub}(\varepsilon)
. By monotonicity properties of
\rho_{i}^{K}
, we have
\forall\varepsilon\in[\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}],\forall\ell_{z}\in[\ell_{1}(\varepsilon),\ell_{2}(\varepsilon)]

\rho_{1}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})-\rho_{0}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\geq\rho_{1}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{1}(\varepsilon))-\rho_{0}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{1}(\varepsilon))\geq\min_{[\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}]}\rho_{1}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{1}(\varepsilon))-\rho_{0}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{1}(\varepsilon))=:2\eta.

By compactness and regularity of
\rho_{1}^{K}
and
\rho_{0}^{K}
,

\eta:=\frac{1}{2}\left(\rho_{1}^{K}(\varepsilon_{0},\ell_{1}(\varepsilon_{0}))-\rho_{0}^{K}(\varepsilon_{0},\ell_{1}(\varepsilon_{0}))\right)\quad\text{for some $\varepsilon_{0}\in[\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{1}]$}.

In order to prove that
\eta>0
, it suffices to note that
\displaystyle\ell_{1}(\varepsilon_{0})>\ell_{lb}(\varepsilon_{0})
so that
\rho^{K}_{0}
and
\rho^{K}_{1}
do not coincides (recall that this case only occurs when
\displaystyle\ell_{1}(\varepsilon_{0})=\ell_{lb}(\varepsilon_{0})
).
In particular, we have:

\rho_{1}^{K}(\varepsilon_{0},\ell_{1}(\varepsilon_{0}))>\rho_{0}^{K}(\varepsilon_{0},\ell_{1}(\varepsilon_{0})).

∎

Consequently, the quantities

\rho^{K}_{0,max}:=\max_{\mathcal{B}_{bound}}\rho^{K}_{0}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\quad,\quad\rho^{K}_{1,min}:=\min_{\mathcal{B}_{bound}}\rho^{K}_{1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\quad\text{and}\quad\rho^{K}_{2,max}:=\max_{\mathcal{B}_{bound}}\rho^{K}_{2}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})

(4.155)

are well-defined. and we have

\rho^{K}_{1,min}-\rho^{K}_{0,max}>2\eta.

(4.156)

Now, we claim that

Lemma 41
.

\forall z\in[0,z_{max}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})]
, there exist
\rho_{1}(z,\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),\rho_{2}(z,\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\in[\rho_{1}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),\rho_{2}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})]
solutions of the equation

E_{\ell_{z}}^{K}(\rho,z)=\varepsilon

which satisfy

\rho_{1}(z,\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\leq\rho_{max}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\leq\rho_{2}(z,\varepsilon,\ell_{z})

Let
(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\in\mathcal{B}_{bound}
.

•

If
z_{max}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})=0
, then by Lemma
37
,
Z^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
is confined in the equatorial plane:

\displaystyle Z^{K,abs}=\left\{\rho_{0}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\right\},

\displaystyle Z^{K,trapped}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})=\left\{\rho_{1}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),\rho_{2}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\right\}.

•

Otherwise, we can choose
\overline{z}_{max}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
and
\tilde{z}_{max}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
such that

0<\overline{z}_{max}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})<\tilde{z}_{max}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})<z_{max}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}).

By Lemma
41
, there exist
\overline{\rho}_{i}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),\tilde{\rho}_{i}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\in]\rho_{1}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),\rho_{2}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})[,i\in\left\{1,2\right\}
which solve the equations

E_{\ell_{z}}^{K}(\rho,\overline{z}_{max}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}))=\varepsilon\quad\text{and}\quad E_{\ell_{z}}^{K}(\rho,\tilde{z}_{max}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}))=\varepsilon

respectively and which satisfy

\rho_{1}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})<\overline{\rho}_{1}(z,\varepsilon,\ell_{z})<\tilde{\rho}_{1}(z,\varepsilon,\ell_{z})<\rho_{max}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})<\tilde{\rho}_{2}(z,\varepsilon,\ell_{z})<\overline{\rho}_{2}(z,\varepsilon,\ell_{z})<\rho_{2}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}).

We introduce the following open subsets of
\mathscr{B}
:

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}^{abs}

\displaystyle:=\left]0,\rho^{K}_{0,max}+\frac{3\eta}{2}\right[\times\mathbb{R},

(4.157)

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{1}

\displaystyle:=\left]\rho^{K}_{0,max}+\frac{\eta}{2},\infty\right[\times\left]\overline{z}_{max}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),+\infty\right[,

(4.158)

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{2}

\displaystyle:=\left]\rho^{K}_{0,max}+\frac{\eta}{2},\infty\right[\times\left]-\infty,-\overline{z}_{max}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\right[,

(4.159)

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{3}

\displaystyle:=\left]\rho^{K}_{0,max}+\frac{\eta}{2},\tilde{\rho}_{1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\right[\times\mathbb{R},

(4.160)

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{4}

\displaystyle:=\left]\tilde{\rho}_{2}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),\infty\right[\times\mathbb{R}.

(4.161)

In order to define
\mathscr{B}_{5}
, we choose
z_{r}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\in]\overline{z}_{max}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),\tilde{z}_{max}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})[
,
\rho^{1}_{r}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\in]\overline{\rho}_{1}(z,\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),\tilde{\rho}_{1}(z,\varepsilon,\ell_{z})[
and
\rho^{2}_{r}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\in]\overline{\rho}_{2}(z,\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),\tilde{\rho}_{2}(z,\varepsilon,\ell_{z})[
and we set

\mathscr{B}_{5}:=]\rho_{r}^{1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),\rho_{r}^{2}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})[\times]-z_{r}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),z_{r}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})[.

(4.162)

Remark 21
.

If
z_{max}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})=0
, then the geodesic is confined in the equatorial plane and
Z^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
is reduced to the set of points
\rho_{i}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
. In this case, there is no work to be done here. Indeed, the classification of the equatorial orbits is the same as that of Schwarzschild geodesics.

In our work, we are interested in the general case (not necessarily equatorial orbits.). Therefore, we will assume that
\displaystyle z_{max}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})>0
.

By construction, the above subsets cover
\mathscr{B}
(See Figure
12
):

Lemma 42
.

We have

\mathscr{B}=\mathscr{B}^{abs}\cup\left(\cup_{i=1\cdots 5}\mathscr{B}_{i}\right).

Now, we introduce the following functions

\displaystyle\rho_{abs}^{0}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},\theta)

\displaystyle:=\sqrt{\Delta(r_{abs}^{0}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},\theta))}\sin\theta,

(4.163)

\displaystyle z_{abs}^{0}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},\theta)

\displaystyle:=(r_{abs}^{0}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},\theta)-1)\cos\theta,

\displaystyle\rho_{tr}^{1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},\theta)

\displaystyle:=\sqrt{\Delta(r_{tr}^{1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},\theta))}\sin\theta,

(4.164)

\displaystyle z_{tr}^{1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},\theta)

\displaystyle:=(r_{tr}^{1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},\theta)-1)\cos\theta,

\displaystyle\rho_{tr}^{2}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},\theta)

\displaystyle:=\sqrt{\Delta(r_{tr}^{2}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},\theta))}\sin\theta,

(4.165)

\displaystyle z_{tr}^{2}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},\theta)

\displaystyle:=(r_{tr}^{2}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},\theta)-1)\cos\theta,

\displaystyle\rho_{tr}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},r)

\displaystyle:=\sqrt{\Delta(r)}\sin\theta_{tr}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},r),

(4.166)

\displaystyle z_{tr}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},r)

\displaystyle:=(r-1)\cos\theta_{tr}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},r),

where
r_{abs}^{0}
,
r_{abs}^{1}
,
r_{abs}^{2}
and (
4.166
) are defined by (
4.119
), (
4.120
), (
4.121
), (
4.122
) respectively.
We state the following lemma

Lemma 43
.

\forall(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\in\mathcal{A}_{bound}
, we have

•

z_{abs}^{0}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},\cdot)
,
z_{tr}^{1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},\cdot)
and
z_{tr}^{2}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},\cdot)
are monotonically decreasing on
]0,\pi[
,

•

\rho_{tr}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},\cdot)
is monotonically increasing on
]r^{K}_{1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),r^{K}_{2}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})[
.

Proof.

1.

First of all, it is easy to see that
\displaystyle z_{abs}^{0}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},\cdot)
is smooth on
]0,\pi[
and
\forall\theta\in]0,\pi[,
we have

\frac{\partial z_{abs}^{0}}{\partial\theta}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},\theta)=\frac{\partial r_{abs}^{0}}{\partial\theta}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},\theta)\cos\theta-(r_{abs}^{0}-1)\sin\theta.

By the monotonicity properties of
r_{abs}^{0}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},\cdot)
(decreasing on
\displaystyle\left]0,\frac{\pi}{2}\right[
and increasing on
\displaystyle\left]\frac{\pi}{2},\pi\right[
), the first term of the right hand side is always negative. Moreover, the second term is always negative on
]0,\pi[
. Therefore,
z_{abs}^{0}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},\cdot)
is monotonically decreasing on
]0,\pi[
and we can write write
\theta
in terms of
z
. The same analysis can be made for
z_{tr}^{1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},\cdot)
and
z_{tr}^{2}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},\cdot)
.

2.

Since
\theta_{tr}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},\cdot)
is smooth,
\rho_{tr}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},\cdot)
is smooth on
]r^{K}_{1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),r^{K}_{2}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})[
and we have

\partial_{r}\rho_{tr}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},r)=\frac{\Delta^{\prime}(r)}{2\sqrt{\Delta(r)}}\sin\theta_{tr}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},r)+\sqrt{\Delta(r)}\partial_{r}\theta_{tr}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},r)\cos\theta_{tr}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},r).

Since
\theta_{tr}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},\cdot)
is monotonically increasing on
]r^{K}_{1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),\tilde{r}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})[
and
\theta_{tr}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},r)\in\left]\overline{\theta}_{max}^{<1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),\frac{\pi}{2}\right[
, the second term is always positive. Therefore,
\rho_{tr}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},\cdot)
is monotonically increasing on
]r^{K}_{1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),r^{K}_{2}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})[
and we can write
r
in terms of
\rho
.

∎

Now, we introduce the following functions

Definition 18
.

Let
(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\in\mathcal{A}_{bound}
.

1.

\displaystyle\Phi^{K,abs}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}:]-\gamma,\gamma[\mapsto]0,\rho_{0}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})]
is defined by

\Phi^{K,abs}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}(z):=\rho^{0}_{abs}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},(z_{abs}^{0}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},\cdot))^{-1}(z)),

(4.167)

2.

\displaystyle\Phi^{K,i}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}
are defined in the following way

\displaystyle\Phi^{K,1}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}

\displaystyle:I^{1}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}:=]\overline{\rho}_{1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),\overline{\rho}_{2}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})[\mapsto]\overline{z}_{max}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),z_{max}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})],

(4.168)

\displaystyle\Phi^{K,2}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}

\displaystyle:I^{2}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}:=]\overline{\rho}_{1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),\overline{\rho}_{2}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})[\mapsto[-z_{max}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),-\overline{z}_{max}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})[

\displaystyle\Phi^{K,3}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}

\displaystyle:I^{3}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}:=]-\tilde{z}_{max}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),\tilde{z}_{max}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})[\mapsto]\tilde{\rho}_{1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),\rho_{1}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})],

\displaystyle\Phi^{K,4}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}

\displaystyle:I^{4}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}:=]-\tilde{z}_{max}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),\tilde{z}_{max}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})[\mapsto]\tilde{\rho}_{2}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),\rho_{2}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})],

where

\displaystyle\Phi^{K,1}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}(\rho):=z_{tr}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},(\rho_{tr}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},\cdot))^{-1}(\rho)),

(4.169)

\displaystyle\Phi^{K,2}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}(\rho):=-\Phi^{K,1}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}(\rho),

\displaystyle\Phi^{K,3}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}(z):=\rho^{1}_{tr}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},(z_{tr}^{1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},\cdot))^{-1}(z)),

\displaystyle\Phi^{K,4}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}(z):=\rho^{2}_{tr}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},(z_{tr}^{2}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},\cdot))^{-1}(z)).

Following Lemma
43
, we obtain

Lemma 44
.

\forall(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\in\mathcal{A}_{bound}
, the functions
\Phi^{K,abs}_{\varepsilon,\ell_{z}}
and
\Phi^{K,i}_{\varepsilon,\ell_{z}}
are well-defined and smooth on
]-\gamma,\gamma[
and
I^{i}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}
respectively. Moreover,

1.

\Phi^{K,abs}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}
admits a unique critical point (a global maximum) on
]-\gamma,\gamma[
given by
\rho^{0}_{abs}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
, reached at the point
z=0
.

2.

\Phi^{K,1}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}
admits a unique critical point (a global maximum) on
I^{1}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}
given by
z_{max}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
, reached at the point
\rho=\rho_{max}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
.

3.

\Phi^{K,2}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}
admits a unique critical point (a global minimum) on
I^{2}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}
given by
-z_{max}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
, reached at the point
\rho=\rho_{max}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
.

4.

\Phi^{K,3}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}
admits a unique critical point (a global minimum) on
I^{3}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}
given by
\rho^{1}_{abs}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
, reached at the point
z=0
.

5.

\Phi^{K,4}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}
admits a unique critical point (a global maximum) on
I^{4}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}
given by
\rho^{2}_{abs}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
, reached at the point
z=0
.

Now, we use the previous results to obtain

Proposition 16
.

\forall(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\in\mathcal{B}_{bound}
,

1.

there exists
\displaystyle\Phi^{K,abs}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}:]-\gamma,\gamma[\mapsto]0,\rho_{0}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})]
such that

Z^{K,abs}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})=\text{Gr}\left(\Phi^{K,abs}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}\right),

2.

there exist
\displaystyle\Phi^{K,i}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}:I^{i}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}\mapsto\mathbb{R},\;i=1\cdots 4
such that

Z^{K,trapped}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})=\bigcup_{i=1\cdots 4}\text{Gr}\left(\Phi^{K,i}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}\right),

where
\displaystyle\Phi^{K,abs}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}
and
\displaystyle\Phi^{K,i}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}
are given by Definition
18
.

To summarise, instead of solving the equation on
\mathscr{B}
, we solve it on
\mathscr{B}_{i}
and
\mathscr{B}^{abs}
and on each of these region, the solutions are either functions of
\rho
or
z
.

Therefore, the problem of finding solutions on
\mathscr{B}
to the equation

E_{\ell_{z}}^{K}(\rho,z)=\varepsilon

(4.170)

is equivalent to the problem of finding solutions on
\mathscr{B}_{i}
and on
\mathscr{B}^{abs}
. Given
(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\in\mathcal{B}_{bound}
, the latter is equivalent to the problem of finding a function defined on
I^{i}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}
or
I^{abs}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}
. Hence, by the above proposition, the solutions are given by
\Phi^{K,abs}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}
in
\mathscr{B}^{abs}
and by
\Phi^{K,i}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}
in
\mathscr{B}_{i}
.

(a)
First component
Z^{K,abs}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})

(b)
Second component
Z^{K,trapped}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})

Figure 12:
The zero velocity curve (in blue) with
d=0.5
and
(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})=(0.98,4)\in\mathcal{A}_{bound}
. The dashed lines separate
\mathscr{B}
into six regions:
\mathscr{B}_{0}
is the region that contains
Z^{K,abs}
and it is delimited by the red line (Figure (a)). The region
\mathscr{B}_{5}
is the region bounded by the cyan rectangle in Figure (b).
\mathscr{B}_{3}
is the region delimited by the two green lines in Figure (b).
\mathscr{B}_{4}
is the region delimited by the cyan vertical line from the left. The horizontal black line above the equatorial plane delimits the region
\mathscr{B}_{1}
from below and the horizontal black line below the equatorial plane delimits the region
\mathscr{B}_{2}
from above.

4.2.4
Domain of trapped geodesics in a sub-extremal Kerr spacetime

The aim of this section is to prove that the region
\mathscr{B}^{trapped}\subset\overline{\mathscr{B}}
where geodesic motion occurs stay away from
\partial\overline{\mathscr{B}}
. From the previous section, we recall that trapped timelike future-directed orbits occur when the associated zero velocity curve has a compact connected component,
Z^{K,trapped}\subset\mathscr{B}
. In this case, the orbit is confined in the region bounded by the latter curve.
We note that the zero velocity curves are only defined in
\mathscr{B}
. More precisely, the effective potential energy is only defined in the exterior region of the spacetime minus the axis of symmetry. However, since we are interested in metrics which are at least
C^{2}-
extendable to the horizon and the axis of symmetry, the boundaries of
\mathscr{B}
, we need to consider the extension of
Z^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
in
\overline{\mathscr{B}}
associated to timelike future-directed geodesics in order to determine
\mathscr{B}^{trapped}\subset\overline{\mathscr{B}}
. Given
(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\in\mathcal{A}_{bound}
,
Z^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
may have accumulation points on the boundary, namely on the poles. However, in the case of the second connected component of
Z^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
, that is
Z^{K,trapped}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
, the accumulation points are distincts from
\partial\overline{\mathscr{B}}
and thus from the poles.

Before we state the precise result of this section, we recall the definition of
\overline{\mathscr{B}}

\overline{\mathscr{B}}:=\overline{\mathscr{B}_{\mathcal{A}}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{\mathcal{H}}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}}.

where

{\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}}}:=\left\{(\rho,z)\in\overline{\mathscr{B}},\rho^{2}+(z\pm\beta)^{2}>\frac{\beta}{a},|z|+|\rho|<\left(1+\frac{1}{b}\right)\beta\right\},

{\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}}:=\left\{(\rho,z)\in\overline{\mathscr{B}},\rho^{2}+(z\pm\beta)^{2}>\frac{\beta}{a},|z|+|\rho|>\left(1-\frac{1}{b}\right)\beta\right\}.

{\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}}:=\left\{(\rho,z)\in\overline{\mathscr{B}},\;z\neq\beta,\;\rho^{2}+(z-\beta)^{2}<\frac{\beta}{c}\right\}\cup\left\{(s,\chi)\in\overline{\mathscr{B}}\quad/0\leq s,\chi<\left(\frac{\beta}{e}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}\right\}.

{\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}}:=\left\{(\rho,z)\in\overline{\mathscr{B}},\;z\neq-\beta,\;\rho^{2}+(z+\beta)^{2}<\frac{\beta}{c}\right\}\cup\left\{(s^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})\in\overline{\mathscr{B}}\quad/0\leq s^{\prime},\chi^{\prime}<\left(\frac{\beta}{e}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}\right\}.

for
0<e<c<a<b
.

We state the following result

Lemma 45
.

Let
\mathcal{B}_{bound}\subset\subset\mathcal{A}_{bound}
. Then, we can choose
0<e<c
uniform in
(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
such that
\forall(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\in\mathcal{B}_{bound}
,

\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}},\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}}\cap Z^{K,trapped}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})=\emptyset.

(4.171)

Moreover,
\forall(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\in\mathcal{A}_{bound}
, the accumulation points for
Z^{K,abs}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
are
p_{N}:=(0,\beta)
and
p_{S}:=(0,-\beta)
.

Proof.

∎

Now, we define the domain of trapped timelike geodesics,
\mathscr{B}^{\pm,trapped}(a,M)
, which depends only on
(a,M)
by the following proposition

Proposition 17

(Domain of trapped geodesics in a sub-extremal Kerr spacetime)
.

Let
(a,M)
be such that
0<|a|<M
. Then, there exists
\rho^{mb,\pm}(a,M)>0

\forall(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\in\mathcal{A}_{bound}\quad\;\quad\rho^{K,\pm}_{1}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},a,M)>\rho^{mb,\pm}(a,M)>0,

where
\rho_{1}^{K,\pm}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},a,M)
is the second largest root of the equation

E_{\ell_{z}}(\rho,0)=\varepsilon

and

\rho^{mb,\pm}(a,M):=\sqrt{\Delta\left(r^{\pm}_{mb}(a,M)\right)},\quad r^{\pm}_{mb}(a,M):=2M\mp a+2\sqrt{M^{2}\mp aM}.

Hence, we set

\mathscr{B}^{\pm,trapped}(a,M):=\left]\rho^{mb,\pm}(a,M),\infty\right[\times\mathbb{R}

Remark 22
.

We recall that
r^{\pm}_{mb}(a,M)
is defined to be the unique solution of the equation

\Phi_{\pm}(r)=1,

where
\Phi_{\pm}
is defined by (
4.41
).

Proof.

Let
(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\in\mathcal{A}_{bound}
and recall that
\displaystyle d=\frac{a}{M}
. Then, by monotonicity properties of
\tilde{\rho}_{1}^{K,\pm}
19
19

19

Recall that
\tilde{\rho}
is defined by
\rho=M\tilde{\rho}
.
, proved in Lemma
39
, we have

\tilde{\rho}_{1}^{K,\pm}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z},d)>\tilde{\rho}_{1}^{K,\pm}(\varepsilon,\ell_{ub}(\varepsilon,d),d)=\tilde{\rho}^{K,\pm}_{s}(\ell_{ub}^{\pm}(\varepsilon,d),d)>\tilde{\rho}^{K,\pm}_{s}(\ell_{ub}^{\pm}(1,d),d)=\sqrt{\Delta\left(\tilde{r}^{\pm}_{mb}(d)\right)}.

It remains to show that
\displaystyle\rho^{mb,\pm}(a,M)>0
for all
\displaystyle(a,M)
such that
\displaystyle 0<|a|<M
.

By the definition of
\displaystyle\rho^{mb,\pm}(a,M)
, it is easy to see that
\displaystyle\rho^{mb,-}(a,M)
never vanishes and
\displaystyle\rho^{mb,+}(a,M)
vanishes if and only if
|a|=M
. Indeed,

1.

\tilde{\rho}^{mb,+}:[0,1[\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
is monotonically decreasing on
]0,1[
and we have

\lim_{d\to 1}\,\tilde{\rho}^{mb,+}(d)=0.

2.

\tilde{\rho}^{mb,-}:[0,1[\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
is monotonically increasing on
]0,1[
and we have

\lim_{d\to 1}\,\tilde{\rho}^{mb,-}(d)\approx 4.83.

∎

For later purposes, in particular the study of regularity of the matter terms, It will be convenient to adjust the definition of
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}}
and
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}
so that the support of Vlasov matter remain in the region
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}
. More precisely, we will show the following

Lemma 46
.

There exists
b>0
such that
\forall(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\in\mathcal{A}_{bound}
, we have

\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}}\cap Z^{K,trapped}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})=\emptyset\quad\text{and}\quad Z^{K,trapped}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\subset\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}.

Proof.

First of all, we claim that
\forall d\in]0,1[

\tilde{\rho}^{mb,-}(d)>\sqrt{1-d^{2}}\quad\text{and}\quad\tilde{\rho}^{mb,+}(d)>\sqrt{1-d^{2}}.

Therefore,

\forall(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\in\mathcal{A}_{bound}\;,\;\tilde{\rho}_{1}^{K,\pm}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})>\tilde{\rho}^{mb,\pm}(d)>\left(1+\frac{1}{b}\right)\sqrt{1-d^{2}}.

∎

Remark 23
.

We note that, by Proposition
17
, in a sub-extremal Kerr black hole, all trapped timelike geodesics lie in
\mathscr{B}^{\pm,trapped}(a,M)
. However, in the limiting case (extremal Kerr),
\mathscr{B}^{+,trapped}(a,M)
coincides with the whole exterior region,
\mathscr{B}
. As for,
\mathscr{B}^{-,trapped}(a,M)
, it is located at approximatively
4.83M
away from the horizon (See proof of Proposition
17
).

In this work, we use the fact that trapped timelike geodesics lie away from the horizon and thus we consider only the sub extremal case. In fact, we will need a non-trivial lower bound on the inner boundary of the support of the Vlasov matter.

In the extremal case, one possibility could be to consider only orbits which are retrograde.

One can wonder what is the location of trapped non-spherical orbits with respect to the ergoregion
\mathscr{E}
. Recall that the ergosurface, the boundary of
\mathscr{E}
, denoted by
\mathscr{S}
, is defined to be the set of points
(\rho,z)\in\mathscr{B}
such that

g(T,T)=0.

In a sub-extremal Kerr exterior
0<d<1
, the latter is equivalent to the set of points
(\rho,z)\in\overline{\mathscr{B}}
such that

V_{K}(\rho,z)=0.

Direct computations lead to

\mathscr{S}=\left\{(\rho,z)\in\overline{\mathscr{B}}\;:\;z^{2}=\left(1-\frac{\rho}{d}\right)\left(1-d^{2}\left(1-\frac{\rho}{d}\right)\right)\right\}.

We refer to Figure
14
for the shape of
\mathscr{S}
. On the other hand, we have

•

\displaystyle\forall d\in]0,1[,

\tilde{\rho}^{mb,-}(d)>d.

•

\displaystyle d_{0}:=2(\sqrt{2}-1)
is the unique solution in
]0,1[
to

\tilde{\rho}^{mb,+}(d)=d.

Note that
\mathscr{S}
intersect the equatorial plane uniquely at the point
\rho_{eq}=d
. Therefore, it is straightforward to see that

\mathscr{B}^{-,trapped}(a,M)\cap\mathscr{E}=\emptyset.

(4.172)

and

\mathscr{B}^{+,trapped}(a,M)\cap\mathscr{E}=\emptyset\quad\text{if and only if}\quad d<d_{0}.

(4.173)

We refer to Figure
13
to visualise the intersection when
d>d_{0}
.

Figure 13:
Intersection of
\mathscr{E}(d)
(blue curve) and
Z^{K,trapped}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
(red curve) when
d=0.95
. The remaining parameters are set to :
\varepsilon=0.99
and
\ell_{z}=2.5
.

Figure 14:
Shape of
S
in the following cases from the left to the right:
d=0.1
,
d=\sqrt{0.5}
and
d=0.99

Remark 24
.

-

Recall that a necessary condition for energy extraction from the black hole and thus a negative energy
\varepsilon
is that
d\ell_{z}<0
and
g_{tt}>0
, which means that retrograde orbits that lie inside the ergoregion have negative energies. This gives another explanation for (
4.172
).

-

Finally, we note that when computing the matter terms in
9.2.1
, we will have to take into account the ergoregion. In this case, we will have to split the matter sources into a term supported in the ergoregion and another which is supported in the remaining region of the spacetime exterior.

5
Reduced Einstein-Vlasov system

In this section, we compute the components of the energy momentum tensor and we reduce the Einstein equations with a source arising from a Vlasov field to a system of elliptic equations.

5.1
Assumptions and General Framework

Recall from Section
3.2.1
the metric ansatz considered throughout this work:

g=-Vdt^{2}+2Wdtd\phi+Xd\phi^{2}+e^{2\lambda}\left(d\rho^{2}+dz^{2}\right),

(5.1)

where
V,W,X,\lambda:\mathscr{B}\to\mathbb{R}
and
\mathscr{B}=\left\{(\rho,z)\;:\;\rho>0\;,\;z\in\mathbb{R}\right\}.

Following the work
[
16
]
, we replace the metric components
V,W,X,\lambda
by
(X,W,\theta,\sigma,\lambda)
, called "metric data", which reduces under symmetries in a nice manner and where
\theta
and
\sigma
are given by (
3.12
) and (
3.13
) respectively.

5.2
Ansatz for the distribution function

We are interested in stationary and axisymmetric distribution functions. Therefore, we assume that
f:\Gamma_{1}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
takes the form

f(t,\phi,\rho,z,\phi,v^{\rho},v^{\phi},v^{z})=\Phi(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),(X,W,\sigma))

(5.2)

where

•

\displaystyle\Phi:\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
is a
C^{2}
function and is supported on some compact set
\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{bound}
of
\mathcal{A}_{bound}
. Without loss of generality (See Lemma
38
), we assume that
\mathcal{B}_{bound}
has the form

\mathcal{B}_{bound}:=\mathcal{B}_{bound}^{-}\cup\mathcal{B}_{bound}^{+}

where
\mathcal{B}_{bound}^{-}
and
\mathcal{B}_{bound}^{+}
are defined by

\mathcal{B}_{bound}^{\pm}:=[\varepsilon_{1}^{\pm},\varepsilon_{2}^{\pm}]\times[\ell_{1}^{\pm},\ell_{2}^{\pm}]

where
\displaystyle\varepsilon^{i,\pm}_{j}
and
\displaystyle\ell^{i,\pm}_{j}
satisfy

\varepsilon^{\pm}_{min}(d)<\varepsilon^{\pm}_{1}<\varepsilon^{\pm}_{2}<1\quad\text{,}\quad\ell_{lb}(\varepsilon^{+}_{2})<\ell^{+}_{1}<\ell^{+}_{2}<\ell_{ub}(\varepsilon^{+}_{1})\quad\text{and}\quad\ell_{ub}(\varepsilon^{-}_{1})<\ell^{-}_{1}<\ell^{-}_{2}<\ell_{lb}(\varepsilon^{-}_{2}).

(5.3)

•

\eta>0
is a constant that will be specified later (see Section
7
),
\Psi_{\eta}(\cdot,\cdot,h)\in C^{\infty}(]0,\infty[\times\mathcal{A}^{admissible},\mathbb{R}_{+})
is a cut-off function depending on the metric data
h:=(X,W,\sigma)
, such that

\Psi_{\eta}(\cdot,(\varepsilon,\ell),h):=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\mbox{\Large$\chi$}_{\eta}(\cdot-\rho_{1}(h,(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}))),\quad(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\in\mathcal{B}_{bound},\\
&0\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\notin\mathcal{A}_{bound},\end{aligned}\right.

(5.4)

where
\rho_{1}
is a positive function of
(h,\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
which will be defined later
20
20

20

\rho_{1}
is defined by (
7.14
). It can be seen as the perturbation of
\rho^{1}_{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
, the second largest root of the equation
\displaystyle E^{K}_{\ell_{z}}(\rho,0)=0
.
and
\mbox{\Large$\chi$}_{\eta}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R},\mathbb{R}_{+})
is a cut-off function such that

\mbox{\Large$\chi$}_{\eta}(s)=\left\{\begin{aligned} &1\quad s\geq 0,\\
&\leq 1\quad s\in[-\eta,0],\\
&0\quad s<-\eta.\end{aligned}\right.

(5.5)

•

\varepsilon
and
\ell_{z}
are defined by (
3.18
) and (
3.19
).

5.3
Reparametrization of the fibre
\Gamma_{x}
and the components of the energy-momentum tensor

In this section, we will compute the components of the energy momentum tensor
\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha\beta}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha\beta}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha\beta}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha\beta}}}}
provided
g
has the form (
3.11
) and
f
has the form (
5.2
). First of all, we recall the definition of
\Omega
:

\Omega=\frac{\partial}{\partial t}+\omega\frac{\partial}{\partial\phi}\quad\text{where}\quad\omega=-\frac{W}{X},

the timelike vector field defined on
\mathscr{B}
which was fixed in Section
3.2.5
for the time orientation.

Recall that by definition, if
c_{\alpha}
is the coordinate basis associated to the spacetime coordinates, then any tangent vector can be written as

v=v^{\alpha}c_{\alpha}.

The
v^{\alpha}
are then called the conjugate coordinates to the spacetime coordinates.

Now let
(v^{t},v^{\phi},v^{\rho},v^{z})
be the conjugate coordinates to the spacetime coordinates
(t,\phi,\rho,z)
. Let
x=(t,\phi,\rho,z)\in\mathscr{B}
and denote by

c_{0}=\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\;,\;c_{1}=\frac{\partial}{\partial\phi}\;,\;c_{2}=\frac{\partial}{\partial\rho}\;,\;c_{3}=\frac{\partial}{\partial z}

the canonical basis determined by the local coordinates.
We consider the orthonormal frame defined by

e_{0}:=\sqrt{\frac{X}{\sigma^{2}}}\Omega\;,\;e_{1}:=\sqrt{\frac{1}{X}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\phi}\;,\;e_{2}:=e^{-\lambda}\frac{\partial}{\partial\rho}\;,\;e_{3}:=e^{-\lambda}\frac{\partial}{\partial z}.

Let
p^{\alpha}
be the corresponding coordinates. Therefore:

\displaystyle(v^{\alpha})=G(p^{\alpha})\quad\text{where}\quad\quad G=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\sqrt{X}}{\sigma}&0&0&0\\
\frac{\omega\sqrt{X}}{\sqrt{\sigma}}&\frac{1}{\sqrt{X}}&0&0\\
0&0&e^{-\lambda}&0\\
0&0&0&e^{-\lambda}\end{pmatrix}

In the orthonormal frame, the mass shell condition is given by

-(p^{0})^{2}+(p^{1})^{2}+(p^{2})^{2}+(p^{3})^{2}=-1.

Now, we can express the
p^{0}
in terms of the remaining
p^{i}
:

p^{0}=\sqrt{1+|p|^{2}}.

We recall the energy-momentum tensor associated to the metric
g
and the distribution function
f
,

\forall(t,\phi,\rho,z)\in\mathcal{M}\;\quad\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha\beta}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha\beta}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha\beta}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha\beta}}}}(t,\phi,\rho,z)=\int_{\Gamma_{x}}v_{\alpha}v_{\beta}f(t,\phi,\rho,z,v^{\rho},v^{\phi},v^{z})\;d\text{vol}_{x}(v),

where
\Gamma_{x}
is given by

\Gamma_{x}:=\left\{v^{\alpha}\in\mathbb{T}_{x}\mathcal{M}\;:\;\mathchoice{g^{{{\alpha\beta}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\alpha\beta}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\alpha\beta}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\alpha\beta}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}v_{\alpha}v_{\beta}=-1,\;v^{0}>0\right\},

where we recall that the condition
v^{0}>0
is equivalent to
v
is future-pointed. Thus, after a first change of variables in the fibre
\Gamma_{x}
, the energy momentum tensor is given by

\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha\beta}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha\beta}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha\beta}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha\beta}}}}[f]=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;v_{\alpha}(p)v_{\beta}(p)f(x,v(p))\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{\sqrt{1+|p|^{2}}}.

We will make a second change of variables in order to simplify the expressions of the energy momentum tensor components. To this end, we compute
\varepsilon
and
\ell_{z}
in terms of the
p^{i}
s:

\displaystyle\varepsilon=-v_{0}=\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{X}}\left(1+|p|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\omega\ell_{z}

and

\ell_{z}=\sqrt{X}p^{1}.

From the mass shell condition, we have

e^{2\lambda}\left((v^{\rho})^{2}+(v^{z})^{2}\right)=J(\rho,z,\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),

(5.6)

where
J
is given by

J(\rho,z,\varepsilon,\ell_{z})=-1+\frac{X}{\sigma^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}+\frac{2W}{\sigma^{2}}\varepsilon\ell_{z}-\frac{V}{\sigma^{2}}\ell_{z}^{2}.

We recall that
\sigma^{2}=XV+W^{2}
. We introduce the polar variables
(\sqrt{J},\vartheta)\in[0,\infty[\times[0,2\pi]
in the following way:

\displaystyle v^{2}

\displaystyle=e^{-\lambda}\sqrt{J}\sin\vartheta,

\displaystyle v^{3}

\displaystyle=e^{-\lambda}\sqrt{J}\cos\vartheta.

Therefore,

\displaystyle p^{2}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{J}\sin\vartheta,

\displaystyle p^{3}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{J}\cos\vartheta.

Now we make the change of variables from
(p^{1},p^{2},p^{3})
to
(p^{1},\sqrt{J},\vartheta)
. We have

\frac{dp^{1}dp^{2}dp^{3}}{\sqrt{1+|p|^{2}}}=\frac{\sqrt{J}}{\sqrt{1+J+(p^{1})^{2}}}d\sqrt{J}d\vartheta dp^{\phi}.

Therefore,

\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha\beta}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha\beta}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha\beta}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha\beta}}}}[f]=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\;v_{\alpha}(p)v_{\beta}(p)f(x,v(p))\frac{\sqrt{J}}{\sqrt{1+J+(p^{1})^{2}}}d\sqrt{J}d\vartheta dp^{1}.

(5.7)

We make a last change of variables
H(\rho,z):(p^{1},\sqrt{J},\vartheta)\mapsto(E,L,\vartheta)
defined by

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle:=\varepsilon-\omega\ell_{z}=\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{X}}\left(1+J+(p^{1})^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

(5.8)

\displaystyle L

\displaystyle:=\frac{\sqrt{X}}{\rho}p^{1}.

It is easy to see that
H(\rho,z)
is a smooth diffeomorphism on its image and we have

\frac{\sqrt{J}}{\sqrt{1+J+(p^{1})^{2}}}dp^{1}d\sqrt{J}d\vartheta=\frac{\rho}{\sigma}dEdLd\vartheta.

It remains to compute the domain of the variables
E
and
L
, which is the image of above change of variables. From (
5.8
),
\forall J\geq 0,\forall p^{1}\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

E\geq\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{X}}.

By straightforward computations, we obtain

J=\frac{X}{\sigma^{2}}E^{2}-1-\frac{\rho^{2}}{X}L^{2}.

Since
J
is positive,
L
satisfies

|L|\leq\frac{\sqrt{X}}{\rho}\left(-1+\frac{X}{\sigma^{2}}E^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}=:\tilde{L}(E,X,\sigma,\rho,z).

(5.9)

Now we define
D(\rho,z)
to be the set:

D(\rho,z):=\left\{(E,L)\;:\;E\geq\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{X}}\quad\text{and }\quad|L|\leq\tilde{L}(E,X,\sigma,\rho,z)\right\}.

(5.10)

Therefore,

\text{Im}\,H(\rho,z)=D(\rho,z).

By symmetry considerations, we have

\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho\rho}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho\rho}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho\rho}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho\rho}}}}[f](\rho,z)=\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.98846pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.5632pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{zz}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.98846pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.5632pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{zz}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.98846pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.5632pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{zz}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.98846pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.5632pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{zz}}}}[f](\rho,z).

Moreover, the only non-vanishing components of
\mathbb{T}_{\alpha\beta}
are
\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53888pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.52777pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{tt}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53888pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.52777pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{tt}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53888pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.52777pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{tt}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53888pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.52777pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{tt}}}},\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68903pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.3493pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{t\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68903pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.3493pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{t\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68903pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.3493pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{t\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68903pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.3493pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{t\phi}}}},\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi\phi}}}},\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho\rho}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho\rho}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho\rho}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho\rho}}}}
and
\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.98846pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.5632pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{zz}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.98846pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.5632pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{zz}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.98846pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.5632pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{zz}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.98846pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.5632pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{zz}}}}
. Now we compute

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53888pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.52777pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{tt}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53888pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.52777pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{tt}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53888pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.52777pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{tt}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53888pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.52777pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{tt}}}}[f](\rho,z)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\;(v_{0}(p))^{2}f(x,v(p))\frac{\sqrt{J}}{\sqrt{1+J+(p^{1})^{2}}}d\sqrt{J}d\chi dp^{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{D(\rho,z)}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\;(E+\rho\omega L)^{2}\Phi(E+\rho\omega,L)\frac{\rho}{\sigma}\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L),(X,W,\sigma))dEdLd\vartheta

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi\rho}{\sigma}\int_{D(\rho,z)}\left(E+\rho\omega L\right)^{2}\Phi(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L),(X,W,\sigma))\,dEdL.

Here, we used that
v_{0}(p)=-\varepsilon=-(E+\rho\omega L)
,
\ell_{z}=\rho L
and the independence of the integrand on
\vartheta
. The components
\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53888pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.52777pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{tt}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53888pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.52777pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{tt}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53888pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.52777pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{tt}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53888pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.52777pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{tt}}}}[f](\rho,z)
,
\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68903pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.3493pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{t\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68903pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.3493pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{t\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68903pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.3493pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{t\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68903pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.3493pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{t\phi}}}}[f](\rho,z)
and
\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi\phi}}}}[f](\rho,z)
are computed in the same way. As for
\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho\rho}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho\rho}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho\rho}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho\rho}}}}[f](\rho,z)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho\rho}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho\rho}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho\rho}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho\rho}}}}[f](\rho,z)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\;(v_{\rho}(p))^{2}f(x,v(p))\frac{\sqrt{J}}{\sqrt{1+J+(p^{1})^{2}}}d\sqrt{J}d\chi dp^{1}

\displaystyle=e^{2\lambda}\int_{D(\rho,z)}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\;J(E,L)\sin^{2}\vartheta\frac{\rho}{\sigma}\Phi(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L),(X,W,\sigma))dEdLd\vartheta

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi\rho}{\sigma}e^{2\lambda}\int_{D(\rho,z)}\left(\frac{X}{\sigma^{2}}E^{2}-1-\frac{\rho^{2}}{X}L^{2}\right)\Phi(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L),(X,W,\sigma))\,dEdL

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi\rho}{\sigma}e^{2\lambda}\int_{D(\rho,z)}\left(\tilde{L}(E,X,\sigma,\rho,z)^{2}-L^{2}\right)\Phi(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L),(X,W,\sigma))\,dEdL.

The final expression is obtained by (
5.9
). Hence,

\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53888pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.52777pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{tt}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53888pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.52777pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{tt}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53888pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.52777pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{tt}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53888pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.52777pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{tt}}}}[f](\rho,z)=\frac{2\pi\rho}{\sigma}\int_{D(\rho,z)}\left(E+\rho\omega L\right)^{2}\Phi(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L),(X,W,\sigma))\,dEdL,

(5.11)

\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68903pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.3493pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{t\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68903pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.3493pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{t\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68903pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.3493pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{t\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68903pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.3493pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{t\phi}}}}[f](\rho,z)=-\frac{2\pi\rho}{\sigma}\int_{D(\rho,z)}\rho L\left(E+\rho\omega L\right)\Phi(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L),(X,W,\sigma))\,dEdL,

(5.12)

\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi\phi}}}}[f](\rho,z)=\frac{2\pi\rho}{\sigma}\int_{D(\rho,z)}(\rho L)^{2}\Phi(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L),(X,W,\sigma))\,dEdL,

(5.13)

\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho\rho}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho\rho}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho\rho}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho\rho}}}}[f](\rho,z)=\frac{\pi\rho}{\sigma}e^{2\lambda}\int_{D(\rho,z)}\left(\tilde{L}(E,X,\sigma,\rho,z)^{2}-L^{2}\right)\Phi(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L),(X,W,\sigma))\,dEdL,

(5.14)

\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.98846pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.5632pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{zz}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.98846pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.5632pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{zz}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.98846pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.5632pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{zz}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.98846pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.5632pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{zz}}}}[f](\rho,z)=\frac{\pi\rho}{\sigma}e^{2\lambda}\int_{D(\rho,z)}\left(\tilde{L}(E,X,\sigma,\rho,z)^{2}-L^{2}\right)\Phi(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L),(X,W,\sigma))\,dEdL,

(5.15)

where
\tilde{L}
was defined in (
5.9
).
It remains to compute the intersection of
D(\rho,z)
and the support of
\Phi
and
\Psi_{\eta}
. This will be done in Section
9.2
concerning the regularity of the matter terms.

5.4
Static and axisymmetric black holes with matter

In this section, we apply Theorem
1.1
in
[
16
]
concerning the modified Carter-Robinson theory. First of all, we recall the following theorem

Theorem 2

(O.CHODOSH, Y.SHLAPENTOKH-ROTHMAN)
.

Suppose that
(\mathcal{M},g)
solves the Einstein equations for some energy-momentum tensor
\mathbb{T}
satisfying

\mathbb{T}(T,\partial_{\rho})=\mathbb{T}(T,\partial_{z})=\mathbb{T}(\Phi,\partial_{\rho})=\mathbb{T}(\Phi,\partial_{z})=0.

(5.16)

Then, the metric data
(X,W,\theta,\sigma,\lambda)
satisfies the following equations on
\mathscr{B}=\left\{(\rho,z)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\;:\;\rho>0\right\}
:

1.

X satisfies

\sigma^{-1}\partial_{\rho}(\sigma\partial_{\rho}X)+\sigma^{-1}\partial_{z}(\sigma\partial_{z}X)=e^{2\lambda}\left(-2\mathbb{T}(\Phi,\Phi)+{\text{Tr}(\mathbb{T})}X\right)+\frac{(\partial_{\rho}X)^{2}+(\partial_{z}X)^{2}-\theta_{\rho}^{2}-\theta_{z}^{2}}{X},

(5.17)

2.

W satisfies

\partial_{\rho}(X^{-1}W)d\rho+\partial_{z}(X^{-1}W)dz=\frac{\sigma}{X^{2}}(\theta_{\rho}dz-\theta_{z}d\rho),

(5.18)

3.

\theta
satisfies

d\theta=2\sigma^{-1}e^{2\lambda}\left(\mathbb{T}(\Phi,\Phi)W-\mathbb{T}(\Phi,T)X\right)d\rho\wedge dz,

(5.19)

as well as

\sigma^{-1}\partial_{\rho}(\sigma\partial_{\rho}\theta)+\sigma^{-1}\partial_{z}(\sigma\partial_{z}\theta)=\frac{2\theta_{\rho}\partial_{\rho}X+2\theta_{z}\partial_{z}X}{X}

(5.20)

4.

\sigma
satisfies

X^{-1}\exp{-2\lambda}\sigma(\partial^{2}_{\rho}\sigma+\partial^{2}_{z}\sigma)=\mathbb{T}\left(T-X^{-1}W\Phi,T-X^{-1}W\Phi\right)-X^{-2}\sigma^{2}\mathbb{T}(\Phi,\Phi)+X^{-1}\sigma^{2}\text{Tr}(\mathbb{T}),

(5.21)

5.

\lambda
satisfies the following equations at the points where
|\partial\sigma|\neq 0

\partial_{\rho}\lambda=\alpha_{\rho}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\rho}\log X,\quad\partial_{z}\lambda=\alpha_{z}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{z}\log X,

(5.22)

where

\displaystyle((\partial_{\rho}\sigma)^{2}+(\partial_{z}\sigma)^{2})\alpha_{\rho}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(\partial_{\rho}\sigma)\sigma\left(\mathbb{T}(\partial_{\rho},\partial_{\rho})-\mathbb{T}(\partial_{z},\partial_{z})+\frac{1}{2}\frac{(\partial_{\rho}X)^{2}-(\partial_{z}X)^{2}+(\theta_{\rho})^{2}-(\theta_{z})^{2}}{X^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+(\partial_{\rho}\sigma)(\partial^{2}_{\rho}\sigma-\partial^{2}_{z}\sigma)+(\partial_{z}\sigma)(\partial^{2}_{\rho,z}\sigma)

\displaystyle+(\partial_{z}\sigma)\sigma\left(\mathbb{T}(\partial_{\rho},\partial_{z})+\frac{1}{2}X^{-2}((\partial_{\rho}X)(\partial_{z}X)+(\theta_{\rho})(\theta_{z}))\right),

\displaystyle((\partial_{\rho}\sigma)^{2}+(\partial_{z}\sigma)^{2})\alpha_{z}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}(\partial_{z}\sigma)\sigma\left(\mathbb{T}(\partial_{\rho},\partial_{\rho})-\mathbb{T}(\partial_{z},\partial_{z})+\frac{1}{2}\frac{(\partial_{\rho}X)^{2}-(\partial_{z}X)^{2}+(\theta_{\rho})^{2}-(\theta_{z})^{2}}{X^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+(\partial_{\rho}\sigma)((\partial^{2}_{\rho,z}\sigma))-(\partial_{z}\sigma)(\partial^{2}_{\rho}\sigma-\partial^{2}_{z}\sigma)+(\partial_{\rho}\sigma)((\partial^{2}_{\rho,z}\sigma))

\displaystyle+(\partial_{\rho}\sigma)\sigma\left(\mathbb{T}(\partial_{\rho},\partial_{z})+\frac{1}{2}X^{-2}((\partial_{\rho}X)(\partial_{z}X)+(\theta_{\rho})(\theta_{z}))\right),

Independent of the behaviour of
\sigma
,
\lambda
satisfies

\displaystyle 2\partial^{2}_{\rho}\lambda+2\partial^{2}_{z}\lambda

\displaystyle=-\partial^{2}_{\rho}\log{X}-\partial^{2}_{z}\log{X}+\sigma^{-1}((\partial_{\rho}\sigma)^{2}+(\partial_{z}\sigma)^{2})

\displaystyle+e^{2\lambda}\text{Tr}(\mathbb{T})-\mathbb{T}(\partial_{\rho},\partial_{\rho})-\mathbb{T}(\partial_{z},\partial_{z})

\displaystyle-2X^{-1}\left(\mathbb{T}(\Phi,\Phi)-\frac{1}{2}\text{Tr}(\mathbb{T})X\right)e^{2\lambda}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}X^{-2}((\partial_{\rho}X)^{2}+(\partial_{z}X)^{2}+(\theta_{\rho})^{2}+(\theta_{z})^{2}).

Conversely, if the metric data solves each of these equations, and
|\partial\sigma|\neq 0
on
\mathscr{B}
, then we may recover the metric
g
defined on
\mathcal{M}=\mathbb{R}\times(0,2\pi)\times\mathscr{B}
such that
(\mathcal{M},g)
solves the Einstein equations with energy-momentum tensor
\mathbb{T}
.

In our case, all the assumptions of Theorem
2
are satisfied. Therefore, we apply the latter with the components of the energy momentum tensor given by (
5.11
), (
5.12
), (
5.13
), (
5.14
) and (
5.15
) and obtain

Proposition 18
.

Suppose that
(\mathcal{M},g,f)
solves the Einstein-Vlasov equations where
g
is given by

g=-Vdt^{2}+2Wdtd\phi+Xd\phi^{2}+e^{2\lambda}\left(d\rho^{2}+dz^{2}\right)

and
f
is given by

f(x,v)=\Phi(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\Psi_{\eta}\left(\rho,(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),h:=(X,W,\sigma)\right).

Then the metric data
(X,W,\theta,\sigma,\lambda)
satisfies the following equations on
\mathscr{B}

1.

X satisfies

\sigma^{-1}\partial_{\rho}(\sigma\partial_{\rho}X)+\sigma^{-1}\partial_{z}(\sigma\partial_{z}X)=F_{1}(W,X,\sigma)(\rho,z)+\frac{(\partial_{\rho}X)^{2}+(\partial_{z}X)^{2}-\theta_{\rho}^{2}-\theta_{z}^{2}}{X},

(5.23)

2.

W satisfies

\partial_{\rho}(X^{-1}W)d\rho+\partial_{z}(X^{-1}W)dz=\frac{\sigma}{X^{2}}(\theta_{\rho}dz-\theta_{z}d\rho),

(5.24)

3.

\theta
satisfies

d\theta=2\sigma^{-1}e^{2\lambda}F_{2}(W,X,\sigma)(\rho,z)d\rho\wedge dz,

(5.25)

as well as

\sigma^{-1}\partial_{\rho}(\sigma\partial_{\rho}\theta)+\sigma^{-1}\partial_{z}(\sigma\partial_{z}\theta)=\frac{2\theta_{\rho}\partial_{\rho}X+2\theta_{z}\partial_{z}X}{X}.

(5.26)

4.

\sigma
satisfies

X^{-1}\exp{(-2\lambda)}\sigma(\partial^{2}_{\rho}\sigma+\partial^{2}_{z}\sigma)=F_{3}(W,X,\sigma)(\rho,z),

(5.27)

5.

\lambda
satisfies the following equations at the points where
|\partial\sigma|\neq 0

\partial_{\rho}\lambda=\alpha_{\rho}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\rho}\log X,\quad\partial_{z}\lambda=\alpha_{z}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{z}\log X,

(5.28)

where

\displaystyle((\partial_{\rho}\sigma)^{2}+(\partial_{z}\sigma)^{2})\alpha_{\rho}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}(\partial_{\rho}\sigma)\sigma\frac{(\partial_{\rho}X)^{2}-(\partial_{z}X)^{2}+(\theta_{\rho})^{2}-(\theta_{z})^{2}}{X^{2}}+(\partial_{\rho}\sigma)(\partial^{2}_{\rho}\sigma-\partial^{2}_{z}\sigma)

\displaystyle+(\partial_{z}\sigma)(\partial^{2}_{\rho,z}\sigma)+\frac{1}{2}X^{-2}((\partial_{\rho}X)(\partial_{z}X)+(\theta_{\rho})(\theta_{z}))),

\displaystyle((\partial_{\rho}\sigma)^{2}+(\partial_{z}\sigma)^{2})\alpha_{z}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4}(\partial_{z}\sigma)\sigma\frac{(\partial_{\rho}X)^{2}-(\partial_{z}X)^{2}+(\theta_{\rho})^{2}-(\theta_{z})^{2}}{X^{2}}-(\partial_{z}\sigma)(\partial^{2}_{\rho}\sigma-\partial^{2}_{z}\sigma)

\displaystyle+(\partial_{\rho}\sigma)(\partial^{2}_{\rho,z}\sigma)+\frac{1}{2}X^{-2}((\partial_{\rho}X)(\partial_{z}X)+(\theta_{\rho})(\theta_{z}))),

Independent of the behaviour of
\sigma
,
\lambda
satisfies

\displaystyle 2\partial^{2}_{\rho}\lambda+2\partial^{2}_{z}\lambda

\displaystyle=-\partial^{2}_{\rho}\log{X}-\partial^{2}_{z}\log{X}+\sigma^{-1}((\partial_{\rho}\sigma)^{2}+(\partial_{z}\sigma)^{2})+F_{4}(W,X,\sigma)(\rho,z)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}X^{-2}((\partial_{\rho}X)^{2}+(\partial_{z}X)^{2}+(\theta_{\rho})^{2}+(\theta_{z})^{2}),

where
F_{1},F_{2},F_{3},F_{4}
are given by:

F_{1}(W,X,\sigma)(\rho,z):=-e^{2\lambda}\frac{2\pi\rho}{\sigma}\int_{D(\rho,z)}(X+2(\rho L)^{2})\Phi(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L),(X,W,\sigma))\,dEdL,

(5.29)

F_{2}(W,X,\sigma)(\rho,z):=\frac{2\pi\rho}{\sigma}X\int_{D(\rho,z)}\rho LE\Phi(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L),(X,W,\sigma))\,dEdL,

(5.30)

\displaystyle F_{3}(W,X,\sigma)(\rho,z)

\displaystyle:=\frac{2\pi\rho^{3}\sigma}{X^{2}}\int_{D(\rho,z)}\left(\tilde{L}^{2}-L^{2}\right)\Phi(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L),(X,W,\sigma))\,dEdL,

(5.31)

\displaystyle F_{4}(W,X,\sigma,\lambda)(\rho,z):=-\frac{4\pi e^{2\lambda}\rho}{\sigma}

\displaystyle\int_{D(\rho,z)}\left(\frac{X^{2}}{\rho^{2}\sigma^{2}}E^{2}+\left(1-\frac{X}{\rho^{2}}\right)\left(1+\frac{\rho^{2}}{X}L^{2}\right)\right)\Phi(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L)

(5.32)

\displaystyle\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L),(X,W,\sigma))\,dEdL,

Conversely, if the metric data solves each of these equations, and
|\partial\sigma|\neq 0
on
\mathscr{B}
, then we may recover the metric and the distribution function
(\mathcal{M},g,f)
, solving the Einstein-Vlasov equations.

Proof.

We apply Theorem
2
and we compute the components of the energy-momentum tensor.

First of all, we have
