\displaystyle\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho\rho}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho\rho}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho\rho}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho\rho}}}}

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.98846pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.5632pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{zz}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.98846pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.5632pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{zz}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.98846pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.5632pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{zz}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.98846pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.5632pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{zz}}}},

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.25667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.25667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.0276pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.59114pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho z}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.25667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.25667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.0276pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.59114pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho z}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.25667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.25667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.0276pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.59114pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho z}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.25667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.25667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.0276pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.59114pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho z}}}}

\displaystyle=0.

Therefore,
\alpha_{\rho}
and
\alpha_{z}
do not depend on the the matter terms. Now, we compute

\displaystyle\text{Tr}(\mathbb{T})

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{g^{{{\alpha\beta}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\alpha\beta}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\alpha\beta}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\alpha\beta}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha\beta}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha\beta}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha\beta}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha\beta}}}}

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{g^{{{tt}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53888pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.52777pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{tt}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53888pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.52777pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{tt}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53888pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.52777pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{tt}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53888pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.52777pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53888pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.52777pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{tt}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53888pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.52777pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{tt}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53888pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.52777pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{tt}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53888pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.52777pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{tt}}}}+2\mathchoice{g^{{{t\phi}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68903pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.3493pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{t\phi}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68903pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.3493pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{t\phi}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68903pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.3493pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{t\phi}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68903pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.3493pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68903pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.3493pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{t\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68903pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.3493pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{t\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68903pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.3493pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{t\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68903pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.3493pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{t\phi}}}}+\mathchoice{g^{{{\phi\phi}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\phi\phi}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\phi\phi}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\phi\phi}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi\phi}}}}+2e^{-2\lambda}\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho\rho}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho\rho}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho\rho}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho\rho}}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}\left(-X\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53888pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.52777pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{tt}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53888pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.52777pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{tt}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53888pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.52777pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{tt}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53888pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.52777pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{tt}}}}+2W\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68903pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.3493pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{t\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68903pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.3493pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{t\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68903pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.3493pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{t\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68903pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.3493pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{t\phi}}}}+V\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi\phi}}}}\right)+2e^{-2\lambda}\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho\rho}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho\rho}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho\rho}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho\rho}}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi\rho}{\sigma^{3}}\int_{D(\rho,z)}\left(-X(E+\rho\omega L)^{2}-2W\rho L(E+\rho\omega L)+V(\rho L)^{2}+\sigma^{2}\left(\tilde{L}(E,X,\sigma,\rho)^{2}-L^{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\Phi(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L),(X,W,\sigma))\,dEdL,

Now we recall that
\sigma^{2}=XV+W^{2}
and
\displaystyle\tilde{L}(E,X,\sigma,\rho)=\frac{\sqrt{X}}{\rho}\left(-1+\frac{X}{\sigma^{2}}E^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}
. We use the latter to obtain

\displaystyle-X(E+\rho\omega L)^{2}-2W\rho L(E+\rho\omega L)+V(\rho L)^{2}+\sigma^{2}\left(\tilde{L}(E,X,\sigma,\rho)^{2}-L^{2}\right)=-\sigma^{2}.

Therefore,

\text{Tr}(\mathbb{T})=-\frac{2\pi\rho}{\sigma}\int_{D(\rho,z)}\,\Phi(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L),(X,W,\sigma))\,dEdL.

Now, we compute

\displaystyle F_{1}(W,X,\sigma)(\rho,z)

\displaystyle=e^{2\lambda}\left(-2\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi\phi}}}}+{\text{Tr}(\mathbb{T})}X\right)

\displaystyle=-e^{2\lambda}\frac{2\pi\rho}{\sigma}\int_{D(\rho,z)}\,(X+2(\rho L)^{2})\Phi(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L),(X,W,\sigma))\,dEdL.

\displaystyle F_{2}(W,X,\sigma)(\rho,z)=\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi\phi}}}}W-\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68903pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.3493pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi t}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68903pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.3493pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi t}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68903pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.3493pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi t}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68903pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.3493pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi t}}}}X

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi\rho}{\sigma}\int_{D(\rho,z)}\,\left((\rho L)^{2}W+X\rho L(E+\rho\omega L)\right)\Phi(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L),(X,W,\sigma))\,dEdL

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi\rho}{\sigma}X\int_{D(\rho,z)}\,\rho LE\Phi(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L),(X,W,\sigma))\,dEdL.

We have used
\omega:=-WX^{-1}
to obtain the latter expression. Now we compute

\displaystyle\mathbb{T}\left(T-X^{-1}W\Phi,T-X^{-1}W\Phi\right)-X^{-2}\sigma^{2}\mathbb{T}(\Phi,\Phi)+X^{-1}\sigma^{2}\text{Tr}(\mathbb{T})

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53888pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.52777pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{tt}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53888pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.52777pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{tt}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53888pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.52777pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{tt}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.04169pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53888pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.52777pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{tt}}}}+\omega^{2}\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi\phi}}}}+2\omega\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68903pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.3493pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{t\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68903pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.3493pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{t\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68903pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.3493pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{t\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.82918pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68903pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.3493pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{t\phi}}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi\rho}{\sigma}\int_{D(\rho,z)}\,\left((E+\rho\omega L)^{2}+(\rho\omega L)^{2}-2\rho\omega L(E+\rho\omega L)\right)\Phi(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L)

\displaystyle\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L),(X,W,\sigma))\,dEdL.

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi\rho}{\sigma}\int_{D(\rho,z)}\,E^{2}\Phi(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L),(X,W,\sigma))\,dEdL.

Hence,

\displaystyle F_{3}(W,X,\sigma)(\rho,z)=\mathbb{T}\left(T-X^{-1}W\Phi,T-X^{-1}W\Phi\right)-X^{-2}\sigma^{2}\mathbb{T}(\Phi,\Phi)+X^{-1}\sigma^{2}\text{Tr}(\mathbb{T})

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi\rho}{\sigma}\int_{D(\rho,z)}\,\left(E^{2}-X^{-2}\sigma^{2}(\rho L)^{2}-X^{-1}\sigma^{2}\right)\Phi(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L),(X,W,\sigma))\,dEdL

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi\rho}{\sigma}\int_{D(\rho,z)}\,X^{-2}\sigma^{2}\rho^{2}\left(\frac{X^{2}}{\rho^{2}\sigma^{2}}E^{2}-L^{2}-\frac{X}{\rho^{2}}\right)\Phi(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L),(X,W,\sigma))\,dEdL

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi\rho^{3}\sigma}{X^{2}}\int_{D(\rho,z)}\,\left(\frac{X}{\rho^{2}}\left(\frac{X}{\sigma^{2}}E^{2}-1\right)-L^{2}\right)\Phi(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L),(X,W,\sigma))\,dEdL

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi\rho^{3}\sigma}{X^{2}}\int_{D(\rho,z)}\,\left(\tilde{L}^{2}(E,X,\sigma,\rho,z)-L^{2}\right)\Phi(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L),(X,W,\sigma))\,dEdL.

Finally, we compute

\displaystyle F_{4}(W,X,\sigma,\lambda)(\rho,z)

\displaystyle=e^{2\lambda}\text{Tr}(\mathbb{T})-\mathbb{T}(\partial_{\rho},\partial_{\rho})-\mathbb{T}(\partial_{z},\partial_{z})-2X^{-1}\left(\mathbb{T}(\Phi,\Phi)-\frac{1}{2}\text{Tr}(\mathbb{T})X\right)e^{2\lambda}

\displaystyle=2e^{2\lambda}\left(\text{Tr}(\mathbb{T})-e^{-2\lambda}\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho\rho}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho\rho}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho\rho}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\rho\rho}}}}-X^{-1}\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi\phi}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.61667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83917pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.17084pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi\phi}}}}\right)

\displaystyle=-2e^{2\lambda}\frac{2\pi\rho}{\sigma}\int_{D(\rho,z)}\,\left(1+\frac{1}{2}\left(\tilde{L}^{2}(E,X,\sigma,\rho,z)-L^{2}\right)+X^{-1}(\rho L)^{2}\right)\Phi(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L)

\displaystyle\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L),(X,W,\sigma))\,dEdL

\displaystyle=-\frac{4\pi e^{2\lambda}\rho}{\sigma}\int_{D(\rho,z)}\left(\frac{X^{2}}{\rho^{2}\sigma^{2}}E^{2}+\left(1-\frac{X}{\rho^{2}}\right)\left(1+\frac{\rho^{2}}{X}L^{2}\right)\right)\Phi(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L)

\displaystyle\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(E+\rho\omega L,\rho L),(X,W,\sigma))\,dEdL.

∎

5.5
Renormalised unknowns and their equations

This section follows closely
[
17
]
. Nonetheless, we detail the computations and the arguments in order to be self contained.

In order to apply the fixed point theorem in a neighbourhood of a fixed Kerr solution, we introduce new quantities which are normalised with respect to the Kerr metric. This choice of variables was first considered in
[
17
]
and allows to subtract off the leading order singular behaviour near the axis and the horizon.

We begin by defining an adapted Ernst potential,
Y:\mathscr{B}\to\mathbb{R}
. Let us review the construction in the vacuum case just for comparison.

In vacuum, the twist one-form associated to
\displaystyle\Phi=\frac{\partial}{\partial\phi}
vanishes:

d\theta=0.

Therefore, since
\mathscr{B}
is simply connected, there exists a function
Y_{vacuum}:\mathscr{B}\to\mathbb{R}
, which satisfies

\theta=dY_{vacuum}.

(5.33)

This leads to a harmonic map system in
(X,Y_{vacuum})
which decouples from the remaining metric data. Moreover, the requirements of asymptotic flatness and regular extensions to the axis and to the horizon lead to boundary conditions for
X
and
Y_{vacuum}
. This allows one to determine uniquely
(X,Y_{vacuum})
(See
[
55
]
,
[
12
]
). Now, given
(X,Y_{vacuum})
and the boundary conditions, the rest of the metric components are uniquely determined by quadratures, see Section
2.2.2
of the introduction and
[
28
, Chapter 10]
for details.

In the presence of matter, the twist one-form is no longer closed. We recall from (
5.25
) that
\theta
satisfies

d\theta=2\sigma^{-1}e^{2\lambda}F_{2}(W,X,\sigma)(\rho,z)d\rho\wedge dz.

(5.34)

However, we can still define an "Ernst potential"
Y
, which will define, together with
X
a harmonic map system. The idea is to split the twist one-form
\theta
into an Ernst potential piece
dY
and an other one-form
B
, defined on
\overline{\mathscr{B}}
which verifies

dB=2\sigma^{-1}e^{2\lambda}F_{2}(W,X,\sigma)(\rho,z)d\rho\wedge dz\quad\text{on}\quad\mathscr{B}.

(5.35)

1.

We begin by extending the two-form
\omega\in\Omega^{2}(\mathscr{B})
defined by

\omega:=\omega_{\rho z}d\rho\wedge dz:=2\sigma^{-1}e^{2\lambda}F_{2}(W,X,\sigma)(\rho,z)d\rho\wedge dz

to a two-form
\overline{\omega}
defined on
\overline{\mathscr{B}}
so that

\overline{\omega}=\omega\quad\quad\text{on}\quad\mathscr{B}.

First, assume that
\omega_{\rho z}
can be extended to a function
\overline{\omega}_{\rho z}

21
21

21

We identify
\overline{\omega}_{\rho z}
with its coordinate representations in every chart
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}
,
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}}
,
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}}
.
defined on
\overline{\mathscr{B}}
. Now, let
(\xi_{N},\xi_{S},1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})
be the partition of unity subordinate to
(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}},\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}},\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}})
and let
p\in\overline{\mathscr{B}}

•

If
p\in(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}})
, then

\omega_{\rho z}(p)=\overline{\omega}_{\rho z}^{A}(\rho,z)

•

If
p\in\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}
, then

\omega_{\rho z}(p)=\overline{\omega}_{\rho z}^{N}(s,\chi)

•

If
p\in\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}}
, then

\omega_{\rho z}(p)=\overline{\omega}_{\rho z}^{S}(s^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})

where
\overline{\omega}_{\rho z}^{A}
,
\overline{\omega}_{\rho z}^{N}
and
\overline{\omega}_{\rho z}^{S}
are defined on
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}}
,
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}
and
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}}
respectively.
We have

\displaystyle\omega(p)

\displaystyle=\omega_{\rho z}(p)d\rho\wedge dz=\overline{\omega}_{\rho z}(s,\chi)(s^{2}+\chi^{2})ds\wedge d\chi\quad\forall p\in\mathscr{B}_{N}.

Since the coordinate system
(s,\chi)
is defined on
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}
, we set:

\overline{\omega}(p):=\overline{\omega}^{N}_{\rho z}(s,\chi)(s^{2}+\chi^{2})ds\wedge d\chi\quad\forall p\in\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}.

Similarly, we set

\overline{\omega}(p):=\overline{\omega}^{S}_{\rho z}(s^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})((s^{\prime})^{2}+(\chi^{\prime})^{2})ds^{\prime}\wedge d\chi^{\prime},\quad\forall p\in\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}}.

Finally, we set

\displaystyle\overline{\omega}(p)

\displaystyle:=\overline{\omega}_{\rho z}(p)d\rho\wedge dz,\quad\forall p\in\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}}.

This defines
\overline{\omega}
on
\overline{\mathscr{B}}
.

2.

In order to construct
B
, or rather to find the equations for
B
, we first make the following ansatz:

B:=B^{(A)}+\xi_{N}B^{(N)}+\xi_{S}B^{(S)}

(5.36)

where
B^{(N)}
,
B^{(S)}
and
B^{(A)}
are one-forms that are defined on
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}
,
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}}
and
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}}
respectively. Therefore, solving the equations for
B^{(N)}
,
B^{(S)}
and
B^{(A)}
will allow us to determine
B
. Hence, we determine the equations for
B^{(N)}
,
B^{(S)}
and
B^{(A)}
based on (
5.35
):

•

On
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}
, we construct
B^{(N)}
such that

dB^{(N)}=\xi_{N}\overline{\omega}.

In the local coordinates
(s,\chi)
, we have

B^{(N)}=B^{(N)}_{s}ds+B^{(S)}_{\chi}d\chi.

This implies

dB^{(N)}=\partial_{\chi}B^{(N)}_{s}d\chi\wedge ds+\partial_{s}B_{\chi}^{(N)}ds\wedge d\chi.

Therefore,

\xi_{N}\overline{\omega}_{\rho z}(s,\chi)(s^{2}+\chi^{2})ds\wedge d\chi=\partial_{\chi}B^{(N)}_{s}d\chi\wedge ds+\partial_{s}B_{\chi}^{(N)}ds\wedge d\chi.

We make the following gauge choice:

B^{(N)}_{s}(s,\chi)=0\;,\;B_{\chi}^{(N)}(0,\chi)=B_{\chi}^{(N)}(s,0)=0.

Moreover, we require
B^{(N)}_{s}
to satisfy the equation.

\partial_{s}B^{(N)}_{\chi}=\xi_{N}\overline{\omega}_{\rho z}(s,\chi)(s^{2}+\chi^{2}).

Consequently, we define the one-form
B^{(N)}
on
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}
to be the solution the equations

\displaystyle B_{\chi}^{(N)}(0,\chi)

\displaystyle=0,

(5.37)

\displaystyle\partial_{s}B^{(N)}_{\chi}

\displaystyle=2\xi_{N}\sigma^{-1}e^{2\lambda}F_{2}(W,X,\sigma)(\rho,z)(s^{2}+\chi^{2}),

\displaystyle B^{(N)}_{s}(s,\chi)

\displaystyle=0.

•

Similarly, we define the one-form
B^{(S)}
on
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}}
to be the solution the equations

\displaystyle B_{\chi^{\prime}}^{(S)}(0,\chi^{\prime})

\displaystyle=0,

(5.38)

\displaystyle\partial_{s^{\prime}}B^{(S)}_{\chi^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=2\xi_{S}\sigma^{-1}e^{2\lambda}F_{2}(W,X,\sigma)(s^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})((s^{\prime})^{2}+(\chi^{\prime})^{2}),

\displaystyle B^{(S)}_{s^{\prime}}(s^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})

\displaystyle=0.

•

Finally, we use (
5.36
) in order to find the equations satisfied by
B^{(A)}
. We have

\displaystyle dB^{(A)}

\displaystyle=dB-d(\xi_{N}B^{(N)})-d(\xi_{S}B^{(S)})

\displaystyle=dB-\xi_{N}d(B^{(N)})-d\xi_{N}\wedge B^{(N)}-\xi_{S}d(B^{(S)})-d\xi_{S}\wedge B^{(S)}

\displaystyle=(1-\xi_{N}^{2}-\xi_{S}^{2})dB-d\xi_{N}\wedge B^{(N)}-d\xi_{S}\wedge B^{(S)}.

On
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}}
, we have

dB=2\sigma^{-1}e^{2\lambda}F_{2}(W,X,\sigma)(\rho,z)d\rho\wedge dz,

B^{(N)}=B^{(N)}_{\rho}d\rho+B^{(S)}_{z}dz\quad\text{,}\quad B^{(N)}=B^{(S)}_{\rho}d\rho+B^{(S)}_{z}dz\quad{,}\quad B^{(A)}=B^{(A)}_{\rho}d\rho+B^{(A)}_{z}dz

We compute

dB^{(A)}=\partial_{z}B_{\rho}^{(A)}dz\wedge d\rho+\partial_{\rho}B_{z}^{(A)}d\rho\wedge dz,

d\xi_{N}\wedge B^{(N)}=(\partial_{\rho}\xi_{N})B_{z}^{(N)}-(\partial_{z}\xi_{N})B_{\rho}^{(N)}.

We make the following gauge choice

B_{\rho}^{(A)}=0\quad\text{,}\quad B_{z}^{(A)}(0,z)=0

and we require
B_{z}^{(A)}
to satisfy the equation

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}B_{z}^{(A)}(\rho,z)

\displaystyle=2(1-\xi_{N}^{2}-\xi_{S}^{2})\sigma^{-1}e^{2\lambda}F_{2}(W,X,\sigma)(\rho,z)

\displaystyle-(\partial_{\rho}\xi_{N})B_{z}^{(N)}+(\partial_{z}\xi_{N})B_{\rho}^{(N)}-(\partial_{\rho}\xi_{S})B_{z}^{(S)}+(\partial_{z}\xi_{S})B_{\rho}^{(S)}.

Therefore, we define the one-form
B^{(A)}
to be the solution of the equation

\displaystyle B_{z}^{(A)}(0,z)

\displaystyle=0,

(5.39)

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}B_{z}^{(A)}(\rho,z)

\displaystyle=2(1-\xi_{N}^{2}-\xi_{S}^{2})\sigma^{-1}e^{2\lambda}F_{2}(W,X,\sigma)(\rho,z)

\displaystyle-(\partial_{\rho}\xi_{N})B_{z}^{(N)}+(\partial_{z}\xi_{N})B_{\rho}^{(N)}-(\partial_{\rho}\xi_{S})B_{z}^{(S)}+(\partial_{z}\xi_{S})B_{\rho}^{(S)},

\displaystyle B_{\rho}^{(A)}(\rho,z)

\displaystyle=0.

3.

With this construction,
B
is defined by
\displaystyle\left(B_{\chi}^{(N)},B_{\chi^{\prime}}^{(S)},B_{z}^{(A)}\right)
and verifies (
5.35
)

Therefore, we can define the Ernst potential
Y
:

Definition 19
.

We define, up to a constant, the Ernst potential
Y:\mathscr{B}\to\mathbb{R}
to be the function which satisfies:

dY=\theta-B.

(5.40)

Now, we introduce the renormalised unknowns to be the following set of functions and a one-form, which are all assumed to be continuous on
\overline{\mathscr{B}}
and satisfy the following definitions on
\mathscr{B}
:

\displaystyle\overset{\circ}{\sigma}

\displaystyle:=\frac{\sigma-\sigma_{K}}{\sigma_{K}}\quad\sigma_{K}=\rho,

(5.41)

\displaystyle\overset{\circ}{X}

\displaystyle:=X_{K}^{-1}(X-X_{K}),\quad\overset{\circ}{Y}:=X_{K}^{-1}(Y-Y_{K}),

(5.42)

\displaystyle\overset{\circ}{\Theta}

\displaystyle:=X^{-1}W-X_{K}^{-1}W_{K},

(5.43)

\displaystyle\overset{\circ}{\lambda}

\displaystyle:=\lambda-\lambda_{K}.

(5.44)

Henceforth, the quantities
\displaystyle\left(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},\left(B_{\chi}^{(N)},B_{\chi^{\prime}}^{(S)},B_{z}^{(A)}\right),\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}\right),\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda}\right)
will be called the "renormalised unknowns".

Now, given
\displaystyle\left(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},\displaystyle\left(B_{\chi}^{(N)},B_{\chi^{\prime}}^{(S)},B_{z}^{(A)}\right),\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}\right),\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda}\right)
, we can recover the original unknowns in the following way:

\sigma=\sigma_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma}),

(5.45)

X=X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X}),\quad Y=X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{Y}),

(5.46)

X^{-1}W=\overset{\circ}{\Theta}+X_{K}^{-1}W_{K},

(5.47)

\lambda=\overset{\circ}{\lambda}+\lambda_{K}.

(5.48)

In terms of the renormalised unknowns, the matter terms become:

\displaystyle F_{1}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma})(\rho,z):=-\frac{2\pi e^{2\left(\overset{\circ}{\lambda}+\lambda_{K}\right)}}{1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma}}

\displaystyle\int_{D(\rho,z)}(X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})+2(\rho L)^{2})\Phi(E+\rho\left(-\overset{\circ}{\Theta}+\omega_{K}\right)L,\rho L)

\displaystyle\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(E+\rho(-\overset{\circ}{\Theta}+\omega_{K})L,\rho L),(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma}))\,dEdL,

\displaystyle F_{2}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma})(\rho,z):=\frac{2\pi}{1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma}}X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})

\displaystyle\int_{D(\rho,z)}\rho LE\Phi(E+\rho\left(-\overset{\circ}{\Theta}+\omega_{K}\right)L,\rho L)

\displaystyle\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(E+\rho\left(-\overset{\circ}{\Theta}+\omega_{K}\right)L,\rho L),(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma}))\,dEdL,

\displaystyle F_{3}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma})(\rho,z):=\frac{\rho^{4}}{X^{2}_{K}}\frac{2\pi(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma})}{\left(1+\overset{\circ}{X}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle\int_{D(\rho,z)}\left(\tilde{L}^{2}-L^{2}\right)\Phi(E+\rho\left(-\overset{\circ}{\Theta}+\omega_{K}\right)L,\rho L)

\displaystyle\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(E+\rho\left(-\overset{\circ}{\Theta}+\omega_{K}\right)L,\rho L),(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma}))\,dEdL,

\displaystyle F_{4}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma},\overset{\circ}{\lambda})(\rho,z)

\displaystyle:=-\frac{4\pi e^{2\left(\overset{\circ}{\lambda}+\lambda_{K}\right)}}{1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma}}

\displaystyle\int_{D(\rho,z)}\left(\frac{X_{K}^{2}\left(1+\overset{\circ}{X}\right)^{2}}{\rho^{4}\left(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma}\right)^{2}}E^{2}+\left(1-\frac{X_{K}}{\rho^{2}}\left(1+\overset{\circ}{X}\right)\right)\left(1+\frac{\rho^{2}}{X_{K}}\frac{1}{1+\overset{\circ}{X}}L^{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\Phi(E+\rho\left(-\overset{\circ}{\Theta}+\omega_{K}\right)L,\rho L)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(E+\rho\left(-\overset{\circ}{\Theta}+\omega_{K}\right),\rho L),(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma}))\,dEdL,

We apply Proposition
18
in order to obtain the equations for the renormalised unknows.

Proposition 19
.

The renormalised unknowns
\displaystyle\left(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},B,\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}\right),\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda}\right)
verify the following equations

•

\overset{\circ}{\sigma}
satisfies

\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}{\overset{\circ}{\sigma}}=\rho^{-1}\sigma^{-1}{X}e^{2\lambda}F_{3}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\sigma})(\rho,z),

(5.49)

where
\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}
is the Laplacian corresponding to the flat metric on
\mathbb{R}^{4}
given by
g_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}=d\rho^{2}+dz^{2}+\rho^{2}d\mathbb{S}^{2}
.

•

B
satisfies

\displaystyle\partial_{\chi}B^{(N)}_{s}=2\xi_{N}(s\chi)^{-1}(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma})^{-1}e^{2\lambda+2\overset{\circ}{\lambda}}F_{2}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\sigma})(s,\chi)(s^{2}+\chi^{2}),

(5.50)

\displaystyle\partial_{\chi^{\prime}}B^{(S)}_{s^{\prime}}=2\xi_{S}(s^{\prime}(\chi)^{\prime})^{-1}(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma})^{-1}e^{2\lambda+2\overset{\circ}{\lambda}}F_{2}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\sigma})(s^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})((s^{\prime})^{2}+(\chi^{\prime})^{2}),

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}B^{(A)}_{z}(\rho,z)+(\partial_{\rho}\xi_{N})B_{z}^{(N)}-(\partial_{z}\xi_{N})B_{\rho}^{(N)}+(\partial_{\rho}\xi_{S})B_{z}^{(S)}-(\partial_{z}\xi_{S})B_{\rho}^{(S)}=

\displaystyle 2(1-\xi_{N}^{2}-\xi_{S}^{2})\rho^{-1}(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma})^{-1}e^{2\lambda+2\overset{\circ}{\lambda}}F_{2}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\sigma})(\rho,z),

•

(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y})
satisfies

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\overset{\circ}{X}+\frac{2\partial Y_{K}\cdot\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}}{X_{K}}-\frac{2|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}\overset{\circ}{X}+2\frac{\partial X_{K}\cdot\partial Y_{K}}{X_{K}^{2}}\overset{\circ}{Y}

\displaystyle=N_{X}:=N^{(1)}_{X}+N^{(2)}_{X},

(5.51)

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\overset{\circ}{Y}-\frac{2\partial Y_{K}\cdot\partial\overset{\circ}{X}}{X_{K}}-\frac{(|\partial X_{K}|^{2}+|\partial Y_{K}|^{2})}{X_{K}^{2}}\overset{\circ}{Y}

\displaystyle=N_{Y}:=N^{(1)}_{Y}+N^{(2)}_{Y},

where

N^{(1)}_{X}:=\frac{X_{K}^{2}(|\partial\overset{\circ}{X}|^{2}-|\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}|^{2})+(\overset{\circ}{X}\partial Y_{K}-\overset{\circ}{Y}\partial X_{K})\cdot(2X_{K}\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}-\overset{\circ}{X}\partial Y_{K}+\overset{\circ}{Y}\partial X_{K})}{X_{K}^{2}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})},

N^{(1)}_{Y}:=\frac{\partial\overset{\circ}{X}\cdot\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}+2X_{K}(\overset{\circ}{Y}\partial X_{K}-\overset{\circ}{X}\partial Y_{K})\cdot\partial\overset{\circ}{X}}{X_{K}^{2}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})},

\displaystyle N^{(2)}_{X}

\displaystyle:=(\rho^{-1}-\sigma^{-1}\partial_{\rho}\sigma)\frac{\partial_{\rho}(X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X}))}{X_{K}}-\sigma^{-1}\partial_{z}\sigma\frac{\partial_{z}(X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X}))}{X_{K}}-\frac{2\partial_{\rho}(Y_{K}+X_{K}\overset{\circ}{Y})B_{\rho}}{X_{K}^{2}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})}

\displaystyle-\frac{2\partial_{z}(Y_{K}+X_{K}\overset{\circ}{Y})B_{z}}{X_{K}^{2}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})}-\frac{B_{\rho}^{2}+B_{z}^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})}+X^{-1}_{K}F_{1}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma})(\rho,z),

\displaystyle N^{(2)}_{Y}

\displaystyle:=(\rho^{-1}-\sigma^{-1}\partial_{\rho}\sigma)\partial_{\rho}(Y_{K}+X_{K}\overset{\circ}{Y})X_{K}^{-1}-\sigma^{-1}\partial_{\rho}\sigma B_{\rho}X_{K}^{-1}-\sigma^{-1}\partial_{z}\sigma(\partial_{z}(Y_{K}+X_{K}\overset{\circ}{Y})+B_{z})X_{K}^{-1}

\displaystyle+\frac{B_{\rho}\partial_{\rho}(X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X}))+B_{z}\partial_{z}(X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X}))}{X_{K}^{2}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})},

and where
\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}
is the Laplacian corresponding to the flat metric on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
given by
g_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}=d\rho^{2}+dz^{2}+\rho^{2}d\phi^{2}
.

•

\overset{\circ}{\Theta}
satisfies

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}\overset{\circ}{\Theta}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\sigma}{X^{2}}(\partial_{z}Y+B_{z})+\frac{\rho}{X_{K}^{2}}\partial_{z}Y_{K},

(5.52)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\overset{\circ}{\Theta}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma}{X^{2}}(\partial_{\rho}Y+B_{\rho})-\frac{\rho}{X_{K}^{2}}\partial_{\rho}Y_{K}.

•

\overset{\circ}{\lambda}
satisfies

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}\overset{\circ}{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{\rho}-(\alpha_{K})_{\rho}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\rho}\log(1+\overset{\circ}{X}),

(5.53)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\overset{\circ}{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{z}-(\alpha_{K})_{z}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{z}\log(1+\overset{\circ}{X}),

where

\displaystyle(\alpha_{K})_{\rho}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\rho X_{K}^{-2}((\partial_{\rho}X_{K})^{2}-(\partial_{z}X_{K})^{2}+(\partial_{\rho}Y_{K})^{2})-(\partial_{z}Y_{K})^{2},

\displaystyle(\alpha_{K})_{z}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\rho X_{K}^{-2}((\partial_{\rho}X_{K})(\partial_{z}X_{K})+(\partial_{\rho}Y_{K})(\partial_{z}Y_{K})),

where
\alpha_{\rho}
and
\alpha_{z}
satisfy

\displaystyle((\partial_{\rho}\sigma)^{2}+(\partial_{z}\sigma)^{2})\alpha_{\rho}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}(\partial_{\rho}\sigma)\sigma\frac{(\partial_{\rho}X)^{2}-(\partial_{z}X)^{2}+(\theta_{\rho})^{2}-(\theta_{z})^{2}}{X^{2}}+(\partial_{\rho}\sigma)(\partial^{2}_{\rho}\sigma-\partial^{2}_{z}\sigma)+(\partial_{z}\sigma)((\partial^{2}_{\rho,z}\sigma))

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}X^{-2}((\partial_{\rho}X)(\partial_{z}X)+(\theta_{\rho})(\theta_{z}))),

\displaystyle((\partial_{\rho}\sigma)^{2}+(\partial_{z}\sigma)^{2})\alpha_{z}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4}(\partial_{z}\sigma)\sigma\frac{(\partial_{\rho}X)^{2}-(\partial_{z}X)^{2}+(\theta_{\rho})^{2}-(\theta_{z})^{2}}{X^{2}}-(\partial_{z}\sigma)(\partial^{2}_{\rho}\sigma-\partial^{2}_{z}\sigma)+(\partial_{\rho}\sigma)((\partial^{2}_{\rho,z}\sigma))

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}X^{-2}((\partial_{\rho}X)(\partial_{z}X)+(\theta_{\rho})(\theta_{z}))

Proof.

1.

First, we derive the equation for
\overset{\circ}{\sigma}
:
For this, we compute the Laplacian of
\overset{\circ}{\sigma}
with respect to the flat metric
g=d\rho^{2}+dz^{2}+\rho^{2}d\mathbb{S}^{2}
:

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\overset{\circ}{\sigma}

\displaystyle=\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\left(\frac{\sigma}{\rho}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\det g}}\partial_{i}\left(\sqrt{\det g}\mathchoice{g^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\partial_{j}\left(\frac{\sigma}{\rho}\right)\right),\quad i,j\in\left\{1,\cdots 4\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\rho^{2}\sin\theta}\partial_{\rho}\left(\rho^{2}\sin\theta\mathchoice{g^{{{\rho\rho}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\rho\rho}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\rho\rho}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\rho\rho}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.29797pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.06674pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.61908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\partial_{\rho}\left(\frac{\sigma}{\rho}\right)\right)+\frac{1}{\rho^{2}\sin\theta}\partial_{z}\left(\rho^{2}\sin\theta\mathchoice{g^{{{zz}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.98846pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.5632pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{zz}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.98846pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.5632pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{zz}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.98846pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.5632pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{zz}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.21536pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.98846pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.5632pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\partial_{z}\left(\frac{\sigma}{\rho}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\partial_{\rho\rho}\left(\frac{\sigma}{\rho}\right)+\frac{2}{\rho}\partial_{\rho}\left(\frac{\sigma}{\rho}\right)+\frac{1}{\rho}\partial_{zz}\sigma

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\rho}(\partial_{\rho\rho}\sigma+\partial_{zz}\sigma)

By (
5.27
), we have

X^{-1}\exp{(-2\lambda)}\sigma(\partial^{2}_{\rho}\sigma+\partial^{2}_{z}\sigma)=F_{3}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma})(\rho,z),

Therefore,

\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\overset{\circ}{\sigma}=\frac{X_{K}}{\rho^{2}}e^{2\lambda}\frac{1+\overset{\circ}{X}}{1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma}}F_{3}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma})(\rho,z).

2.

The equation for
B
follows from the definition of
\displaystyle\left(B_{\chi}^{(N)},B_{\chi^{\prime}}^{(S)},B_{z}^{(A)}\right)
and the equations (
5.37
), (
5.38
), (
5.39
).

3.

We turn to the equations for
(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y})
:

•

First of all, we recall from the classical Carter Robinson theory (see for example
[
55
]
)that
(X_{K},Y_{K})
forms a harmonic map system and satisfies the equations

\left\{\begin{aligned} \rho^{-1}\partial_{\rho}(\rho\partial_{\rho}X_{K})+\rho^{-1}\partial_{z}(\rho\partial_{z}X_{K})&=\frac{(\partial_{\rho}X_{K})^{2}+(\partial_{z}X_{K})^{2}-(\partial_{\rho}Y_{K})^{2}-(\partial_{z}Y_{K})^{2}}{X_{K}},\\
\rho^{-1}\partial_{\rho}(\rho\partial_{\rho}Y_{K})+\rho^{-1}\partial_{z}(\rho\partial_{z}Y_{K})&=\frac{2(\partial_{\rho}Y_{K})(\partial_{\rho}X_{K})+2(\partial_{z}Y_{K})(\partial_{z}X_{K})}{X_{K}}.\end{aligned}\right.

(5.54)

•

Now, we compute

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\overset{\circ}{X}

\displaystyle=\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\frac{X-X_{K}}{X_{K}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\rho}\partial_{\rho}\left(\rho\partial_{\rho}\left(\frac{X-X_{K}}{X_{K}}\right)\right)+\partial_{zz}\left(\frac{X-X_{K}}{X_{K}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\rho}\frac{(\partial_{\rho}X)X_{K}-(\partial_{\rho}X_{K})X}{X^{2}_{K}}+\partial_{\rho}\left(\frac{(\partial_{\rho}X)X_{K}-(\partial_{\rho}X_{K})X}{X^{2}_{K}}\right)+\partial_{z}\left(\frac{(\partial_{z}X)X_{K}-(\partial_{z}X_{K})X}{X^{2}_{K}}\right).

We expand

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}\left(\frac{(\partial_{\rho}X)X_{K}-(\partial_{\rho}X_{K})X}{X^{2}_{K}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{(\partial_{\rho\rho}X)X_{K}-(\partial_{\rho\rho}X_{K})X}{X^{2}_{K}}-2\frac{(\partial_{\rho}X_{K})(\partial_{\rho}X)}{X^{2}_{K}}+2X\frac{(\partial_{\rho}X_{K})(\partial_{\rho}X)}{X^{3}_{K}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(\partial_{\rho\rho}X)X_{K}-(\partial_{\rho\rho}X_{K})X}{X^{2}_{K}}-2\frac{1}{X_{K}}\partial_{\rho}\overset{\circ}{X}\partial_{\rho}X_{K},

and

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\left(\frac{(\partial_{z}X)X_{K}-(\partial_{z}X_{K})X}{X^{2}_{K}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{(\partial_{zz}X)X_{K}-(\partial_{zz}X_{K})X}{X^{2}_{K}}-2\frac{(\partial_{z}X_{K})(\partial_{z}X)}{X^{2}_{K}}+2X\frac{(\partial_{z}X_{K})(\partial_{z}X)}{X^{3}_{K}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(\partial_{\rho\rho}X)X_{K}-(\partial_{\rho\rho}X_{K})X}{X^{2}_{K}}-2\frac{1}{X_{K}}\partial_{z}\overset{\circ}{X}\partial_{z}X_{K}.

Moreover, from (
5.54
), we have

\frac{\partial_{\rho}X_{K}}{\rho}+\partial_{\rho\rho}X_{K}+\partial_{zz}X_{K}=\frac{(\partial_{\rho}X_{K})^{2}+(\partial_{z}X_{K})^{2}-(\partial_{\rho}Y_{K})^{2}-(\partial_{z}Y_{K})^{2}}{X_{K}}.

Recall that

\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}=\partial_{\rho\rho}+\partial_{zz}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\overset{\circ}{X}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{X_{K}}\left(\frac{1}{\rho}\left(\partial_{\rho}X\right)+\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}X\right)-2\frac{1}{X_{K}}\partial_{\rho}\overset{\circ}{X}\partial_{\rho}X_{K}-2\frac{1}{X_{K}}\partial_{z}\overset{\circ}{X}\partial_{z}X_{K}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{X_{K}}\left(\frac{1}{\rho}\left(\partial_{\rho}X\right)+\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}X\right)-\frac{X}{X^{2}_{K}}\left(\frac{(\partial_{\rho}X_{K})^{2}+(\partial_{z}X_{K})^{2}-(\partial_{\rho}Y_{K})^{2}-(\partial_{z}Y_{K})^{2}}{X_{K}}\right)

\displaystyle-2\frac{1}{X_{K}}\partial\overset{\circ}{X}\cdot\partial X_{K}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{X_{K}}\left(\frac{1}{\rho}\left(\partial_{\rho}X\right)+\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}X\right)-\frac{1+\overset{\circ}{X}}{X_{K}}\left(|\partial X_{K}|^{2}-|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}\right)-2\frac{1}{X_{K}}\partial\overset{\circ}{X}\cdot\partial X_{K}.

•

We replace
X
by
X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})
in the left hand side of (
5.23
) and we compute

\displaystyle\sigma^{-1}\partial_{\rho}(\sigma\partial_{\rho}X)+\sigma^{-1}\partial_{z}(\sigma\partial_{z}X)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma}(\partial_{\rho}\sigma\partial_{\rho}X+\sigma\partial_{\rho\rho}X)+\frac{1}{\sigma}(\partial_{z}\sigma\partial_{z}X+\sigma\partial_{zz}X)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma}(\partial_{\rho}\sigma\partial_{\rho}X+\partial_{z}\sigma\partial_{z}X)+\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}X

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma}\partial_{\rho}\sigma\partial_{\rho}(X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X}))+\frac{1}{\sigma}\partial_{z}\sigma\partial_{z}(X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})).

Therefore, by (
5.23
), we have

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}X

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\sigma}\partial_{\rho}\sigma\partial_{\rho}(X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X}))-\frac{1}{\sigma}\partial_{z}\sigma\partial_{z}(X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X}))+F_{1}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma})(\rho,z)

(5.55)

\displaystyle+\frac{(\partial_{\rho}X)^{2}+(\partial_{z}X)^{2}-\theta_{\rho}^{2}-\theta_{z}^{2}}{X}.

•

Recall that
\displaystyle dY=\theta-B
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\theta_{\rho}

\displaystyle=\partial_{\rho}Y+B_{\rho},

\displaystyle\theta_{z}

\displaystyle=\partial_{z}Y+B_{z}.

This implies

\displaystyle\theta_{\rho}^{2}

\displaystyle=(\partial_{\rho}Y)^{2}+B^{2}_{\rho}+2B_{\rho}\partial_{\rho}Y,

\displaystyle\theta_{z}^{2}

\displaystyle=(\partial_{z}Y)^{2}+B^{2}_{z}+2B_{z}\partial_{z}Y.

•

Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{(\partial_{\rho}X)^{2}+(\partial_{z}X)^{2}-\theta_{\rho}^{2}-\theta_{z}^{2}}{X}

\displaystyle=\frac{|\partial X|^{2}-|\partial Y|^{2}}{X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})}-\frac{B_{\rho}^{2}+B_{z}^{2}}{X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})}-2\frac{B_{\rho}\partial_{\rho}(Y_{K}+\overset{\circ}{Y}X_{K})}{X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})}

(5.56)

\displaystyle-2\frac{B_{z}\partial_{z}(Y_{K}+\overset{\circ}{Y}X_{K})}{X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})}.

•

We set

\displaystyle N^{(2)}_{X}

\displaystyle:=(\rho^{-1}-\sigma^{-1}\partial_{\rho}\sigma)\left(\frac{\partial_{\rho}(X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X}))}{X_{K}}\right)-\sigma^{-1}\frac{\partial_{z}\sigma\partial_{z}(X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X}))}{X_{K}}+X^{-1}_{K}F_{1}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma})(\rho,z)

(5.57)

\displaystyle-\frac{B^{2}_{\rho}+B^{2}_{z}}{X^{2}_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})}-\frac{2B_{\rho}\partial_{\rho}(Y_{K}+\overset{\circ}{Y}X_{K})}{X^{2}_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})}-\frac{2B_{z}\partial_{z}(Y_{K}+\overset{\circ}{Y}X_{K})}{X^{2}_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})}.

•

From (
5.55
), (
5.56
) and (
5.57
), we obtain

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\overset{\circ}{X}

\displaystyle=N_{X}^{(2)}-\frac{1+\overset{\circ}{X}}{X_{K}}\left(|\partial X_{K}|^{2}-|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}\right)-2\frac{1}{X_{K}}\partial\overset{\circ}{X}\cdot\partial X_{K}+\frac{\left(|\partial X|^{2}-|\partial Y|^{2}\right)}{X_{K}^{2}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})}.

Now, we replace
X
by
X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})
and
Y
by
Y_{K}+X_{K}\overset{\circ}{Y}
in the last term of the right hand side and we expand so that we obtain

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\overset{\circ}{X}

\displaystyle=N_{X}^{(2)}-\frac{1+\overset{\circ}{X}}{X_{K}}\left(|\partial X_{K}|^{2}-|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}\right)-2\frac{1}{X_{K}}\partial\overset{\circ}{X}\cdot\partial X_{K}+\frac{\left(|\partial X|^{2}-|\partial Y|^{2}\right)}{X_{K}^{2}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})}

\displaystyle=\frac{X_{K}^{2}(|\partial\overset{\circ}{X}|^{2}-|\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}|^{2})+\overset{\circ}{X}(\overset{\circ}{X}+2)|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}-|\partial X_{K}|^{2}\overset{\circ}{Y}^{2}-2\overset{\circ}{Y}\partial Y_{K}\cdot\partial X_{K}}{X_{K}^{2}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})}

\displaystyle-\frac{2X_{K}\overset{\circ}{Y}\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}\cdot\partial X_{K}-2X_{K}\partial Y_{K}\cdot\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}}{X_{K}^{2}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})}

\displaystyle=\frac{X_{K}^{2}(|\partial\overset{\circ}{X}|^{2}-|\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}|^{2})+2\overset{\circ}{X}(\overset{\circ}{X}+1)|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}-\overset{\circ}{X}^{2}|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}-2(\overset{\circ}{X}+1)\overset{\circ}{Y}\partial Y_{K}\cdot\partial X_{K}}{X_{K}^{2}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})}

\displaystyle+\frac{2\overset{\circ}{X}\overset{\circ}{Y}\partial Y_{K}\cdot X_{K}-2X_{K}\overset{\circ}{Y}\partial X_{K}\cdot\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}-2(1+\overset{\circ}{X})X_{K}\partial Y_{K}\cdot\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}+2\overset{\circ}{X}X_{K}\partial Y_{K}\cdot\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}}{X_{K}^{2}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})}.

•

We set

N^{(1)}_{X}:=\frac{X_{K}^{2}(|\partial\overset{\circ}{X}|^{2}-|\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}|^{2})+(\overset{\circ}{X}\partial Y_{K}-\overset{\circ}{Y}\partial X_{K})\cdot(2X_{K}\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}-\overset{\circ}{X}\partial Y_{K}+\overset{\circ}{Y}\partial X_{K})}{X_{K}^{2}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})},

•

Hence,

\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\overset{\circ}{X}=N_{X}^{(2)}+N_{X}^{(1)}-\frac{2\partial Y_{K}\cdot\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}}{X_{K}}+\frac{2|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}\overset{\circ}{X}-2\frac{\partial X_{K}\cdot\partial Y_{K}}{X_{K}^{2}}\overset{\circ}{Y}.

which is equivalent to (
5.51
).

•

The equation for
\overset{\circ}{Y}
is derived in the same way.

4.

We derive the equation for
\overset{\circ}{\Theta}
:
\overset{\circ}{\Theta}
is defined by

X^{-1}W=\overset{\circ}{\Theta}+X_{K}^{-1}W_{K}.

Moreover, by (
5.24
), we have

\partial_{\rho}(X^{-1}W)d\rho+\partial_{z}(X^{-1}W)dz=\frac{\sigma}{X^{2}}(\theta_{\rho}dz-\theta_{z}d\rho)

and
X_{K}^{-1}W_{K}
satisfies

\partial_{\rho}(X_{K}^{-1}W_{K})d\rho+\partial_{z}(X_{K}^{-1}W_{K})dz=\frac{\rho}{X_{K}^{2}}(\partial_{\rho}Y_{K}dz-\partial_{z}Y_{K}d\rho).

We recall that

\displaystyle\theta_{\rho}

\displaystyle=\partial_{\rho}Y+B_{\rho},

\displaystyle\theta_{z}

\displaystyle=\partial_{z}Y+B_{z}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}\overset{\circ}{\Theta}d\rho+\partial_{z}\overset{\circ}{\Theta}dz

\displaystyle=\partial_{\rho}(X_{K}^{-1}W_{K})d\rho+\partial_{z}(X_{K}^{-1}W_{K})dz-\left(\partial_{\rho}(X_{K}^{-1}W_{K})d\rho+\partial_{z}(X_{K}^{-1}W_{K})dz\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma}{X^{2}}(\theta_{\rho}dz-\theta_{z}d\rho)-\frac{\rho}{X_{K}^{2}}(\partial_{\rho}Y_{K}dz-\partial_{z}Y_{K}d\rho)

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma}{X^{2}}(\partial_{\rho}Ydz-\partial_{z}Yd\rho)+\frac{\sigma}{X^{2}}(B_{\rho}dz-B_{z}d\rho)-\frac{\rho}{X_{K}^{2}}(\partial_{\rho}Y_{K}dz-\partial_{z}Y_{K}d\rho).

Thus, we find the following pair of equations for
\overset{\circ}{\Theta}
:

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}\overset{\circ}{\Theta}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\sigma}{X^{2}}(\partial_{z}Y+B_{z})+\frac{\rho}{X_{K}^{2}}\partial_{z}Y_{K},

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\overset{\circ}{\Theta}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma}{X^{2}}(\partial_{\rho}Y+B_{\rho})-\frac{\rho}{X_{K}^{2}}\partial_{\rho}Y_{K}.

5.

Finally, we derive the equations for
\overset{\circ}{\lambda}
:
\lambda
satisfies

\partial_{\rho}\lambda=\alpha_{\rho}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\rho}\log X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X}).

This implies

\partial_{\rho}\overset{\circ}{\lambda}=\alpha_{\rho}-(\partial_{\rho}\lambda_{K}+\frac{1}{2}\log X_{K})-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\rho}\log(1+\overset{\circ}{X}).

Similarly,

\partial_{z}\overset{\circ}{\lambda}=\alpha_{z}-(\partial_{z}\lambda_{K}+\frac{1}{2}\log X_{K})-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{z}\log(1+\overset{\circ}{X}).

Now, recall that
\lambda_{K}
satisfies

\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{\rho}\lambda_{K}&=\frac{1}{4}\rho{X_{K}}^{-2}((\partial_{\rho}X_{K})^{2}-(\partial_{z}X_{K})^{2}+(\partial_{\rho}Y_{K})^{2}-(\partial_{z}Y_{K})^{2})-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\rho}\log{X_{K}},\\
\partial_{z}\lambda_{K}&=\frac{1}{4}\rho{X_{K}}^{-2}((\partial_{\rho}X_{K})(\partial_{z}X_{K})+(\partial_{\rho}Y_{K})(\partial_{z}Y_{K}))-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{z}\log{X_{K}}.\end{aligned}\right.

Define
(\alpha_{K})_{\rho}
and
(\alpha_{K})_{z}
by setting

\displaystyle(\alpha_{K})_{\rho}

\displaystyle:=\partial_{\rho}\lambda_{K}+\frac{1}{2}\log X_{K},

\displaystyle(\alpha_{K})_{z}

\displaystyle:=\partial_{z}\lambda_{K}+\frac{1}{2}\log X_{K}.

Therefore,
\overset{\circ}{\lambda}
satisfies

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}\overset{\circ}{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{\rho}-(\alpha_{K})_{\rho}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\rho}\log(1+\overset{\circ}{X}),

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\overset{\circ}{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{z}-(\alpha_{K})_{z}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{z}\log(1+\overset{\circ}{X}).

∎

Remark 25
.

As in
[
17
]
, the order we have presented the renormalised unknowns reflects the order in which we will treat their equations. We will first solve for
\overset{\circ}{\sigma}
, for
B
, for
(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y})
, for
\overset{\circ}{\Theta}
then for
\overset{\circ}{\lambda}
. Indeed, one has to solve for
\overset{\circ}{Y}
and
B
before solving the
\overset{\circ}{\Theta}
equation since the necessary boundary condition to integrate the equation for
\overset{\circ}{\Theta}
.

5.6
Functional spaces on
\overline{\mathscr{B}}

In this section, we define the functional spaces for the renormalised unknowns in order to apply standard elliptic theory to solve non-homogeneous linear problems and to establish non-linear estimates.

Firstly, for any
(x,y,z)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
, let
(\rho,\vartheta,z)\in[0,\infty[\times\mathbb{R}\times(0,2\pi)
be its cylindrical coordinates defined by

\displaystyle x

\displaystyle=\rho\cos\vartheta,

\displaystyle y

\displaystyle=\rho\sin\vartheta,

\displaystyle z

\displaystyle=z.

To any function
f:\overline{\mathscr{B}}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
we associate an axisymmetric function
f_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}:{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
by setting

f_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(x,y,z):=f(\rho(x,y),z).

(5.58)

Now, we introduce the following function spaces which are associated to
\overline{\mathscr{B}}
:

\displaystyle\dot{W}^{k,p}_{axi}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})

\displaystyle:=\left\{f(\rho,z),\quad f_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\in\dot{W}^{k,p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\right\},

\displaystyle W^{k,p}_{axi}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})

\displaystyle:=\left\{f(\rho,z),\quad f_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\in W^{k,p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\right\},

\displaystyle C^{k,\alpha}_{axi}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})

\displaystyle:=\left\{f(\rho,z),\quad f_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\in C^{k,\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\right\},

\displaystyle C^{k,\alpha}_{0,axi}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})

\displaystyle:=\left\{f(\rho,z),\quad f_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\in C^{k,\alpha}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\right\}.

The above spaces can also be seen as the Sobolev and Hölder spaces of axially symmetric functions defined on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
.
Now, for any
(x,y,u,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
, let
(s,\vartheta_{1},\chi,\vartheta_{2})\in[0,\infty[\times(0,2\pi)\times[0,\infty[\times(0,2\pi)
or
(s^{\prime},\vartheta_{1},\chi^{\prime},\vartheta_{2})\in[0,\infty[\times(0,2\pi)\times[0,\infty[\times(0,2\pi)
be its polar coordinates defined by

\displaystyle x

\displaystyle=s\cos\vartheta_{1},

(5.59)

\displaystyle y

\displaystyle=s\sin\vartheta_{1},

\displaystyle u

\displaystyle=\chi\cos\vartheta_{2},

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle=\chi\sin\vartheta_{2}.

Now, to any function
f^{N}:\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
or
f^{S}:\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
, we associate a function
f^{N}_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}:\mathbb{R}^{4}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
or
f^{S}_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}:\mathbb{R}^{4}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
by setting

f^{N}_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}(x,y,u,v):=f^{N}(s(x,y),\chi(u,v)),

f^{S}_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}(x,y,u,v):=f^{N}(s^{\prime}(x,y),\chi^{\prime}(u,v)).

Another family of function spaces will be used during the analysis. They are defined by

\displaystyle\widehat{\dot{W}}^{k,p}_{axi}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})

\displaystyle:=\left\{f(\rho,z),\;(\xi_{N}f)_{\mathbb{R}^{4}},(\xi_{S}f)_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\in\dot{W}^{k,p}(\mathbb{R}^{4}),\;((1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})f)_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\in\dot{W}^{k,p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\right\},

\displaystyle\widehat{W}^{k,p}_{axi}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})

\displaystyle:=\left\{f(\rho,z),\;(\xi_{N}f)_{\mathbb{R}^{4}},(\xi_{S}f)_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\in W^{k,p}(\mathbb{R}^{4}),\;((1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})f)_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\in W^{k,p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\right\},

\displaystyle\widehat{C}^{k,\alpha}_{axi}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})

\displaystyle:=\left\{f(\rho,z),\;(\xi_{N}f)_{\mathbb{R}^{4}},(\xi_{S}f)_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\in C^{k,\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{4}),\;((1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})f)_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\in C^{k,\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\right\},

\displaystyle\widehat{C}^{k,\alpha}_{0,axi}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})

\displaystyle:=\left\{f(\rho,z),\;(\xi_{N}f)_{\mathbb{R}^{4}},(\xi_{S}f)_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\in C^{k,\alpha}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{4}),\;((1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})f)_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\in C^{k,\alpha}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\right\}.

Similarly, we define the spaces
\displaystyle\widehat{C}^{k}_{axi}
and
\displaystyle\widehat{C}^{k}_{axi,0}
, and we set
\displaystyle C^{\infty}:=\cap_{k=1}^{\infty}\widehat{C}^{k}_{axi}
and
\displaystyle C^{\infty}:=\cap_{k=1}^{\infty}\widehat{C}^{k}_{axi,0}
.

Remark 26
.

When there is unlikely to be any confusion, we will often drop the subscript "axi".

We state the following lemma on the relationship between the hatted and non-hatted Hölder spaces

Lemma 47
.

Let
\alpha\in(0,1)
and
k\in\mathbb{N}
. We have the continuous inclusion

C^{k,\alpha}_{axi}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})\subset\hat{C}^{k,\alpha}_{axi}(\overline{\mathscr{B}}).

Proof.

See proof of Lemma 3.2.1 in
[
17
]
.
∎

In the following section, we introduce the function spaces for the renormalised unknowns

\displaystyle\left(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},B,\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}\right),\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda}\right)

.

5.7
Function spaces for the renormalised unknowns

Let
\alpha_{0}\in(0,1)
be fixed and let the function
r:[0,\infty[\times\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
be defined by
r(\rho,z):=\sqrt{1+\rho^{2}+z^{2}}
.
Note that
r(\rho,z)
is different from the radial coordinate appearing in BL coordinates. See Section
4
.

5.7.1
Function spaces for
{\sigma}

Definition 20
.

The Banach space
\left(\mathcal{L}_{\sigma},||\cdot||_{\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}}\right)
is defined to be the completion of smooth functions
f\in\hat{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})
under the norm

\displaystyle||f||_{\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}}

\displaystyle:=||f||_{C^{3,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}+||r^{2}f||_{L^{\infty}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}+||r^{3}\partial f||_{L^{\infty}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}+||r^{4}\log^{-1}(4r)\partial^{2}f||_{L^{\infty}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}

\displaystyle+||r^{4}\log^{-1}(4r)\partial^{2}f||_{C^{0,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}.

The Banach space
\left(\mathcal{N}_{\sigma},||\cdot||_{\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}}\right)
is defined to be the completion of smooth functions
f\in\hat{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})
under the norm

||f||_{\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}}:=||r^{5}f||_{C^{1,\alpha_{0}}({\overline{\mathscr{B}}})}.

5.7.2
Function spaces for
{B}

Definition 21
.

The Banach space
\left(\mathcal{L}_{B},||\cdot||_{\mathcal{L}_{B}}\right)
is defined to be the completion of triples
\left(F_{z}^{(A)},F_{\chi}^{(N)},F_{\chi^{\prime}}^{(S)}\right)\in\left(\hat{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})\right)^{3}
under the norm

\displaystyle||\left(F_{z}^{(A)},F_{\chi}^{(N)},F_{\chi^{\prime}}^{(S)}\right)||_{\mathcal{L}_{B}}

\displaystyle:=\left|\left|\frac{(1+\rho^{10})(1+r^{10})}{\rho^{10}}F_{z}^{(A)}\right|\right|_{\hat{C}^{1,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}})}

\displaystyle+\left|\left|s^{-10}F^{(N)}_{\chi}\right|\right|_{\hat{C}^{1,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}})}+\left|\left|(s^{\prime})^{-10}F^{(S)}_{\chi^{\prime}}\right|\right|_{\hat{C}^{1,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}})}.

The Banach space
\left(\mathcal{N}_{B},||\cdot||_{\mathcal{N}_{B}}\right)
is defined to be the completion of smooth functions
f\in\hat{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})
under the norm

\displaystyle||\left(F_{z}^{(A)},F_{\chi}^{(N)},F_{\chi^{\prime}}^{(S)}\right)||_{\mathcal{N}_{B}}

\displaystyle:=\left|\left|\frac{(1+\rho^{15})(1+r^{10})}{\rho^{15}}F_{z}^{(A)}\right|\right|_{\hat{C}^{1,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}})}

\displaystyle+\left|\left|s^{-15}F^{(N)}_{\chi}\right|\right|_{\hat{C}^{1,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}})}+\left|\left|(s^{\prime})^{-15}F^{(S)}_{\chi^{\prime}}\right|\right|_{\hat{C}^{1,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}})}.

5.7.3
Function spaces for
X
and
Y

Definition 22
.

The Banach space
\left(\mathcal{L}_{X},||\cdot||_{\mathcal{L}_{X}}\right)
is defined to be the completion of smooth functions
f\in\hat{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})
under the norm

||f||_{\mathcal{L}_{X}}:=||f||_{\dot{W}^{2,p}_{axi}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}+||f||_{\hat{C}^{2,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}+||rf||_{L^{\infty}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}+||r^{2}\hat{\partial}f||_{L^{\infty}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}+||r^{3}\log^{-1}(4r)\hat{\partial}^{2}f||_{C^{0,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}.

The Banach space
\left(\mathcal{N}_{X},||\cdot||_{\mathcal{N}_{X}}\right)
is defined to be the completion of smooth functions
f\in\hat{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})
under the norm

||f||_{\mathcal{N}_{X}}:=||r^{3}(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})f||_{C^{0,\alpha_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+||(\chi^{2}+s^{2})\xi_{N}f||_{C^{0,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}})}+||((\chi^{\prime})^{2}+(s^{\prime})^{2})\xi_{S}f||_{C^{0,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}})}.

Definition 23
.

The Banach space
\left(\mathcal{L}_{Y},||\cdot||_{\mathcal{L}_{Y}}\right)
is defined to be the completion of smooth functions
f\in\hat{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})
under the norm

\displaystyle||f||_{\mathcal{L}_{Y}}

\displaystyle:=||f||_{\dot{W}^{2,p}_{axi}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}+|||\partial h|f||_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+||f||_{\hat{C}^{2,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}+||X_{K}^{-1}f||_{\hat{C}^{2,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}+||r^{3}X_{K}^{-1}f||_{L^{\infty}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}

\displaystyle+||r^{4}\hat{\partial}(X^{-1}_{K}f)||_{L^{\infty}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}+||r^{5}\log^{-1}(4r)\hat{\partial}^{2}(X^{-1}_{K}f)||_{C^{0,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}.

The Banach space
\left(\mathcal{N}_{Y},||\cdot||_{\mathcal{N}_{Y}}\right)
is defined to be the completion of smooth functions
f\in\hat{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})
under the norm

\displaystyle||f||_{\mathcal{N}_{Y}}

\displaystyle:=||fr^{5}X^{-1}_{K}||_{\hat{C}^{0,\alpha_{0}}\left(\left(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\right)\cap\left\{\rho\leq 1\right\}\right)}+||fr^{4}||_{\hat{C}^{0,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}}\cap\left\{\rho\geq 1\right\})}+||(\chi^{2}+s^{2})\xi_{N}f||_{C^{0,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}})}

\displaystyle+||((\chi^{\prime})^{2}+(s^{\prime})^{2})\xi_{S}f||_{C^{0,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}})}.

5.7.4
Function spaces for
\Theta

Definition 24
.

The Banach space
\left(\mathcal{L}_{\Theta},||\cdot||_{\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}}\right)
is defined to be the completion of smooth functions
f\in\hat{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})
under the norm

||f||_{\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}}:=||r^{2}f||_{\hat{C}^{2,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}.

The Banach space
\left(\mathcal{N}_{\Theta},||\cdot||_{\mathcal{N}_{\Theta}}\right)
is defined to be the completion of pairs of smooth compatly supported closed
1-
forms
F
under the norm

\displaystyle||F||_{\mathcal{N}_{\Theta}}

\displaystyle:=||r^{3}(1+\rho^{-1})F_{\rho}||_{\hat{C}^{1,\alpha_{0}}\left(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\right)}+||r^{3}F_{z}||_{\hat{C}^{1,\alpha_{0}}(\left(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\right))}

\displaystyle+||s^{-1}F_{s}||_{\hat{C}^{1,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}})}+||F_{\chi}||_{\hat{C}^{1,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}})}

\displaystyle+||(s^{\prime})^{-1}F_{s^{\prime}}||_{\hat{C}^{1,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}})}+||F_{\chi^{\prime}}||_{\hat{C}^{1,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}})}

5.7.5

\lambda

Definition 25
.

The Banach space
\left(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda},||\cdot||_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}\right)
is defined to be the completion of smooth functions
f\in\hat{C}^{\infty}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})
under the norm

||f||_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}:=||f||_{\hat{C}^{1,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}.

It is easy to see that all these spaces are Banach spaces.

6
Main Result

In this section, we give a more detailed formulation of our result.
More precisely, we have

Theorem 3
.

Let

1.

0<|a|<M
and
\mathcal{M}=\mathbb{R}\times(0,2\pi)\times\mathscr{B}
be the the domain of outer communications minus the axis of symmetry parametrised by the standard Weyl coordinates
(t,\phi,\rho,z)
,

2.

\mathcal{B}_{bound}\subset\subset\mathcal{A}_{bound}
be a compact subset of the set
\mathcal{A}_{bound}
defined by Definition
16
,

3.

\Phi:\mathcal{A}_{bound}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}^{\ast}_{+}
be a
C^{2}
function with respect to the first two variables,
C^{1}
with respect to the third variable and such that

•

\displaystyle\forall\delta\in[0,\infty[\;
,
\displaystyle\Phi(\cdot,\cdot;\delta)
is supported in
\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{bound}
.

•

\displaystyle\forall(E,\ell)\in\mathcal{A}_{bound}\,,\;\;\Phi(E,\ell;0)=\partial_{\ell}\Phi(E,\ell;0)=0
and
\displaystyle\forall\delta>0,\Phi(\cdot,\cdot,\delta)
does not identically vanish on
\mathcal{B}_{bound}
.

Then, there exists
\delta_{0}>0
and a one-parameter family of functions

(V_{\delta},W_{\delta},X_{\delta},\lambda_{\delta})_{\delta\in[0,\delta_{0}[}\in(C^{2,\alpha}(\mathscr{B}))^{4},\;f^{\delta}\in C^{2}(\mathscr{B}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})

with the following properties

1.

(V_{0},W_{0},X_{0},\lambda_{0})=(V_{K},W_{K},X_{K},\lambda_{K})
corresponds to a Kerr solution with parameters
(a,M)
.

2.

For all
(E,\ell_{z})\in\mathcal{B}_{bound}
, the equation

E_{\ell_{z}}(W_{\delta},X_{\delta},\sigma_{\delta},\rho,z)=E^{2}

(6.1)

admits a unique solution curve with two connected components, such that one of them is diffeomorphic to
\mathbb{S}^{1}
. Moreover, there exists
\eta>0
depending only on
\delta_{0}
such that

\rho_{0}(W_{\delta},X_{\delta},\sigma_{\delta},E,\ell_{z})+\eta<\rho_{1}(W_{\delta},X_{\delta},\sigma_{\delta},E,\ell_{z})

where
\displaystyle\rho_{i}(W_{\delta},X_{\delta},\sigma_{\delta},E,\ell_{z})
are the two smallest solutions of the equation (
6.1
) with
z=0
.

3.

The function
\displaystyle f^{\delta}
takes the form

f^{\delta}(x,v)=\Phi(E^{\delta},\ell;\delta)\Psi_{\eta}\left(r,(\varepsilon_{\delta},(\ell_{z})_{\delta}),W_{\delta},X_{\delta},\sigma_{\delta}\right),

for
(x,v)\in\mathcal{O}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
with coordinates
(t,r,\theta,\phi,v^{r},v^{\theta},v^{\phi})
and where

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\delta}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma_{\delta}}{\sqrt{X_{\delta}}}(1+|p|^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}-\frac{W_{\delta}}{X_{\delta}}(\ell_{z})_{\delta},

\displaystyle(\ell_{z})_{\delta}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{X_{\delta}}p^{\phi},

and
\Psi_{\eta}
is defined by (
5.4
).

4.

Let
g_{\delta}
be defined on
\overline{\mathscr{B}}
by

g_{\delta}:=-V_{\delta}dt^{2}+2W_{\delta}dtd\phi+X_{\delta}d\phi^{2}+e^{2\lambda_{\delta}}\left(d\rho^{2}+dz^{2}\right)

then
(\mathcal{O},g_{\delta},f^{\delta})
is a stationary and axially symmetric solution to the Einstein-Vlasov system (
3.1
) - (
3.7
) - (
3.9
) describing a matter shell orbiting a Kerr like black hole in the following sense:

•

\displaystyle\exists\rho_{min}^{\delta},\rho_{max}^{\delta}\in]0,\infty[
and
Z_{min}^{\delta},Z_{max}^{\delta}\in\mathbb{R}
which satisfy

\displaystyle\rho_{min}^{\delta}<\rho_{max}^{\delta},\quad\text{and}\quad\rho_{min}^{\delta}>\rho^{mb,+}(a,M)

\displaystyle Z_{min}^{\delta}<0<Z_{max}^{\delta},

•

we have

\supp_{(\rho,z)}f\subset\subset\left[\rho_{min}(h),\rho_{max}(h)\right]\times\left[Z_{min}(h),Z_{max}(h)\right].

and
\exists(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{z})\in\left[\rho_{min}(h),\rho_{max}(h)\right]\times\left[Z_{min}(h),Z_{max}(h)\right],

f^{\delta}(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{z},\cdot)>0.

•

The region
\mathscr{H}=\left\{(\rho,z)\;:\;\rho=0\,,\,|z|<\beta\right\}
corresponds to a non-degenerate bifurcate Killing horizon on which the metric has a
C^{2,\alpha}
extension in sense of Definition
4
.

Overview of the poof

In this section, we give an overview of the proof of Theorem
3
.

1.

First of all, we show in Section
7
that the compact connected component of ZVC associated to a trapped geodesic with parameters
(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\in\mathcal{B}_{bound}
moving in a Kerr exterior,
Z^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
, remains stable under stationary and axisymmetric perturbations of the Kerr metric. Then, using the compactness of
\mathcal{B}_{bound}
, we show that trapped geodesics moving in the perturbed spacetimes lie in a compact region of
\mathscr{B}
which is uniform in
(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
. This allows us to obtain a distribution function which is compactly supported in
\mathscr{B}
. Consequently, all the matter terms
F_{i}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma})
are compactly supported in
\mathscr{B}
and vanish in a neighbourhoods of the horizon, the axis of symmetry and the poles.

2.

Then, to resolve the nonlinear aspects of the problem, we will used two fixed point lemmas, which are introduced in Section
8.1
. We will start with the study of a toy model which illustrates the application of these lemmas. In the general case, we will have to deal with the difficulty related to the nonlinear coupling of the equations.

3.

At this stage, we introduce a bifurcation parameter
\delta\geq 0
in the ansatz for the distribution function which turns on the presence of Vlasov matter. This allows us to transform the problem of finding solutions to the reduced EV system for the renormalised quantities into that of finding a one-parameter family of solutions which depends on
\delta
, by applying a fixed point lemma, considered as a zero of a well-defined operator.

4.

Note that we will solve each equation separately and the order in which we solve them matters. See Remark
25
. More precisely:

•

We begin by solving the equation for
\overset{\circ}{\sigma}
in terms of the remaining quantities and the bifurcation parameter
\delta
. The regularity for the matter terms will allow us to have a
C^{1}
dependence of
\overset{\circ}{\sigma}
in
(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda})
and a continuous dependance with respect to
\delta
. To this end, we apply a fixed point lemma.

•

Then, we solve the equations for
B
in terms of
(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta})
. Note that
\overset{\circ}{\sigma}
depends on the other renormalised quantities and
\delta
. Therefore, after the application of the fixed point theorem, we will obtain a one parameter family of solutions
(B,\overset{\circ}{\sigma})
which depend in
C^{1}
manner of
(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda})
and continuously on
\delta
.

•

We iterate the solving process in order to solve the equations for
(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y})
in terms of
(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta)
, then
\overset{\circ}{\Theta}
in terms of
(\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta)
and finally the equations for
\overset{\circ}{\lambda}
in terms of
\delta
only.

•

Consequently, we obtain a one-parameter family of solutions
(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},B,\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda})
which depends continuously on
\delta
.

7
Perturbation of trapped Kerr geodesics

In this section, we show that given a compact set of parameters
(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
leading to trapped orbits, the latter remain stable under small metric data perturbations.

First of all, let
\mathcal{B}_{bound}
be a compact subset of the form (
38
) and let
(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\in\mathcal{B}_{bound}
. We recall from
16
that

1.

there exists
\displaystyle\Phi^{K,abs}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}:]-\beta,\beta[\mapsto]0,\rho_{0}^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})]
such that

Z^{K,abs}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})=\text{Gr}\left(\Phi^{K,abs}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}\right),

2.

there exist
\displaystyle\Phi^{K,i}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}:I^{i}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}\mapsto\mathbb{R},\;i=1\cdots 4
such that

Z^{K,trapped}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})=\bigcup_{i=1\cdots 4}\text{Gr}\left(\Phi^{K,i}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}\right),

where
\displaystyle I^{i}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})},\Phi^{K,i}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}
and
\displaystyle\Phi^{K,abs}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}
are defined in (
4.168
).

Furthermore, we recall from Lemma
42
that
\forall(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\in\mathcal{B}_{bound}
, there exist
\mathscr{B}_{i},\;i=1\cdots 5
and
\mathscr{B}^{abs}
such that

\mathscr{B}=\mathscr{B}^{abs}\cup\left(\cup_{i=1\cdots 5}\mathscr{B}_{i}\right).

(7.1)

Before we state the main result of this section, we give the following definition of
\delta
-perturbations of a zero velocity curve,
Z^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
associated to a timelike future-directed geodesic with constants of motion
(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\in\mathcal{A}_{bound}
moving in Kerr exterior.

Definition 26

(
Z^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
perturbations)
.

Let
(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\in\mathcal{A}_{bound}
and
\delta_{0}>0
. A
\delta_{0}
-perturbation of
Z^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
is a continuous one-parameter family of curves
(Z(\delta,\varepsilon,\ell_{z}))_{\delta\in[0,\delta_{0}[}
such that

1.

\displaystyle Z(0,\varepsilon,\ell_{z})=Z^{K}(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
.

2.

\forall\delta\in[0,\delta_{0}[
,
Z(\delta,\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
consists of two connected components:
\displaystyle Z^{abs}(\delta,\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
which is diffeomorphic to
\mathbb{R}
and
Z^{trapped}(\delta,\varepsilon,\ell_{z})
which is diffeomorphic to
\mathbb{S}^{1}
and such that

•

there exists
\Phi_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}^{\delta,abs}:]-\beta,\beta[\to]0,\infty[
such that

Z^{abs}(\delta,\varepsilon,\ell_{z})=\text{Gr}\left(\Phi^{\delta,abs}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}\right)

and

\left|\left|\Phi^{\delta,abs}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}-\Phi^{K,abs}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}\right|\right|_{C^{1}(]-\beta,\beta[)}<\delta_{0}.

•

there exist
\displaystyle\Phi^{\delta,i}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}:I^{i}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}\mapsto\mathbb{R},\;i=1\cdots 4
such that

Z^{trapped}(\delta,\varepsilon,\ell_{z})=\bigcup_{i=1\cdots 4}\text{Gr}\left(\Phi^{\delta,i}_{(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})}\right),

and
