\omega\ell_{2}^{-}\leq-\omega\rho s\leq\omega\ell_{1}^{-}\quad\text{and}\quad E_{\rho s}(h,\rho,z)\leq\varepsilon_{2}^{-}.

Hence,

\tilde{E}(s,h,\rho,z)\leq\varepsilon^{-}_{2}-\omega\rho s\leq\varepsilon^{-}_{2}-\omega\ell_{1}^{-}.

Since
\tilde{E}
is monotonically decreasing on
\mathbb{R}_{-}
with respect to
s
, we obtain a lower bound on
s
:

s\geq-\tilde{L}(\varepsilon_{2}^{-}-\omega\ell_{1}^{-},h,\rho,z)

and we also have

s\leq\frac{\ell_{2}^{-}}{\rho}.

This ends the proof.

∎

Finally, we set
f_{\phi_{\pm},\eta}^{i,\pm}
to be

\displaystyle f_{\Phi_{\pm},\eta}^{1,\pm}(s,h,\rho,z)

\displaystyle:=\int_{E_{\rho s}(h,\rho,z)}^{\varepsilon^{\pm}_{2}}(X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})+2(\rho s)^{2})\Phi_{\pm}(\xi,\rho s)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(\xi,\rho s),h)\,d\xi,

(9.18)

\displaystyle f_{\Phi_{\pm},\eta}^{2,\pm}(s,h,\rho,z)

\displaystyle:=\int_{E_{\rho s}(h,\rho,z)}^{\varepsilon^{\pm}_{2}}\rho s(\xi-\rho\left(-\overset{\circ}{\Theta}+\omega_{K}\right)s)\Phi_{\pm}(\xi,\rho s)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(\xi,\rho s),h)\,d\xi,

(9.19)

\displaystyle f_{\Phi_{\pm},\eta}^{3,\pm}(s,h,\rho,z)

\displaystyle:=\int_{E_{\rho s}(h,\rho,z)}^{\varepsilon^{\pm}_{2}}\left(\tilde{L}^{2}\left(\xi-\rho\left(-\overset{\circ}{\Theta}+\omega_{K}\right)s,X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X}),\sigma,\rho,z\right)-s^{2}\right)\Phi_{\pm}(\xi,\rho s)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(\xi,\rho s),h)\,d\xi,

(9.20)

\displaystyle f_{\Phi_{\pm},\eta}^{4,\pm}(s,h,\rho,z)

\displaystyle:=\int_{E_{\rho s}(h,\rho,z)}^{\varepsilon^{\pm}_{2}}g^{4}(s,h,\rho,z,\xi)\Phi_{\pm}(\xi,\rho s)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(\xi,\rho s),h)\,d\xi,\;\text{where}

(9.21)

\displaystyle g^{4}(s,h,\rho,z,\xi)

\displaystyle:=\left(\frac{X_{K}^{2}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})^{2}}{\rho^{4}\left(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma}\right)^{2}}\left(\xi-\rho\left(-\overset{\circ}{\Theta}+\omega_{K}\right)s\right)^{2}+\left(1-\frac{X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})}{\rho^{2}}\right)\left(1+\frac{\rho^{2}}{X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})}s^{2}\right)\right)

(9.22)

Hence,
F_{i}(h)
are given by:

•

\displaystyle\forall(\rho,z)\in\mathscr{B}_{A}

F_{i}(h,\overset{\circ}{\lambda})(\rho,z):=F_{+}^{i}(h,\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\rho,z)+F_{-}^{i}(h,\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\rho,z),

(9.23)

where

\displaystyle F_{+}^{1}(h,\rho,z)

\displaystyle:=-\frac{2\pi e^{2\left(\overset{\circ}{\lambda}+\lambda_{K}\right)}}{1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma}}\int_{\frac{\ell_{1}^{+}}{\rho}}^{\tilde{L}(\varepsilon_{2}^{+},h,\rho,z)}\;f_{\Phi_{+},\eta}^{1,+}(s,h,\rho,z)\,ds,

(9.24)

\displaystyle F_{+}^{2}(h,\rho,z)

\displaystyle:=\frac{2\pi X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})}{1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma}}\int_{\frac{\ell_{1}^{+}}{\rho}}^{\tilde{L}(\varepsilon_{2}^{+},h,\rho,z)}\;f_{\Phi_{+},\eta}^{2,+}(s,h,\rho,z)\,ds,

(9.25)

\displaystyle F_{+}^{3}(h,\rho,z)

\displaystyle:=\frac{\rho^{4}}{X_{K}^{2}}\frac{2\pi(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma})}{(1+\overset{\circ}{X})^{2}}\int_{\frac{\ell_{1}^{+}}{\rho}}^{\tilde{L}(\varepsilon_{2}^{+},h,\rho,z)}\;f_{\Phi_{+},\eta}^{3,+}(s,h,\rho,z)\,ds,

(9.26)

\displaystyle F_{+}^{4}(h,\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\rho,z)

\displaystyle:=-\frac{4\pi e^{2\left(\overset{\circ}{\lambda}+\lambda_{K}\right)}}{1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma}}\int_{\frac{\ell_{1}^{+}}{\rho}}^{\tilde{L}(\varepsilon_{2}^{+},h,\rho,z)}\;f_{\Phi_{+},\eta}^{4,+}(s,h,\rho,z)\,ds,

(9.27)

\displaystyle F_{-}^{1}(h,\rho,z)

\displaystyle:=-\frac{2\pi e^{2\left(\overset{\circ}{\lambda}+\lambda_{K}\right)}}{1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma}}\int^{\frac{\ell_{1}^{-}}{\rho}}_{-\tilde{L}(\varepsilon_{2}^{-},h,\rho,z)}\;f_{\Phi_{-},\eta}^{1,-}(s,h,\rho,z)\,ds,

(9.28)

\displaystyle F_{-}^{2}(h,\rho,z)

\displaystyle:=\frac{2\pi X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})}{1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma}}\int^{\frac{\ell_{1}^{-}}{\rho}}_{-\tilde{L}(\varepsilon_{2}^{-},h,\rho,z)}\;f_{\Phi_{-},\eta}^{2,-}(s,h,\rho,z)\,ds,

(9.29)

\displaystyle F_{-}^{3}(h,\rho,z)

\displaystyle:=\frac{\rho^{4}}{X_{K}^{2}}\frac{2\pi(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma})}{(1+\overset{\circ}{X})^{2}}\int^{\frac{\ell_{1}^{-}}{\rho}}_{-\tilde{L}(\varepsilon_{2}^{-},h,\rho,z)}\;f_{\Phi_{-},\eta}^{3,-}(s,h,\rho,z)\,ds,

(9.30)

\displaystyle F_{-}^{4}(h,\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\rho,z)

\displaystyle:=-\frac{4\pi e^{2\left(\overset{\circ}{\lambda}+\lambda_{K}\right)}}{1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma}}\int^{\frac{\ell_{1}^{-}}{\rho}}_{-\tilde{L}(\varepsilon_{2}^{-},h,\rho,z)}\;f_{\Phi_{-},\eta}^{4,-}(s,h,\rho,z)\,ds.

(9.31)

•

Otherwise, they vanish.

Following the previous lemma, we obtain

Lemma 59
.

\forall h\in B_{\delta_{0}}\left(\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}\right)
, there exists an open neighbourhood of the axis of symmetry
\mathscr{A}
, say
\tilde{\mathscr{A}}\subset\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}
, such that the matter terms
F^{i}(h,\cdot)
vanish in
\tilde{\mathscr{A}}
.

Proof.

Recall that
\mathscr{A}\subset\left\{(\rho,z)\,:\;\rho=0\right\}
. By Proposition
22
, we have

\supp_{(\rho,z)}f\subset\subset\left[\rho_{min}(h),\rho_{max}(h)\right]\times\left[Z_{min}(h),Z_{max}(h)\right].

Since
\rho_{min}(h)>0
, we choose any open subset of
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\cap([0,\rho_{min}(h)[\times\mathbb{R})
.
∎

Before we study the regularity of the matter terms, we state the following lemmas from which we obtain the regularity of the different matter terms.

Lemma 60
.

Let
u\in\mathbb{R}
and define the mapping
\displaystyle E_{u}:B_{\delta_{0}}\subset\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}\to\hat{C}^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})
by

E_{u}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma})(\rho,z):=E_{\rho u}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma},\rho,z)=-\overset{\circ}{\Theta}\rho u-W_{K}X_{K}^{-1}\rho u+\frac{\rho}{X_{K}}\frac{\overset{\circ}{\sigma}+1}{\overset{\circ}{X}+1}\sqrt{(\rho u)^{2}+X_{K}(\overset{\circ}{X}+1)}.

Then
E_{u}
is well defined on
B_{\delta_{0}}
and it is continuously Fréchet differentiable on
B_{\delta_{0}}
with derivative:
\displaystyle\forall(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma})\in B_{\delta_{0}}\,,\,\forall(\tilde{\Theta},\tilde{X},\tilde{\sigma})\in\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}\,,\,\forall(\rho,z)\in\overline{\mathscr{B}}

\displaystyle DE_{u}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma})[\tilde{\Theta},\tilde{X},\tilde{\sigma}](\rho,z)=

(9.32)

\displaystyle-\rho u\tilde{\Theta}+\frac{\rho}{X_{K}}\frac{\tilde{\sigma}}{1+\overset{\circ}{X}}\sqrt{\rho^{2}u^{2}+X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})}+\frac{\rho}{X_{K}}\tilde{X}(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma})

\displaystyle\left(-\frac{\sqrt{\rho^{2}u^{2}+X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})}}{(1+\overset{\circ}{X})^{2}}+\frac{X_{K}}{2(1+\overset{\circ}{X})\sqrt{\rho^{2}u^{2}+X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})}}\right)

Proof.

Let
u\in\mathbb{R}
and define, for
x\in]-\delta_{0},\delta_{0}[^{3},(\rho,z)\in\overline{\mathscr{B}}
,

\mathscr{E}_{u}(x,(\rho,z)):=x_{1}\rho u-W_{K}X_{K}^{-1}(\rho,z)\rho u+\frac{\rho}{X_{K}(\rho,z)}\frac{1+x_{2}}{1+x_{3}}\sqrt{(\rho u)^{2}+X_{K}(\rho,z)(x_{2}+1)}.

By Proposition
4
,
\forall u\in\mathbb{R}\,,\;\mathscr{E}_{u}
is well-defined on
\overline{\mathscr{B}}
and it is smooth on
]-\delta_{0},\delta_{0}[^{3}\times\overline{\mathscr{B}}
. Moreover,
\forall u\in\mathbb{R},\forall(x,(\rho,z))\in]-\delta_{0},\delta_{0}[^{3}\times\overline{\mathscr{B}}\;:

\partial_{x}\mathscr{E}_{u}(x,(\rho,z))=\left(\begin{aligned} &\rho u\\
&\frac{\rho}{X_{K}(\rho,z)}\frac{1}{1+x_{3}}\left(\sqrt{(\rho u)^{2}+X_{K}(\rho,z)(x_{2}+1)}+\frac{(1+x_{2})X_{K}(\rho,z)}{2\sqrt{(\rho u)^{2}+X_{K}(\rho,z)(x_{2}+1)}}\right)\\
&-\frac{\rho}{X_{K}(\rho,z)}\frac{1+x_{2}}{(1+x_{3})^{2}}\sqrt{(\rho u)^{2}+X_{K}(\rho,z)(x_{2}+1)}\end{aligned}\right)

Now, we set

DE_{u}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma})[\tilde{\Theta},\tilde{X},\tilde{\sigma}](\rho,z)=((\tilde{\Theta}(\rho,z)\;\;\tilde{X}(\rho,z)\;\;\tilde{\sigma}(\rho,z))\partial_{x}\mathscr{E}_{u}((\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma})(\rho,z),(\rho,z)).

Set
h=(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma})
and
\tilde{h}=(\tilde{\Theta},\tilde{X},\tilde{\sigma})
. We will show that for

\lim_{||\tilde{h}||_{\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}}\to 0}\frac{||E_{u}(h+\tilde{h})-E_{u}(h)-DE_{u}(h)[\tilde{h}]||_{\hat{C}^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}}{||\tilde{h}||_{\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}}}

\forall(\rho,z)\in\overline{\mathscr{B}}
, we have

\displaystyle E_{u}(h+\tilde{h})(\rho,z)-E_{u}(h)(\rho,z)-DE_{u}(h)[\tilde{h}](\rho,z)=

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{u}(h(\rho,z)+\tilde{h}(\rho,z),(\rho,z))-\mathscr{E}_{u}(h(\rho,z),(\rho,z))-\tilde{h}(\rho,z)\cdot\partial_{x}\mathscr{E}_{u}(h(\rho,z),(\rho,z)).

Denote by
′
the differential with respect to
(\rho,z)
, then

\displaystyle\left(E_{u}(h+\tilde{h})(\rho,z)-E_{u}(h)(\rho,z)-DE_{u}(h)[\tilde{h}](\rho,z)\right)^{\prime}=

\displaystyle(\partial_{x}\mathscr{E}_{u}(h(\rho,z)+\tilde{h}(\rho,z),(\rho,z)))^{t}(h^{\prime}(\rho,z)+\tilde{h}^{\prime}(\rho,z))+(\mathscr{E}_{u})^{\prime}(h(\rho,z)+\tilde{h}(\rho,z),(\rho,z))

\displaystyle-(\partial_{x}\mathscr{E}_{u}(h(\rho,z),(\rho,z)))^{t}(h^{\prime}(\rho,z))-(\mathscr{E}_{u})^{\prime}(h(\rho,z),(\rho,z))

\displaystyle-(\partial_{x}\mathscr{E}_{u}(h(\rho,z),(\rho,z)))^{t}\tilde{h}^{\prime}(\rho,z)-\tilde{h}(\rho,z)(\partial_{x}\mathscr{E}_{u})^{\prime}(h(\rho,z),(\rho,z))-\tilde{h}(\rho,z)\partial_{xx}\mathscr{E}_{u}(h(\rho,z),(\rho,z))h^{\prime}(\rho,z).

In the same way, we compute the second derivatives. We provide only the second derivative of the term
E_{u}(h)(\rho,z)
in order to detail the Hölder estimates. The other terms are estimated using similar arguments:

\displaystyle E_{u}(h)^{\prime\prime}(\rho,z)

\displaystyle=((\partial_{x}\mathscr{E}_{u}(h(\rho,z),(\rho,z)))^{t}(h^{\prime}(\rho,z)))^{\prime}+((\mathscr{E}_{u})^{\prime}(h(\rho,z),(\rho,z)))^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{3}(\partial_{x}\mathscr{E}_{u})_{i}(h(\rho,z),(\rho,z))h^{\prime\prime}_{i}(\rho,z)

\displaystyle+h^{\prime}(\rho,z)^{t}(\mathscr{E}_{u})^{\prime}(h(\rho,z),(\rho,z))+((\partial_{x}\mathscr{E}_{u})^{\prime}(h(\rho,z),(\rho,z)))^{t}h^{\prime\prime}(\rho,z)+(\mathscr{E}_{u})^{\prime\prime}(h(\rho,z),(\rho,z))

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{3}(h^{\prime}(\rho,z))^{t}(\partial_{xx}\mathscr{E}_{u})_{i}(h(\rho,z),(\rho,z))h^{\prime}(\rho,z).

Now, we estimate the
C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})
norm of
E_{u}(h+\tilde{h})-E_{u}(h)-DE_{u}(h)[\tilde{h}]

1.

Since
\mathscr{E}_{u}
is smooth and bounded with respect to
x
, there exists
C=C(u,\delta_{0})
such that
\forall(\rho,z)\in\overline{\mathscr{B}}

\left|E_{u}(h+\tilde{h})(\rho,z)-E_{u}(h)(\rho,z)-DE_{u}(h)[\tilde{h}](\rho,z)\right|\leq C||\tilde{h}||^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}}.

2.

Moreover,

\displaystyle(\mathscr{E}_{u})^{\prime}(h(\rho,z)+\tilde{h}(\rho,z),(\rho,z))-(\mathscr{E}_{u})^{\prime}(h(\rho,z),(\rho,z))-\tilde{h}(\rho,z)(\partial_{x}\mathscr{E}_{u})^{\prime}(h(\rho,z),(\rho,z))=

\displaystyle(\mathscr{E}_{u})^{\prime}(h(\rho,z)+\tilde{h}(\rho,z),(\rho,z))-(\mathscr{E}_{u})^{\prime}(h(\rho,z),(\rho,z))-\tilde{h}(\rho,z)(\partial_{x}(\mathscr{E}_{u})^{\prime})(h(\rho,z),(\rho,z))=

\displaystyle O(|\tilde{h}(\rho,z)|^{2}).

Since
\mathscr{E}_{u}
is smooth and bounded with respect to
(\rho,z)
and their derivatives with respect to
(\rho,z)
are also bounded on
\overline{\mathscr{B}}
by Proposition
4
, then there exists
C=C(u,\delta_{0})>0
such that
\forall(\rho,z)\in\overline{\mathscr{B}}
, we have

\left|(\mathscr{E}_{u})^{\prime}(h(\rho,z)+\tilde{h}(\rho,z),(\rho,z))-(\mathscr{E}_{u})^{\prime}(h(\rho,z),(\rho,z))-\tilde{h}(\rho,z)(\partial_{x}\mathscr{E}_{u})^{\prime}(h(\rho,z),(\rho,z))\right|\leq C||\tilde{h}||^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}}.\\

3.

Furthermore,

\displaystyle(\partial_{x}\mathscr{E}_{u}(h(\rho,z)+\tilde{h}(\rho,z),(\rho,z)))^{t}(h^{\prime}(\rho,z)+\tilde{h}^{\prime}(\rho,z))-(\partial_{x}\mathscr{E}_{u}(h(\rho,z),(\rho,z)))^{t}(h^{\prime}(\rho,z))

\displaystyle-(\partial_{x}\mathscr{E}_{u}(h(\rho,z),(\rho,z)))^{t}\tilde{h}^{\prime}(\rho,z)-\tilde{h}(\rho,z)\partial_{xx}\mathscr{E}_{u}(h(\rho,z),(\rho,z))h^{\prime}(\rho,z)=

\displaystyle(\partial_{x}\mathscr{E}_{u}(h(\rho,z)+\tilde{h}(\rho,z),(\rho,z))-\partial_{x}\mathscr{E}_{u}(h(\rho,z),(\rho,z))-\partial_{xx}\mathscr{E}_{u}(h(\rho,z),(\rho,z))(\tilde{h}(\rho,z))^{t})^{t}(h^{\prime}(\rho,z))

\displaystyle+\left(\partial_{x}\mathscr{E}_{u}(h(\rho,z)+\tilde{h}(\rho,z),(\rho,z))-\partial_{x}\mathscr{E}_{u}(h(\rho,z),(\rho,z)))^{t}(h^{\prime}(\rho,z))\right)

\displaystyle(\partial_{x}\mathscr{E}_{u}(h(\rho,z)+\tilde{h}(\rho,z),(\rho,z))-\partial_{x}\mathscr{E}_{u}(h(\rho,z),(\rho,z))-\partial_{xx}\mathscr{E}_{u}(h(\rho,z),(\rho,z))(\tilde{h}(\rho,z))^{t})^{t}(\tilde{h}^{\prime}(\rho,z))

\displaystyle+(\tilde{h}(\rho,z)\partial_{x}\mathscr{E}_{u}(h(\rho,z),(\rho,z)))^{t}\tilde{h}^{\prime}(\rho,z)

\displaystyle=(h^{\prime}(\rho,z))O((\tilde{h}(\rho,z))^{2})+O(|\tilde{h}^{\prime}(\rho,z)||\tilde{h}(\rho,z)|)

Thus, there exists
C=C(u,\delta_{0})>0
such that
\forall(\rho,z)
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\left(E_{u}(h+\tilde{h})(\rho,z)-E_{u}(h)(\rho,z)-DE_{u}(h)[\tilde{h}](\rho,z)\right)^{\prime}\right|\leq C||\tilde{h}||^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}}.

4.

The
C^{2,\alpha}
estimates and the Hölder semi-norm of the second derivatives are obtained as above: we use the smoothness ad boundedness of
\mathscr{E}_{u}
as well as its derivatives.

Therefore,
E_{u}
is Fréchet-differentiable on
B_{\delta_{0}}
and its Fréchet differential is given by
It remains to show that
DE_{u}
is continuous on
B_{\delta_{0}}
. Let
h_{1},h_{2}\in B_{\delta_{0}}
and let
\tilde{h}\in\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}
such that
||\tilde{h}||_{\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}}\leq 1
. We have

\displaystyle||DE_{u}(h_{1})[\tilde{h}]-DE_{u}(h_{2})[\tilde{h}]||_{\hat{C}^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}

\displaystyle=||\tilde{h}\cdot(\partial_{x}\mathscr{E}_{u}(h_{1}(\cdot),\cdot)-\partial_{x}\mathscr{E}_{u}(h_{2}(\cdot),\cdot))||_{\hat{C}^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}

\displaystyle\leq||\tilde{h}||_{\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}}\;||(\partial_{x}\mathscr{E}_{u}(h_{1}(\cdot),\cdot)-\partial_{x}\mathscr{E}_{u}(h_{2}(\cdot),\cdot))||_{\hat{C}^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}

Since
\mathscr{E}_{u}
is smooth, we have
\forall(\rho,z)\in\overline{\mathscr{B}},

\partial_{x}\mathscr{E}_{u}(h_{1}(\rho,z),(\rho,z))-\partial_{x}\mathscr{E}_{u}(h_{2}(\rho,z),(\rho,z))=\partial_{xx}\mathscr{E}_{u}(h_{1}(\rho,z),(\rho,z))\cdot(h_{1}(\rho,z)-h_{2}(\rho,z))^{t}+O(|h_{1}(\rho,z)-h_{2}(\rho,z)|^{2})

Now, since
h_{1}\in B_{\delta_{0}}
and by Proposition
4
, there exists
C=C(u,\delta_{0})
such that
\forall(\rho,z)\in\overline{\mathscr{B}}

|\partial_{xx}\mathscr{E}_{u}(h_{1}(\rho,z),(\rho,z))|\leq C\quad\text{and}\quad O(|h_{1}(\rho,z)-h_{2}(\rho,z)|^{2})=O(||h_{1}-h_{2}||^{2}).

Consequently,

||(\partial_{x}\mathscr{E}_{u}(h_{1}(\cdot),\cdot)-\partial_{x}\mathscr{E}_{u}(h_{2}(\cdot),\cdot))||_{\hat{C}^{0}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}\leq C(u,\delta_{0})||h_{1}-h_{2}||.

In the same way, we estimate the
\hat{C}^{1}
,
\hat{C}^{2}
norms and the Hölder part.
We conclude that
E_{u}
is continuously Fréchet differentiable on
B_{\delta_{0}}

∎

Remark 29
.

As in the previous lemma, we will show the Fréchet differentiabilty of functionals of
(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma})
defined on
B_{\delta_{0}}(0)\subset\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}
which also depend (smoothly) on
(\rho,z)\in\overline{\mathscr{B}}
. The steps are similar to what has been done in the previous lemma and we only compute the differential in the proofs.

Lemma 61
.

Let
E\in\mathbb{R}
and consider the mapping
\tilde{L}(E,\cdot,\cdot)\,:\,B_{\delta_{0}}\subset(\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\sigma})\to\widehat{C}^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}})
defined by

\tilde{L}(E,h,\rho,z):=\frac{\sqrt{X_{K}}}{\rho}\sqrt{1+\overset{\circ}{X}}\left(-1+\frac{1+\overset{\circ}{X}}{(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma})^{2}}\frac{X_{K}}{\rho^{2}}E^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Then,
\tilde{L}(E,\cdot,\cdot)
is well-defined and it is continuously Fréchet differentiable on
B_{\delta_{0}}
.

Proof.

Let
E\in\mathbb{R}
.

1.

First, we introduce the mapping
g_{E}:]-\delta_{0},\delta_{0}[^{2}\times\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}

g_{E}(x,y,\rho,z):=\frac{\sqrt{X_{K}}}{\rho}\sqrt{1+x}\left(-1+\frac{1+x}{(1+y)^{2}}\frac{X_{K}}{\rho^{2}}E^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

By Proposition
4
,
\forall(x,y)\in]-\delta_{0},\delta_{0}[^{2}\,;\,g_{E}(x,y,\cdot)
is well-defined on
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}
and it is smooth. Moreover,
\forall(\rho,z)\in\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}
,
g_{E}(\cdot,\rho,z)
is smooth on
]-\delta_{0},\delta_{0}[^{2}
and we have,

\forall(\rho,z)\in\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\;,\;\nabla_{(x,y)}g_{E}(x,y,\rho,z)=\frac{\sqrt{X_{K}}}{\rho}\frac{(\nabla_{(x,y)}\left(-(1+x)+\frac{(1+x)^{2}}{(1+y)^{2}}E^{2}\right)}{2\left(-(1+x)^{2}+\frac{(1+x)^{2}}{(1+y)^{2}}E^{2}\right)}.

\forall(x,y)\in]-\delta_{0},\delta_{0}[^{2}\;,\;\nabla_{(\rho,z)}g_{E}(x,y,\rho,z)=\sqrt{1+x}\frac{\nabla_{(\rho,z)}\left(-\frac{X_{K}}{\rho^{2}}+\frac{1+x}{(1+y)^{2}}\frac{X_{K}^{2}}{\rho^{4}}E^{2}\right)}{2\left(-\frac{X_{K}}{\rho^{2}}+\frac{1+x}{(1+y)^{2}}\frac{X_{K}^{2}}{\rho^{4}}E^{2}\right)}.

2.

Let
h=(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma})\in B_{\delta_{0}}
. First, we show that
\tilde{L}(E,h,\cdot)\in\widehat{C}^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}})
. We have

\tilde{L}(E,h,\rho,z)=g_{E}(\overset{\circ}{X}(\rho,z),\overset{\circ}{\sigma}(\rho,z),\rho,z).

g_{E}(x,\cdot)
is smooth on
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}
and
\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma}\in\widehat{C}^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}})
. Thus,
\tilde{L}(E,h,\cdot)
is well-defined and it also lies in
\widehat{C}^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}})
.

3.

Now,
\tilde{L}(E,\cdot,\cdot)
is Fréchet differentiable on
B_{\delta_{0}}
with derivative
\forall h\in B_{\delta_{0}}
,
\forall\tilde{h}=(\tilde{X},\tilde{\sigma})\in\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}

D_{h}\tilde{L}(E,h,\cdot)[\tilde{h}](\rho,z)=(\tilde{X}\;\;\;\tilde{\sigma})\nabla_{(x,y)}g_{E}(\overset{\circ}{X}(\rho,z),\overset{\circ}{\sigma}(\rho,z),\rho,z).

(9.33)

Moreover , by smoothness of
g_{E}
with respect to
(x,y)
, there exists
C=C(\delta_{0},E)
such that
\forall\overline{h}\in\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}\times\mathcal{L}_{X}

\left|\left|D_{h}\tilde{L}(E,h,\cdot)[\overline{h}]\right|\right|_{\widehat{C}^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}})}\leq C(\delta_{0})||h||.

The latter follows by similar arguments in the proof of Lemma
60

∎

Lemma 62
.

Let
s\in\mathbb{R}
and assume that

•

\Phi_{\pm}
is compactly supported on
\mathcal{B}_{bound}^{\pm}
,

•

\forall\xi\in\mathbb{R}\;,\;\ell_{z}\to\Phi_{\pm}(\xi,\ell_{z})
lies in
C^{2,\alpha}(\mathbb{R})
,

•

\forall h=(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma})\in\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\sigma},\forall\xi\in\mathbb{R}\;,\;(\rho,\ell_{z})\to\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(\xi,\ell_{z}),h)
lies in
C^{2,\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
.

Then,
\forall i\in\left\{1,2\right\}
,

\displaystyle f^{i,\pm}_{\Phi,\eta}(s,\cdot,\cdot):

\displaystyle B_{\delta_{0}}\to\hat{C}^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})

(9.34)

\displaystyle h=(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma})\mapsto f^{i,\pm}_{\Phi,\eta}(s,h,\cdot)

and
\forall j\in\left\{3,4\right\}
,

\displaystyle f^{j,\pm}_{\Phi,\eta}(s,\cdot,\cdot):

\displaystyle B_{\delta_{0}}\to\hat{C}^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}})

(9.35)

\displaystyle h=(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma})\mapsto f^{j,\pm}_{\Phi,\eta}(s,h,\cdot)

are well-defined and are continuously Fréchet differentiable mappings on
B_{\delta_{0}}
with Fréchet differentials:
\forall h=(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma})\in B_{\delta_{0}}\;,\;\forall\tilde{h}=(\tilde{\Theta},\tilde{X},\tilde{\sigma})\in\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}
we have

\displaystyle Df^{i,\pm}_{\phi_{\pm},\eta}(s,h)[\tilde{h}](\rho,z)=-DE_{s}(h)[\tilde{h}](\rho,z)P^{i}(h,s,\xi)(\rho,z)+\int^{\varepsilon_{2}^{\pm}}_{E_{\rho s}(h,\rho,z)}\,D_{h}P^{i}(h,s,\xi)[\tilde{h}](\rho,z)\,d\xi

(9.36)

where
\displaystyle P^{i}:B_{\delta_{0}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\to\hat{C}^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})
and
\displaystyle P^{j}:B_{\delta_{0}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\to\hat{C}^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}})
are the continuously Fréchet différentiable mappings defined by

P^{1}(h,s,\xi)(\rho,z):=(X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})+2(\rho s)^{2})\phi_{\pm}(\xi,\rho s)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(\xi,\rho s),h),

(9.37)

P^{2}(h,s,\xi)(\rho,z):=\rho s(\xi-\rho\left(-\overset{\circ}{\Theta}+\omega_{K}\right)s)\phi_{\pm}(\xi,\rho s)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(\xi,\rho s),h),

(9.38)

P^{3}(h,s,\xi)(\rho,z):=\left(\tilde{L}^{2}\left(\xi-\rho\left(-\overset{\circ}{\Theta}+\omega_{K}\right)s,X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X}),\sigma,\rho,z\right)-s^{2}\right)\phi_{\pm}(\xi,\rho s)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(\xi,\rho s),h)

(9.39)

where
\tilde{L}
is defined by (
9.10
) and

\displaystyle P^{4}(h,s,\xi)(\rho,z)

\displaystyle:=\left(\frac{X_{K}^{2}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})^{2}}{\rho^{4}\left(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma}\right)^{2}}\left(\xi-\rho\left(-\overset{\circ}{\Theta}+\omega_{K}\right)s\right)^{2}+\left(1-\frac{X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})}{\rho^{2}}\right)\left(1+\frac{\rho^{2}}{X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})}s^{2}\right)\right)

(9.40)

\displaystyle\phi_{\pm}(\xi,\rho s)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(\xi,\rho s),h),

with derivatives:

\displaystyle D_{h}P^{1}(h,s,\xi)[\tilde{h}](\rho,z)

\displaystyle=-D_{h}\rho_{1}(h,\xi,\rho s)[\tilde{h}]\mbox{\Large$\chi$}_{\eta}^{\prime}(\rho-\rho_{1}(h,\xi,\rho s))(X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})+2(\rho s)^{2})\phi_{\pm}(\xi,\rho s)

(9.41)

\displaystyle+X_{K}\tilde{X}\phi_{\pm}(\xi,\rho s)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(\xi,\rho s),h),

\displaystyle D_{h}P^{2}(h,s,\xi)[\tilde{h}](\rho,z)

\displaystyle=-\rho sD_{h}\rho_{1}(h,\xi,\rho s)[\tilde{h}]\mbox{\Large$\chi$}_{\eta}^{\prime}(\rho-\rho_{1}(h,\xi,\rho s))(\xi-\rho\left(-\overset{\circ}{\Theta}+\omega_{K}\right)s)\phi_{\pm}(\xi,\rho s)

(9.42)

\displaystyle+\rho^{2}s\tilde{\Theta}\phi_{\pm}(\xi,\rho s)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(\xi,\rho s),h),

\displaystyle D_{h}P^{3}(h,s,\xi)[\tilde{h}](\rho,z)=

(9.43)

\displaystyle-D_{h}\rho_{1}(h,\xi,\rho s)[\tilde{h}]\mbox{\Large$\chi$}_{\eta}^{\prime}(\rho-\rho_{1}(h,\xi,\rho s))\left(\tilde{L}^{2}\left(\xi-\rho\left(-\overset{\circ}{\Theta}+\omega_{K}\right)s,X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X}),\sigma,\rho,z\right)-s^{2}\right)\phi_{\pm}(\xi,\rho s)

\displaystyle+2\tilde{L}\left(\xi-\rho\left(-\overset{\circ}{\Theta}+\omega_{K}\right)s,X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X}),\sigma,\rho,z\right)\phi_{\pm}(\xi,\rho s)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(\xi,\rho s),h)

\displaystyle\left(\rho s\tilde{\Theta}\frac{\partial\tilde{L}}{\partial E}\left(\xi-\rho\left(-\overset{\circ}{\Theta}+\omega_{K}\right)s,X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X}),\sigma,\rho,z\right)+X_{K}\tilde{X}\frac{\partial\tilde{L}}{\partial X}\left(\xi-\rho\left(-\overset{\circ}{\Theta}+\omega_{K}\right)s,X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X}),\sigma,\rho,z\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\rho\tilde{\sigma}\frac{\partial\tilde{L}}{\partial\sigma}\left(\xi-\rho\left(-\overset{\circ}{\Theta}+\omega_{K}\right)s,X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X}),\sigma,\rho,z\right)\right)

and

\displaystyle D_{h}P^{4}(h,s,\xi)[\tilde{h}](\rho,z)=-D_{h}\rho_{1}(h,\xi,\rho s)[\tilde{h}]\mbox{\Large$\chi$}_{\eta}^{\prime}(\rho-\rho_{1}(h,\xi,\rho s))

(9.44)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{X_{K}^{2}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})^{2}}{\rho^{4}\left(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma}\right)^{2}}\left(\xi-\rho\left(-\overset{\circ}{\Theta}+\omega_{K}\right)s\right)^{2}+\left(1-\frac{X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})}{\rho^{2}}\right)\left(1+\frac{\rho^{2}}{X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})}s^{2}\right)\right)\phi_{\pm}(\xi,\rho s)

\displaystyle+\phi_{\pm}(\xi,\rho s)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(\xi,\rho s),h)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{X_{K}^{2}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})^{2}}{\rho^{3}\left(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma}\right)^{2}}s\tilde{\Theta}\left(\xi-\rho\left(-\overset{\circ}{\Theta}+\omega_{K}\right)s\right)-2\tilde{\sigma}\left(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma}\right)\frac{X_{K}^{2}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})^{2}}{\rho^{4}\left(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma}\right)^{4}}\left(\xi-\rho\left(-\overset{\circ}{\Theta}+\omega_{K}\right)s\right)^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+2\frac{X_{K}^{2}\tilde{X}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})}{\rho^{4}\left(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma}\right)^{2}}\left(\xi-\rho\left(-\overset{\circ}{\Theta}+\omega_{K}\right)s\right)^{2}-\frac{X_{K}\tilde{X}}{\rho^{2}}\left(1+\frac{\rho^{2}}{X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})}s^{2}\right)-\frac{\tilde{X}\rho^{2}s^{2}}{X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})^{2}}\left(1-\frac{X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})}{\rho^{2}}\right)\right).

Proof.

The proof follows from Lemma
60
, Lemma
61
and the dominated convergence theorem. We give details for
f^{1,\pm}_{\Phi,\eta}(s,\cdot,\cdot)
. Regularity for the remaining functions is proved in the same manner.

First of all, let
s\in\mathbb{R}
and recall the definition of
\displaystyle f^{1,\pm}_{\Phi,\eta}(s,\cdot,\cdot)
:

f_{\Phi,\eta}^{1,\pm}(s,h,\rho,z):=\int_{E_{\rho s}(h,\rho,z)}^{\varepsilon^{\pm}_{2}}P^{1}(h,s,\xi)(\rho,z)\,d\xi

where

P^{1}(h,s,\xi)(\rho,z):=(X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})+2(\rho s)^{2})\phi_{\pm}(\xi,\rho s)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(\xi,\rho s),h),

•

We recall

\Psi_{\eta}(\cdot,(\varepsilon,\ell),h):=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\mbox{\Large$\chi$}_{\eta}(\cdot-\rho_{1}(h,(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}))),\quad(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\in\mathcal{B}_{bound}\\
&0\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\notin\mathcal{A}_{bound},\end{aligned}\right.

By Lemma
48
,
\forall(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\in\mathcal{B}_{bound}\,,\,\rho_{1}(\cdot,(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}))
is continuously Fréchet differentiable on
B_{\delta_{0}}
. By smoothness of
\mbox{\Large$\chi$}_{\eta}
,
\Psi_{\eta}
is continuously Fréchet differentiable with respect to
h
with derivative
\forall\tilde{h}\in\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}

D_{h}\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(\xi,\rho s),h)=\left\{\begin{aligned} &-D_{h}\rho_{1}(h,\xi,\rho s)[\tilde{h}]\mbox{\Large$\chi$}_{\eta}^{\prime}(\rho-\rho_{1}(h,\xi,\rho s))\quad\text{if}\;(\xi,\rho s)\in\mathcal{B}_{bound},\\
&0\quad\text{otherwise.}\end{aligned}\right.

•

Hence,
P^{1}
is Fréchet differentiable with respect to
h
with derivative given by (
9.41
).

•

Now, let
\overline{P}^{1}(h,s,\cdot)(\rho,z)
be a primitive of
P^{1}(h,s,\cdot)(\rho,z)
. We write

f_{\Phi,\eta}^{1,\pm}(s,h,\rho,z)=\overline{P}^{1}(h,s,\varepsilon_{2}^{\pm})(\rho,z)-\overline{P}^{1}(h,s,E_{\rho s}(h,\rho,z))(\rho,z).

Hence,
\forall\tilde{h}\in\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}
, we have

\displaystyle D_{h}f_{\Phi,\eta}^{1,\pm}(s,h,\rho,z)

\displaystyle=D_{h}\overline{P}^{1}(h,s,\varepsilon_{2}^{\pm})[\tilde{h}](\rho,z)-D_{h}\overline{P}^{1}(h,s,E_{\rho s}(h,\rho,z))[\tilde{h}](\rho,z)

\displaystyle-D_{h}E_{s}(h)[\tilde{h}](\rho,z)\partial_{\xi}\overline{P}^{1}(h,s,E_{\rho s}(h,\rho,z))(\rho,z)

\displaystyle=\int^{\varepsilon_{2}^{\pm}}_{E_{\rho s}(h,\rho,z)}\,D_{h}P^{1}(h,s,\xi)[\tilde{h}](\rho,z)\,d\xi-DE_{s}(h)[\tilde{h}](\rho,z)P^{1}(h,s,\xi)(\rho,z).

The first term of the latter expression was obtained by the dominated convergence theorem and the second term is obtained by the definition of
\overline{P}^{1}
.

•

It remains to show that the derivative of
f_{\Phi,\eta}^{1,\pm}
with respect to
h
is continuous. This follows using similar arguments.

∎

9.3
Introduce the bifurcation parameter
\delta

Solutions to the Einstein-Vlasov system will be obtained by perturbing the Kerr spacetime using a bifurcation parameter
\delta\geq 0
. The latter turns on in the presence of Vlasov matter supported on
\mathcal{B}_{bound}\subset\subset\mathcal{A}_{bound}
. In order to introduce the latter in the equations, we adjust the ansatz for
f
(
5.2
) in oder to make the dependence on
\delta
explicit:

f^{\delta}(t,\phi,\rho,z,\phi,p^{\rho},p^{\phi},p^{z})=\Phi(\varepsilon,\ell_{z};\delta)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(\varepsilon,\ell_{z}),(X,W,\sigma))

(9.49)

such that

\forall(\varepsilon,\ell_{z})\in\mathcal{A}_{bound}\;,\;\quad\Phi(\varepsilon,\ell_{z};0)=0,

where
\Phi:\mathcal{A}_{bound}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
. We will impose the regularity assumptions on
\Phi
given in Lemma
62
so that the solution operator is well defined. Assuming that
(g,f^{\delta})
solves the EV-system, we can apply Theorem (
18
) with the ansatz (
9.49
) to obtain the explicit dependence of
F_{i}
on the bifurcation parameter
\delta
:

\displaystyle F_{1}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma};\delta)(\rho,z):=-\frac{2\pi e^{2\left(\overset{\circ}{\lambda}+\lambda_{K}\right)}}{1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma}}

\displaystyle\int_{D(\rho,z)}(X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})+2(\rho L)^{2})\Phi(E+\rho\left(-\overset{\circ}{\Theta}+\omega_{K}\right)L,\rho L)

\displaystyle\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(E+\rho(-\overset{\circ}{\Theta}+\omega_{K})L,\rho L),(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma}))\,dEdL,

\displaystyle F_{2}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma};\delta)(\rho,z):=\frac{2\pi}{1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma}}X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})

\displaystyle\int_{D(\rho,z)}\rho LE\Phi(E+\rho\left(-\overset{\circ}{\Theta}+\omega_{K}\right)L,\rho L)

\displaystyle\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(E+\rho\left(-\overset{\circ}{\Theta}+\omega_{K}\right)L,\rho L),(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma}))\,dEdL,

\displaystyle F_{3}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma};\delta)(\rho,z):=\frac{\rho^{4}}{X^{2}_{K}}\frac{2\pi(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma})}{\left(1+\overset{\circ}{X}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle\int_{D(\rho,z)}\left(\tilde{L}^{2}-L^{2}\right)\Phi(E+\rho\left(-\overset{\circ}{\Theta}+\omega_{K}\right)L,\rho L)

\displaystyle\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(E+\rho\left(-\overset{\circ}{\Theta}+\omega_{K}\right)L,\rho L),(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma}))\,dEdL,

\displaystyle F_{4}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma};\delta)(\rho,z)

\displaystyle:=-\frac{4\pi e^{2\left(\overset{\circ}{\lambda}+\lambda_{K}\right)}}{1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma}}\int_{D(\rho,z)}\left(\frac{X_{K}^{2}\left(1+\overset{\circ}{X}\right)^{2}}{\rho^{4}\left(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma}\right)^{2}}E^{2}+\left(1-\frac{X_{K}}{\rho^{2}}\left(1+\overset{\circ}{X}\right)\right)\left(1+\frac{\rho^{2}}{X_{K}}\frac{1}{1+\overset{\circ}{X}}L^{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\Phi(E+\rho\left(-\overset{\circ}{\Theta}+\omega_{K}\right)L,\rho L)\Psi_{\eta}(\rho,(E+\rho\left(-\overset{\circ}{\Theta}+\omega_{K}\right),\rho L),(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma}))\,dEdL.

Finally, we note that
\forall(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma}),\forall(\rho,z)\,
,

is continuously Fréchet differentiable on
[0,\delta_{0}[
.

9.4
Solving for
\sigma

In this section, we solve for
\overset{\circ}{\sigma}
in terms of the renormalised unkonws
\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda}\right)
and
\delta
. We recall that
\overset{\circ}{\sigma}
verifies

\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}{\overset{\circ}{\sigma}}=\rho^{-1}\sigma^{-1}{X}e^{2\lambda}F_{3}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma},\delta)(\rho,z),

where
\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}
is the Laplacian corresponding to the flat metric on
\mathbb{R}^{4}
given by
g_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}=d\rho^{2}+dz^{2}+\rho^{2}d\mathbb{S}^{2}
.
To any function
f:\overline{\mathscr{B}}\to\mathbb{R}
, we associate a function
f_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}:\mathbb{R}^{4}\to\mathbb{R}
defined in the following way: let
x=(x_{i})_{i=1\dots 4}\in\mathbb{R}
and define its cylindrical coordinates
(\rho,\theta,\phi,z)\in[0,\infty[\times\mathbb{S}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}
such that

\displaystyle x_{1}

\displaystyle=\rho\sin\theta\cos\phi

\displaystyle x_{2}

\displaystyle=\rho\cos\theta\sin\phi

\displaystyle x_{3}

\displaystyle=\rho\cos\theta

\displaystyle x_{4}

\displaystyle=z.

Now define
f_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}
by

f_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}(x):=f(\rho=\sqrt{x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}^{2}},z=x_{4}).

In the following, we confound
f
with
f_{\mathbb{R}}^{4}
. We start with solving the linear problem.

9.4.1
Linear problem

We prove the following result

Proposition 25
.

Let
H_{\sigma}\in\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}
. Then, there exists a unique
\overset{\circ}{\sigma}\in\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}
which solves the equation

\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\overset{\circ}{\sigma}=H_{\sigma}.

It is given by

\overset{\circ}{\sigma}(\rho,z)=\overset{\circ}{\sigma}_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}(x)=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\,\frac{1}{|x-y|^{2}}(H_{\sigma})_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}(y)\,dy.

Moreover, there exists
C(\alpha_{0})>0
such that

||\overset{\circ}{\sigma}||_{\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}}\leq C(\alpha_{0})||H_{\sigma}||_{\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}}.

The proof is based on Theorem
10
and the following Newtonian estimates

Lemma 63
.

Let
n\geq 3
and
F:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}
satisfies
|F(x)|\leq C\langle x\rangle^{-k}
where
\displaystyle\langle x\rangle:=(1+|x|^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}
for some
k>2
and
k\neq n
. Let
u:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}
be the corresponding Newton potential:

u(x):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\;|x-y|^{2-n}F(y)\,dy.

Then, we have

|u(x)|\leq C\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left|\langle y\rangle^{k}F(y)\right|\left(\langle x\rangle^{2-n}+\langle x\rangle^{2-k}\right),

(9.50)

and

|\partial u(x)|\leq C\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left|\langle y\rangle^{k}F(y)\right|\left(\langle x\rangle^{1-n}+\langle x\rangle^{1-k}\right).

(9.51)

Proof.

1.

First of all, we have
\forall x\in\mathbb{R}

\displaystyle|u(x)|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\,|x-y|^{2-n}|F(y)|\,dy

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left|\langle y\rangle^{k}F(y)\right|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\,|x-y|^{2-n}\langle y\rangle^{-k}\,dy.

Set

A(x):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\;|x-y|^{2-n}\langle y\rangle^{-k}\,dy,

we have

\displaystyle A(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{y,|x-y|\leq\frac{\langle x\rangle}{4}}\;|x-y|^{2-n}\langle y\rangle^{-k}\,dy+\int_{y,|x-y|\geq\frac{\langle x\rangle}{4}}\;|x-y|^{2-n}\langle y\rangle^{-k}\,dy=I+II.

We estimate the first term in the right hand side.

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle:=\int_{y,|x-y|\leq\frac{\langle x\rangle}{4}}\;|x-y|^{2-n}\langle y\rangle^{-k}\,dy.

By the reversed triangular inequality, we have

|y|=|x-(x-y)|\geq||x|-|x-y||,

Thus,

\langle y\rangle^{-k}=(1+|y|^{2})^{\frac{-k}{2}}\leq(\langle x\rangle^{2}-2\langle x\rangle|x-y|+|x-y|^{2})^{\frac{-k}{2}},

We obtain,

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle\leq\int_{y,|x-y|\leq\frac{\langle x\rangle}{4}}\;|x-y|^{2-n}(\langle x\rangle^{2}-2\langle x\rangle|x-y|+|x-y|^{2})^{\frac{-k}{2}}\,dy,

\displaystyle\leq\int^{\frac{\langle x\rangle}{4}}_{0}\;\frac{r^{2-n}}{(\langle x\rangle^{2}-2\langle x\rangle r+r^{2})^{\frac{k}{2}}}r^{n-1}\,dr,

\displaystyle\leq\int^{\frac{\langle x\rangle}{4}}_{0}\;\frac{r^{2-n}}{(\langle x\rangle^{2}-2\langle x\rangle r)^{\frac{k}{2}}}r^{n-1}\,dr,

\displaystyle=\langle x\rangle^{-k}\int^{\frac{\langle x\rangle}{4}}_{0}\;\frac{r}{(1-2\langle x\rangle^{-1}r)^{\frac{k}{2}}}r^{n-1}\,dr,

\displaystyle=\langle x\rangle^{2-k}\int^{\frac{1}{4}}_{0}\;\frac{r^{\prime}}{(1-2r^{\prime})^{\frac{k}{2}}}\,dr^{\prime}.

In the last line, we changed the variable by setting
r^{\prime}=\langle x\rangle^{-1}r

Hence

I\leq C\langle x\rangle^{2-k}.

Now we estimate the second term
II
given by

II:=\int_{y,|x-y|\geq\frac{\langle x\rangle}{4}}\;|x-y|^{2-n}\langle y\rangle^{-k}\,dy.

We will deal with two cases
k\leq n
and
k\geq n
.

Suppose that
k\geq n
. Then

\displaystyle II

\displaystyle=\int_{y,|x-y|\geq\frac{\langle x\rangle}{4}}\;|x-y|^{2-n}\langle y\rangle^{-k}\,dy,

\displaystyle\leq C\langle x\rangle^{2-n}\int_{y,|x-y|\geq\frac{\langle x\rangle}{4}}\;\langle y\rangle^{-k}\,dy,

\displaystyle\leq C\langle x\rangle^{2-n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\;\langle y\rangle^{-k}\,dy,

\displaystyle\leq C\langle x\rangle^{2-n}\int^{\infty}_{0}\;\frac{r^{n-1}}{(1+r^{2})^{\frac{k}{2}}}\,dr,

When
r\to\infty
, we have

\frac{r^{n-1}}{(1+r^{2})^{\frac{k}{2}}}\sim\frac{1}{r^{k-n+1}}

Since
k>n
the integral converges. Thus,

I\leq C\langle x\rangle^{2-n}.

Now suppose that
k\leq n
. We write

II=\int_{\mathcal{D}_{1}(x)}\;|x-y|^{2-n}\langle y\rangle^{-k}\,dy+\int_{\mathcal{D}_{2}(x)}\;|x-y|^{2-n}\langle y\rangle^{-k}\,dy=II_{1}+II_{2},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{1}(x)

\displaystyle:=\left\{y,|x-y|\geq\frac{\langle x\rangle}{4}\quad\text{and}\quad|y|\leq 2\langle x\rangle\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{2}(x)

\displaystyle:=\left\{y,|x-y|\geq\frac{\langle x\rangle}{4}\quad\text{and}\quad|y|\geq 2\langle x\rangle\right\}.

Now

\displaystyle II_{2}

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathcal{D}_{1}(x)}\;|x-y|^{2-n}\langle y\rangle^{-k}\,dy,

\displaystyle=\int_{|y|\geq 2\langle x\rangle}\;|x-y|^{2-n}\langle y\rangle^{-k}\,dy,

\displaystyle\leq\int_{|y|\geq 2\langle x\rangle}\;||x|-|y||^{2-n}\langle y\rangle^{-k}\,dy,\quad\quad\text{by reversed triangle inequality},

\displaystyle=\int_{|y|\geq 2\langle x\rangle}\;(|y|-|x|)^{2-n}\langle y\rangle^{-k}\,dy,

\displaystyle\leq\int_{|y|\geq 2\langle x\rangle}\;(|y|-\langle x\rangle)^{2-n}\langle y\rangle^{-k}\,dy,

\displaystyle=\int^{\infty}_{2\langle x\rangle}\;\frac{1}{(r-\langle x\rangle)^{n-2}}\frac{1}{(1+r^{2})^{\frac{k}{2}}}r^{n-1}\,dr,

\displaystyle=\int^{\infty}_{2\langle x\rangle}\;\frac{1}{(1-\frac{\langle x\rangle}{r})^{n-2}}\frac{r}{(1+r^{2})^{\frac{k}{2}}}\,dr.

Since
r\geq 2\langle x\rangle
, we have the following estimate

\frac{1}{(1-\frac{\langle x\rangle}{r})^{n-2}}\leq 2^{n-2}.

Hence,

\displaystyle II_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\int^{\infty}_{2\langle x\rangle}\;\frac{r}{(1+r^{2})^{\frac{k}{2}}}\,dr,

\displaystyle=C\left[\frac{1}{-\frac{k}{2}+1}(1+r^{2})^{-\frac{k}{2}+1}\right],

\displaystyle\leq C\langle x\rangle^{2-k}.

Now we estimate
II_{1}
.

\displaystyle II_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{\left\{|x-y|\geq\frac{\langle x\rangle}{4}\right\}\cap\left\{|y|\leq 2\langle x\rangle\right\}}\;|x-y|^{2-n}\langle y\rangle^{-k}\,dy,

\displaystyle=\int_{B(0,2\langle x\rangle)\backslash B(x,\frac{\langle x\rangle}{4})}\;|x-y|^{2-n}\langle y\rangle^{-k}\,dy,

\displaystyle\leq C\langle x\rangle^{2-n}\int_{B(0,2\langle x\rangle)\backslash B(x,\frac{\langle x\rangle}{4})}\;\langle y\rangle^{-k}\,dy,

\displaystyle\leq C\langle x\rangle^{2-n}\int^{2\langle x\rangle}_{0}\;\frac{r^{n-1}}{(1+r^{2})^{\frac{k}{2}}}\,dr

If
k<n
then,

\displaystyle II_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C\langle x\rangle^{2-n}\int^{2\langle x\rangle}_{0}\;\frac{1}{r^{k-n+1}}\,dr,

\displaystyle\leq C\langle x\rangle^{2-n}\langle x\rangle^{n-k}=\langle x\rangle^{2-k}.

If
k=n
, then

\displaystyle II_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C\langle x\rangle^{2-n}\int^{2\langle x\rangle}_{0}\;r^{n-1}(1+r^{2})^{-\frac{n}{2}}\,dr,

\displaystyle\leq C\langle x\rangle^{2-n}\log\langle x\rangle.

2.

For the first order estimates, by the integrability condition of
F
and the regularisation property of the convolution product
u
is differentiable and we have

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{i}}u(x)

\displaystyle=(2-n)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\;\partial_{x_{i}}(|x-y|)|x-y|^{1-n}F(y)\,dy,

\displaystyle=(2-n)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\;(x_{i}-y_{i})|x-y|^{-n}F(y)\,dy.

Thus,

\displaystyle|\partial u(x)|

\displaystyle\leq(n-2)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\;|x_{i}-y_{i}||x-y|^{-n}F(y)\,dy,

\displaystyle\leq(n-2)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\;|x-y|^{1-n}F(y)\,dy,

\displaystyle\leq C\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left|\langle y\rangle^{k}F(y)\right|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\;|x-y|^{1-n}\langle y\rangle^{-k}\,dy.

We use similar arguments to the first point in order to obtain the first order estimates.

∎

Lemma 64
.

Let
n\geq 3
and
F:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}
satisfies
|F(x)|+\sup_{|x-y|\leq 1}\frac{|F(x)-F(y)|}{|x-y|^{\alpha}}\leq C\langle x\rangle^{-k}
for some
k>2
and
k\neq n
. Let
u:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}
be the corresponding Newton potential:

u(x):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\;|x-y|^{2-n}F(y)\,dy.

Then

\displaystyle|\partial^{2}u(x)|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left|\langle y\rangle^{k}F(y)\right|+\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\langle y\rangle^{k}\left(\sup_{z,|z-y|\leq 1}\frac{|F(z)-F(y)|}{|z-y|^{\alpha}}\right)\right)\right)\log(4\langle x\rangle)\left(\langle x\rangle^{-n}+\langle x\rangle^{-k}\right),

Proof.

1.

We write
25
25

25

The details of computations are given in
[
34
, Chapter 10]
.
the second weak derivatives of u ,
\partial_{ij}u
:

\partial^{2}_{x_{i}x_{j}}u(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\;\partial^{2}_{x_{i}x_{j}}G(x,y)(F(y)-F(x))\,dy,

where

G(x,y):=\frac{1}{|x-y|^{n-2}}.

2.

Note that

\forall\lambda>0\;\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}\;\frac{\partial^{2}G}{\partial_{x_{i}}\partial{x_{j}}}(0,\lambda\sigma)\,d\sigma=0.

Indeed, we compute

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}G}{\partial{x_{i}}\partial{x_{j}}}(x,y)

\displaystyle=(2-n)\partial_{x_{i}}(x_{j}-y_{j})|x-y|^{-n},

\displaystyle=(2-n)\delta_{ij}|x-y|^{-n}-n(2-n)(x_{i}-y_{i})(x_{j}-y_{j})|x-y|^{-n-2}.

Besides, observe that

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}\;\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\,d\sigma=0,\quad\text{if $i\neq j$},

and

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}\;\sigma_{i}^{2}\,d\sigma=\frac{|\mathbb{S}^{n-1}|}{n},\quad\text{if $i=j$}.

Hence

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}\;\frac{\partial^{2}G}{\partial{x_{i}}\partial{x_{j}}}(0,\lambda\sigma)\,d\sigma

\displaystyle=(n-2)\lambda^{-n}(n\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}-\delta_{ij}),

\displaystyle=0.

3.

Now we proceed as in the previous lemma and we use Holder estimates for
F
in order to control the terms when
|x-y|\leq 1
.

We have

\displaystyle\partial^{2}_{x_{i}x_{j}}u(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\;\partial^{2}_{x_{i}x_{j}}G(x,y)(F(y)-F(x))\,,

\displaystyle=\int_{y,|x-y|\leq\frac{\langle x\rangle}{4}}\;\partial^{2}_{x_{i}x_{j}}|x-y|^{2-n}(F(y)-F(x))\,dy+\int_{y,|x-y|\geq\frac{\langle x\rangle}{4}}\;\partial^{2}_{x_{i}x_{j}}|x-y|^{2-n}(F(y)-F(x))\,dy,

\displaystyle=I+II.

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle:=\int_{\left\{y,|x-y|\leq\frac{\langle x\rangle}{4}\right\}}\;\partial^{2}_{x_{i}x_{j}}|x-y|^{2-n}(F(y)-F(x))\,dy,

\displaystyle=\int_{\left\{y,|y|\leq\frac{\langle x\rangle}{4}\right\}}\;\frac{\partial^{2}G}{\partial{x_{i}}\partial{x_{j}}}(0,y)(F(y+x)-F(x))\,dy,

\displaystyle=\int_{\left\{y,|y|\leq 1\right\}}\;\frac{\partial^{2}G}{\partial{x_{i}}\partial{x_{j}}}(0,y)(F(y+x)-F(x))\,dy+\int_{\left\{y,1\leq|y|\leq\frac{\langle x\rangle}{4}\right\}}\;\frac{\partial^{2}G}{\partial{x_{i}}\partial{x_{j}}}(0,y)(F(y+x)-F(x))\,dy,

\displaystyle=I_{1}+I_{2}.

Note that the set
\left\{y,1\leq|y|\leq\frac{\langle x\rangle}{4}\right\}
is empty if
\langle x\rangle<2.

\displaystyle|I_{2}|

\displaystyle:=\left|\int_{\left\{y,1\leq|y|\leq\frac{\langle x\rangle}{4}\right\}}\;\frac{\partial^{2}G}{\partial{x_{i}}\partial{x_{j}}}(0,y)(F(y+x)-F(x))\,dy\right|,

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{\left\{y,1\leq|y|\leq\frac{\langle x\rangle}{4}\right\}}\;\frac{\partial^{2}G}{\partial{x_{i}}\partial{x_{j}}}(0,y)F(y+x)\,dy\right|,

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\left\{y,1\leq|y|\leq\frac{\langle x\rangle}{4}\right\}}\;\frac{|F(y)|}{|x-y|^{n}}\,dy,

\displaystyle\leq C\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left|\langle y\rangle^{k}F(y)\right|\int_{\left\{y,1\leq|y|\leq\frac{\langle x\rangle}{4}\right\}}\;\frac{\langle y\rangle^{-k}}{|x-y|^{n}}\,dy,

We estimate for
\langle x\rangle\geq 2
,

\displaystyle\int_{\left\{y,1\leq|y|\leq\frac{\langle x\rangle}{4}\right\}}\;\frac{\langle y\rangle^{-k}}{|x-y|^{n}}\,dy

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\left\{y,1\leq|y|\leq\frac{\langle x\rangle}{4}\right\}}\;|x-y|^{-n}(\langle x\rangle^{2}-2|x||x-y|+|x-y|^{2})^{-\frac{k}{2}}\,dy,

\displaystyle\leq\int^{\frac{\langle x\rangle}{4}}_{1}\frac{1}{r}(\langle x\rangle^{2}-2|x|r+r^{2})^{-\frac{k}{2}}\,dr,

\displaystyle\leq\langle x\rangle^{-k}\int^{\frac{1}{4}}_{\frac{1}{\langle x\rangle}}\;\frac{1}{r}(1-2\frac{|x|}{\langle x\rangle}r)^{-\frac{k}{2}}\,dr,

\displaystyle\leq\langle x\rangle^{-k}+\langle x\rangle^{-k}\log(4\langle x\rangle).

Now we estimate
I_{1}
:

\displaystyle|I_{1}|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\left\{y,|y|\leq 1\right\}}\;|y|^{-n}|F(y+x)-F(x)|\,dy,

\displaystyle=\int_{\left\{y,|y|\leq 1\right\}}\;|y|^{\alpha-n}\frac{|F(y+x)-F(x)|}{|y|^{\alpha}}\,dy,

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\langle x\rangle^{-k}\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\langle y\rangle^{k}\left(\sup_{z,|z-y|\leq 1}\frac{|F(z)-F(y)|}{|z-y|^{\alpha}}\right)\right)\right)\int_{\left\{y,|y|\leq 1\right\}}\;|y|^{\alpha-n}\,dy,

\displaystyle=C\left(\langle x\rangle^{-k}\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\langle y\rangle^{k}\left(\sup_{z,|z-y|\leq 1}\frac{|F(z)-F(y)|}{|z-y|^{\alpha}}\right)\right)\right)\int^{1}_{0}\;r^{\alpha-1}\,dr.

The integral of the last line converges. Hence,

|I_{1}|\leq C\left(\langle x\rangle^{-k}\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\langle y\rangle^{k}\left(\sup_{z,|z-y|\leq 1}\frac{|F(z)-F(y)|}{|z-y|^{\alpha}}\right)\right)\right).

Thus,

|I|\leq C\left(\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left|\langle y\rangle^{k}F(y)\right|+\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\langle y\rangle^{k}\left(\sup_{z,|z-y|\leq 1}\frac{|F(z)-F(y)|}{|z-y|^{\alpha}}\right)\right)\right)\log(4\langle x\rangle)\langle x\rangle^{-k}.

(9.52)

Now we estimate the remaining term,

\displaystyle|II|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{y,|x-y|\geq\frac{\langle x\rangle}{4}}\;\partial^{2}_{x_{i}x_{j}}|x-y|^{2-n}(F(y)-F(x))\,dy\right|,

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{y,|x-y|\geq\frac{\langle x\rangle}{4}}\;\partial^{2}_{x_{i}x_{j}}|x-y|^{2-n}F(y)\,dy\right|,

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left|\langle y\rangle^{k}F(y)\right|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\;|x-y|^{1-n}\langle y\rangle^{-k}\,dy.

Set

\displaystyle II_{1}

\displaystyle:=\int_{\left\{y,|x-y|\geq\frac{\langle x\rangle}{4}\hskip 8.19447pt\text{and}\hskip 8.19447pt|y|\leq 4\langle x\rangle\right\}}\;|x-y|^{-n}\langle y\rangle^{-k}\,dy,

\displaystyle II_{2}

\displaystyle:=\int_{\left\{y,|x-y|\geq\frac{\langle x\rangle}{4}\hskip 8.19447pt\text{and}\hskip 8.19447pt|y|\geq 4\langle x\rangle\right\}}\;|x-y|^{-n}\langle y\rangle^{-k}\,dy.

Then,

\displaystyle|II_{1}|

\displaystyle\leq C\langle x\rangle^{-n}\int_{\left\{y,|y|\leq 4\langle x\rangle\right\}}\;\langle y\rangle^{-k}\,dy,

\displaystyle\leq C\langle x\rangle^{-n}\int^{4\langle x\rangle}_{0}\;r^{n-k-1}\,dr,

\displaystyle\leq C\langle x\rangle^{-n}|B_{1}|+C\langle x\rangle{-n}\int^{4\langle x\rangle}_{1}\;r^{n-k-1}\,dr,

\displaystyle\leq C\langle x\rangle^{-n}+\left\{\begin{aligned} &\langle x\rangle^{-k}\int^{4\langle x\rangle}_{1}\;\frac{1}{r}\quad\text{if $n-k\geq 0$},\\
&\langle x\rangle^{-n}\int^{4\langle x\rangle}_{1}\;\frac{1}{r}\quad\text{else},\end{aligned}\right.

\displaystyle\leq\langle x\rangle^{-n}+\log(4\langle x\rangle)\left(\langle x\rangle^{-n}+\langle x\rangle^{-k}\right).

Now we estimate

\displaystyle|II_{2}|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\left\{y,|y|\geq 4\langle x\rangle\right\}}\;|x-y|^{-n}\langle y\rangle^{-k}\,dy,

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\left\{y,|y|\geq 4\langle x\rangle\right\}}\;||x|-|y||^{-n}\langle y\rangle^{-k}\,dy,

\displaystyle=\int^{\infty}_{4\langle x\rangle}\;(r-\langle x\rangle)^{-n}\frac{r^{n-1}}{(1+r^{2})^{\frac{k}{2}}}\,dr,

\displaystyle\leq C\int^{\infty}_{4\langle x\rangle}\;\frac{1}{r(1+r^{2})^{\frac{k}{2}}}\,dr,

\displaystyle\leq C\int^{\infty}_{4\langle x\rangle}\;\frac{1}{r^{k+1}}\,dr,

\displaystyle=C\langle x\rangle^{-k}.

Finally we obtain the second order estimate.

The Hölder part follows by using similar arguments.

∎

Now, we prove Proposition
25
.

Proof.

Let
H_{\sigma}\in\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}
. Then,

\forall x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}\;\;|H(x)|\leq\langle x\rangle^{-5}.

We set

\overset{\circ}{\sigma}:=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}|x-y|^{-2}H_{\sigma}(y)\,dy

and we apply Lemma
63
with
n=4
and
k=5
. We obtain:

||r^{2}\overset{\circ}{\sigma}||_{L^{\infty}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}\leq C||H_{\sigma}||_{\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}}

and

||r^{3}\partial\overset{\circ}{\sigma}||_{L^{\infty}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}\leq C||H_{\sigma}||_{\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}}.

Since
H_{\sigma}\in C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{4})
and
H_{\sigma}\in L^{1}\cap L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{4})
, we obtain Theorem
10
in order to obtain
\overset{\circ}{\sigma}\in C^{3,\alpha}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})

and

||\overset{\circ}{\sigma}||_{C^{3,\alpha}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}\leq C||H_{\sigma}||_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}\leq C||H_{\sigma}||_{\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}}.

Finally, we apply Lemma
64
with with
n=4
,
k=4
and
F=H_{\sigma}
in order to obtain:

||r^{4}\log^{-1}(4r)\partial^{2}f||_{L^{\infty}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}\leq C||H_{\sigma}||_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{4})}\leq C||H_{\sigma}||_{\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}}.

∎

9.4.2
Non-linear estimates

We apply Theorem
6
in order to obtain

Proposition 26
.

Let
\alpha_{0}\in(0,1)
and let
\overline{\delta}_{0}>0
. Then, there exists
0<\delta_{0}\leq\overline{\delta}_{0}
such that
\displaystyle\forall\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\delta\right)\in B_{\delta_{0}}\left(\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\times[0,\infty[\right)
there exists a unique one parameter family
\overset{\circ}{\sigma}\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\delta\right)\in B_{\delta_{0}}\left(\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}\right)
which solves (
5.49
) and which satisfies

\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{\sigma}\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\delta\right)\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}}\leq C(\alpha_{0})\left(||\overset{\circ}{X}||^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{X}}+||\overset{\circ}{\Theta}||^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}}+||\overset{\circ}{\lambda}||^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}+\delta\right)

and
\displaystyle\forall\left(\overset{\circ}{X}_{i},\overset{\circ}{\Theta}_{i},\overset{\circ}{\lambda}_{i},\delta_{i}\right)\in B_{\delta_{0}}\left(\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\times[0,\infty[\right)
,

\displaystyle\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{\sigma}\left(\overset{\circ}{X}_{1},\overset{\circ}{\Theta}_{1},\overset{\circ}{\lambda}_{1},\delta_{1}\right)-\overset{\circ}{\sigma}\left(\overset{\circ}{X}_{2},\overset{\circ}{\Theta}_{2},\overset{\circ}{\lambda}_{2},\delta_{2}\right)\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{X}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\alpha_{0})\left(\left(\left|\left|\left(\overset{\circ}{X}_{1},\overset{\circ}{\Theta}_{1},\overset{\circ}{\lambda}_{1}\right)\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}+\left|\left|\left(\overset{\circ}{X}_{2},\overset{\circ}{\Theta}_{2},\overset{\circ}{\lambda}_{2}\right)\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}\right)\left|\left|\left(\overset{\circ}{X}_{1},\overset{\circ}{\Theta}_{1},\overset{\circ}{\lambda}_{1}\right)-\left(\overset{\circ}{X}_{2},\overset{\circ}{\Theta}_{2},\overset{\circ}{\lambda}_{2}\right)\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\left|\delta_{1}-\delta_{2}\right|\right).

Before we prove the above proposition, we introduce the following notations

1.

Define
H_{\sigma}
on
\overline{\mathscr{B}}
to be the mapping

H_{\sigma}(\rho,z)=\frac{X_{K}e^{2\lambda_{K}}}{\rho^{2}}\frac{1+\overset{\circ}{X}}{1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma}}e^{2\overset{\circ}{\lambda}}F_{3}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\sigma},\delta)(\rho,z).

2.

Let
\overline{\delta}_{0}>0
. Define the nonlinear operator
N_{\sigma}
on
B_{\overline{\delta}_{0}}(\mathcal{L}_{\sigma})\times B_{\overline{\delta}_{0}}(\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\times[0,\infty[)
by

N_{\sigma}\left(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta)\right)(\rho,z):=H_{\sigma}(\rho,z).

In order to prove the above proposition, we will need the following lemma

Lemma 65
.

1.

There exists
\overline{\delta}_{0}>0
such that
\displaystyle N_{\sigma}\left(B_{\overline{\delta}_{0}}(\mathcal{L}_{\sigma})\times B_{\overline{\delta}_{0}}(\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\times[0,\infty[)\right)\subset\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}
.

2.

There exist
0<\delta_{0}\leq\overline{\delta}_{0}
and
C(\alpha_{0})>0
such that
\forall\left(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta)\right)\in B_{\overline{\delta}_{0}}(\mathcal{L}_{\sigma})\times B_{\overline{\delta}_{0}}(\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\times[0,\infty[)

||H_{\sigma}||_{\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}}\leq C(\alpha_{0})\left(||\overset{\circ}{\sigma}||^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}}+||(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda})||^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}+\delta\right).

Proof.

1.

Let
\overline{\delta}_{0}>0
and let
(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta)\in B_{\overline{\delta}_{0}}(\mathcal{L}_{\sigma})\times B_{\overline{\delta}_{0}}(\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\times[0,\infty[)
.

By Proposition
24
, we have
F_{3}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\sigma};\delta)\in\hat{C}^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})
. Moreover, it is compactly supported in
\mathscr{B}
and
K:=\supp F_{3}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\sigma};\delta)\not\subset(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}\cap\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}})
. Therefore,
1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S}=1
on
K
and we have
Hence,

\displaystyle||r^{4}H_{\sigma}||_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}

\displaystyle=||r^{4}\frac{X_{K}e^{2\lambda_{K}}}{\rho^{2}}\frac{1+\overset{\circ}{X}}{1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma}}e^{2\overset{\circ}{\lambda}}F_{3}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\sigma},\delta)||_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}

\displaystyle=\left|\left|r^{4}\frac{X_{K}e^{2\lambda_{K}}}{\rho^{2}}F_{3}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\sigma};\delta)\right|\right|_{C^{1,\alpha}(K)}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\left|r^{4}\frac{X_{K}e^{2\lambda_{K}}}{\rho^{2}}\right|\right|_{C^{1,\alpha}(K)}\,\left|\left|\frac{1+\overset{\circ}{X}}{1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma}}e^{2\overset{\circ}{\lambda}}\right|\right|_{C^{1,\alpha}(K)}\,||F_{3}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\sigma};\delta)||_{C^{1,\alpha}(K)}

\displaystyle\leq C||(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})F_{3}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\sigma};\delta)||_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq C||F_{3}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\sigma};\delta)||_{\hat{C}^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}.

2.

By Proposition
24
, we have
F_{3}
is continuously Fréchet differentiable on
B_{\overline{\delta}_{0}}
. Hence, there exists
\delta_{0}\leq\overline{\delta}_{0}
such that
\forall(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta};\delta)\in B_{\delta_{0}}

\displaystyle F_{3}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\sigma};\delta)

\displaystyle=D_{h}F_{3}(0,0,0;0;0)[(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\sigma})]+D_{\delta}F_{3}(0,0,0;0)\delta+O(||(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\sigma})||^{2}+\delta^{2})

\displaystyle=D_{\delta}F_{3}(0,0,0;0)\delta+O(||(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda})||^{2}+\delta^{2}).

Therefore,

\displaystyle||r^{4}H_{\sigma}||_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}

\displaystyle\leq C||F_{3}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\sigma};\delta)||_{\hat{C}^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}

\displaystyle\leq C(||D_{\delta}F_{3}(0,0,0;0)||\delta+||(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda})||^{2}+\delta^{2})

\displaystyle\leq C\left(||\overset{\circ}{\sigma}||^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}}+||\overset{\circ}{X}||^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{X}}+||\overset{\circ}{\Theta}||^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}}+\delta\right)

\displaystyle\leq C(\alpha_{0})\left(||\overset{\circ}{\sigma}||^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}}+||(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda})||^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}+\delta\right).

∎

Now, we prove Proposition
26
.

Proof.

We apply Theorem
6
with

L=\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\;,\;N=N_{\sigma},

\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}\;,\;\mathcal{Q}=\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\;,\;\mathcal{P}=[0,\delta_{0}[.

By Lemma
86
, the assumptions of Theorem
6
are satisfied. Therefore, we obtain the desired result.
∎

9.5
Solving for
\displaystyle\left(B_{\chi}^{(N)},B_{\chi^{\prime}}^{(S)},B_{z}^{(A)}\right)

In this section, we solve the equations

\displaystyle B_{\chi}^{(N)}(0,\chi)

\displaystyle=0,

(9.53)

\displaystyle B_{\chi}^{(N)}(s,0)

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\partial_{s}B^{(N)}_{\chi}

\displaystyle=2\xi_{N}\sigma^{-1}e^{2\lambda}F_{2}(W,X,\sigma)(\rho,z)(s^{2}+\chi^{2}),

\displaystyle B^{(N)}_{\chi}(s,\chi)

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle B_{\chi^{\prime}}^{(S)}(0,\chi^{\prime})

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle B_{\chi^{\prime}}^{(S)}(s^{\prime},0)

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\partial_{s^{\prime}}B^{(S)}_{\chi^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=2\xi_{S}\sigma^{-1}e^{2\lambda}F_{2}(W,X,\sigma)(s^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})((s^{\prime})^{2}+(\chi^{\prime})^{2}),

\displaystyle B^{(S)}_{s^{\prime}}(s^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle B_{z}^{(A)}(0,z)

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}B_{z}^{(A)}(\rho,z)

\displaystyle=2(1-\xi_{N}^{2}-\xi_{S}^{2})\sigma^{-1}e^{2\lambda}F_{2}(W,X,\sigma)(\rho,z)-(\partial_{\rho}\xi_{N})B_{z}^{(N)}+(\partial_{z}\xi_{N})B_{\rho}^{(N)}-(\partial_{\rho}\xi_{S})B_{z}^{(S)}+(\partial_{z}\xi_{S})B_{\rho}^{(S)},

\displaystyle B_{\rho}^{(A)}

\displaystyle=0,

for
\displaystyle\left(B_{\chi}^{(N)},B_{\chi^{\prime}}^{(S)},B_{z}^{(A)}\right)
in terms of the renormalised unknowns
\left((\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}),\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda}\right)
and
\delta
.

By Proposition
22
, we have

\displaystyle\xi_{N}\sigma^{-1}e^{2\lambda}F_{2}(W,X,\sigma)(\rho,z)

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\xi_{S}\sigma^{-1}e^{2\lambda}F_{2}(W,X,\sigma)(\rho,z)

\displaystyle=0.

Therefore,

B_{\chi}^{(N)}(s,\chi)=0\;,\;B_{\chi}^{(N)}(0,\chi)=0\;,\;B_{\chi^{\prime}}^{(S)}(s^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})=0\;,\;B_{\chi}^{(S)}(0,\chi^{\prime})=0,

and

B^{(N)}_{\rho}=0\;,\;B^{(N)}_{z}=0\;,\;B^{(S)}_{\rho}=0\;,\;B^{(S)}_{z}=0.

9.5.1
Linear problem

In order to solve for
B_{z}^{(A)}
, we start with solving the linear problem:

\partial_{\rho}B_{z}^{(A)}(\rho,z)=(1-\xi_{N}^{2}-\xi_{S}^{2})H^{(A)}_{B}\quad\quad\text{for}\quad(H^{(A)}_{B},0,0)\in\mathcal{N}_{B}

with boundary condition

B_{z}^{(A)}(0,z)=0.

We state the following result

Proposition 27
.

Let
\displaystyle\left((0,0,H^{(A)}_{B}\right)\in\mathcal{N}_{B}.
Then, there exists a unique solution
\left(0,0,B_{z}^{(A)}\right)\in\mathcal{L}_{B}
which solves the equation

\partial_{\rho}B_{z}^{(A)}(\rho,z)=(1-\xi_{N}^{2}-\xi_{S}^{2})H^{(A)}_{B}\quad\quad\text{and}\quad B_{z}^{(A)}(0,z)=0.

It is given by

B_{z}^{(A)}(\rho,z)=\int_{0}^{\rho}(1-\xi_{N}^{2}-\xi_{S}^{2})(\tilde{\rho},z)H^{(A)}_{B}(\tilde{\rho},z)\,d\tilde{\rho}.

Moreover, there exists
C(\alpha_{0})>0

\left|\left|\left(0,0,B_{z}^{(A)}\right)\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{B}}\leq C(\delta_{0})\left|\left|\left(0,0,H^{(A)}_{B}\right)\right|\right|_{\mathcal{N}_{B}}.

Proof.

1.

First of all, since

\left|\left|\frac{(1+\rho^{15})(1+r^{10})}{\rho^{15}}H_{B}^{(A)}\right|\right|_{\hat{C}^{1,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}})}<\infty,

B_{z}^{(A)}
is well-defined on
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}}
.

2.

As for the estimates, we will show that

\left((1-\xi_{N}^{2}-\xi_{S}^{2})\frac{(1+\rho^{10})(1+r^{10})}{\rho^{10}}B_{z}^{(A)}\right)_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\in C^{1,\alpha_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3}).

First of all, we recall the following change of variables

\displaystyle x

\displaystyle=\rho\cos\vartheta

\displaystyle y

\displaystyle=\rho\sin\vartheta.

We have

\partial_{x}=\frac{x}{\sqrt{x^{2}+y^{2}}}\partial_{\rho}\;,\;\partial_{y}=\frac{y}{\sqrt{x^{2}+y^{2}}}\partial_{\rho}.

•

if
(\rho,z)\in\tilde{\mathscr{A}}
where
\tilde{\mathscr{A}}
is a neighbourhood of the axis. We assume that
\rho<1
. Then

\frac{\rho^{15}}{1+\rho^{15}}\leq\frac{\rho^{10}}{1+\rho^{10}}

and we have

\displaystyle\left|B_{z}^{(A)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{\rho}\frac{\tilde{\rho}^{10}}{1+\tilde{\rho}^{10}}\frac{1}{1+(1+\tilde{\rho}^{2}+z^{2})^{5}}\,d\tilde{\rho}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\rho^{10}}{1+\rho^{10}}\int_{0}^{\rho}\frac{1}{1+(1+\tilde{\rho}^{2}+z^{2})^{5}}\,d\tilde{\rho}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\rho^{10}}{(1+\rho^{10})(1+r^{10})}\int_{0}^{\rho}\frac{1+(1+\rho^{2}+z^{2})^{5}}{1+(1+\tilde{\rho}^{2}+z^{2})^{5}}\,d\tilde{\rho}.

The integrand is bounded uniformly in
z
. Moreover, since
\rho\in[0,1]
, we can bound the term
\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\rho}\frac{1+(1+\rho^{2}+z^{2})^{5}}{1+(1+\tilde{\rho}^{2}+z^{2})^{5}}\,d\tilde{\rho}
uniformly in
(\rho,z)
.

•

Away from the axis, say
(\rho,z)\in[\rho_{0},\infty[\times\mathbb{R}
for some
\rho_{0}>1
. We have

1\leq\frac{\rho^{10}}{(1+\rho^{10})}

and

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\rho}\frac{\tilde{\rho}^{15}}{1+\tilde{\rho}^{15}}\frac{1}{1+(1+\tilde{\rho}^{2}+z^{2})^{5}}\,d\tilde{\rho}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{\rho}\frac{\tilde{\rho}^{8}}{1+\tilde{\rho}^{15}}\frac{4\tilde{\rho}(1+\tilde{\rho}^{2}+z^{2})^{3}}{(1+\tilde{\rho}^{2}+z^{2})^{5}}\,d\tilde{\rho}

\displaystyle\leq Cr(\rho,z)^{-10}\leq C\frac{1}{1+r^{10}}

Hence,

\displaystyle\left|B_{z}^{(A)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{\rho}\frac{\tilde{\rho}^{15}}{1+\tilde{\rho}^{15}}\frac{1}{1+(1+\tilde{\rho}^{2}+z^{2})^{5}}\,d\tilde{\rho}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\rho^{10}}{(1+\rho^{10})(1+r^{10})}.

•

Away from the axis, the
L^{\infty}
estimate and the Hölder estimate of the derivative are straightforward.

•

Near the axis, we use the above change of coordinates in order to estimate the derivatives and the Hölder part.

∎

9.5.2
Non-linear estimates

We apply Theorem
6
in order to obtain

Proposition 28
.

Let
\alpha_{0}\in(0,1)
and let
\overline{\delta}_{0}>0
. Then, there exists
0<\delta_{0}\leq\overline{\delta}_{0}
such that
\displaystyle\forall\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\delta\right)\in B_{\delta_{0}}\left(\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\times[0,\infty[\right)
there exists a unique one parameter family
\left(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},\left(0,0,B_{z}^{(A)}\right)\right)
depending on
\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\delta\right)\in B_{\delta_{0}}\left(\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}\times\mathcal{L}_{B}\right)
which solves (
5.49
) and (
9.53
) and which satisfies

\left|\left|\left(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},\left(0,0,B_{z}^{(A)}\right)\right)\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\delta\right)\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}\times\mathcal{L}_{B}}\leq C(\alpha_{0})\left(||\overset{\circ}{X}||^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{X}}+||\overset{\circ}{\Theta}||^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}}+||\overset{\circ}{\lambda}||^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}+\delta\right)

and
\displaystyle\forall\left(\overset{\circ}{X}_{i},\overset{\circ}{\Theta}_{i},\overset{\circ}{\lambda}_{i},\delta_{i}\right)\in B_{\delta_{0}}\left(\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\times[0,\infty[\right)
,

\displaystyle\left|\left|\left(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},\left(0,0,B_{z}^{(A)}\right)\right)\left(\overset{\circ}{X}_{1},\overset{\circ}{\Theta}_{1},\overset{\circ}{\lambda}_{1},\delta_{1}\right)-\left(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},\left(0,0,B_{z}^{(A)}\right)\right)\left(\overset{\circ}{X}_{2},\overset{\circ}{\Theta}_{2},\overset{\circ}{\lambda}_{2},\delta_{2}\right)\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}\times\mathcal{L}_{B}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\alpha_{0})\left(\left(\left|\left|\left(\overset{\circ}{X}_{1},\overset{\circ}{\Theta}_{1},\overset{\circ}{\lambda}_{1}\right)\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}+\left|\left|\left(\overset{\circ}{X}_{2},\overset{\circ}{\Theta}_{2},\overset{\circ}{\lambda}_{2}\right)\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}\right)\left|\left|\left(\overset{\circ}{X}_{1},\overset{\circ}{\Theta}_{1},\overset{\circ}{\lambda}_{1}\right)-\left(\overset{\circ}{X}_{2},\overset{\circ}{\Theta}_{2},\overset{\circ}{\lambda}_{2}\right)\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\left|\delta_{1}-\delta_{2}\right|\right).

Before we prove the above proposition, we introduce the following notations

1.

Define
H_{B}^{(A)}
on
\overline{\mathscr{B}}
to be the mapping

H_{B}^{(A)}(\rho,z)=2\sigma^{-1}e^{2\lambda}F_{2}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma},\delta)(\rho,z).

2.

Let
\overline{\delta}_{0}>0
be obtained by Proposition (
26
). Define the nonlinear operator
N_{B}
on
B_{\overline{\delta}_{0}}(\mathcal{L}_{B})\times B_{\overline{\delta}_{0}}\left(\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\times[0,\infty[\right)
by

N_{B}\left(\left(0,0,B_{z}^{(A)}\right),(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta)\right)(\rho,z):=\frac{2}{\rho}\left(\overset{\circ}{\sigma}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\delta)\right)^{-1}e^{2\lambda}F_{2}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\delta),\delta)(\rho,z)

where
\overset{\circ}{\sigma}
is the mapping obtained by Proposition
26
.

Lemma 66
.

1.

\displaystyle N_{B}\left(B_{\overline{\delta}_{0}}(\mathcal{L}_{B})\times B_{\overline{\delta}_{0}}(\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\times[0,\infty[)\right)\subset\mathcal{N}_{B}
.

2.

There exist
0<\delta_{0}\leq\overline{\delta}_{0}
and
C(\alpha_{0})>0
such that
\forall\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta\right)\in B_{\overline{\delta}_{0}}(\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\times[0,\infty[)

||(0,0,H^{(A)}_{B})||_{\mathcal{N}_{B}}\leq C(\alpha_{0})\left(||(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda})||^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}+\delta\right).

(9.54)

Proof.

We use the same arguments used to prove Lemma
86
: the compact support of
F_{2}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma};\delta)
and the differentiability of
F_{2}
with respect to each variable.

In order to obtain (
9.72
), we write

\displaystyle F_{2}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta);\delta)

\displaystyle=D_{h}F_{3}(0,0,0;0)[(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\sigma}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta))]+D_{\delta}F_{3}(0,0,0;0)\delta

\displaystyle+O(||(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\sigma}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta))||^{2}+\delta^{2})

\displaystyle=D_{\delta}F_{3}(0,0,0;0)\delta+O(||(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\sigma}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta))||^{2}+\delta^{2}).

By Proposition
26
, we have

\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{\sigma}\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\delta\right)\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}}\leq C(\alpha_{0})\left(||\overset{\circ}{X}||^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{X}}+||\overset{\circ}{\Theta}||^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}}+||\overset{\circ}{\lambda}||^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}+\delta\right)

Hence,

||F_{2}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta);\delta)||_{\hat{C}^{1,\alpha_{0}}}\leq C(\alpha_{0})\left(||\overset{\circ}{X}||^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{X}}+||\overset{\circ}{\Theta}||^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}}+||\overset{\circ}{\lambda}||^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}+\delta\right).

The estimate (
9.72
) follows from the latter and the compact support of
F_{2}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta);\delta)
.
∎

Now, we prove Proposition
28
.

Proof.

1.

First of all, by Proposition
26
, there exists a solution map
\overset{\circ}{\sigma}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta)
defined on
B_{\delta_{0}}
which solves (
5.49
).

2.

We apply Theorem
6
with
\displaystyle L=\partial_{\rho}\;,\;N=N_{B}\;,\;\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{L}_{B}\;,\;\mathcal{Q}=\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}
and
\displaystyle\mathcal{P}=[0,\delta_{0}[
. By the previous lemma, all the assumptions are satisfied and we obtain the desired result.

∎

9.6
Solving for
(X,Y)

9.6.1
Linear problem

The aim of this section is to solve the linear problem for
\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}\right)
:

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\overset{\circ}{X}+\frac{2\partial Y_{K}\cdot\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}}{X_{K}}-\frac{2|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}\overset{\circ}{X}+2\frac{\partial X_{K}\cdot\partial Y_{K}}{X_{K}^{2}}\overset{\circ}{Y}=H_{X},

(9.55)

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\overset{\circ}{Y}-\frac{2\partial Y_{K}\cdot\partial\overset{\circ}{X}}{X_{K}}-\frac{(|\partial X_{K}|^{2}+|\partial Y_{K}|^{2})}{X_{K}^{2}}\overset{\circ}{Y}=H_{Y}.

where
\left(X_{K},Y_{K}\right)
are the renormalised quantities for the Kerr metric and where
\displaystyle\left(H_{X},H_{Y}\right)\in\mathcal{N}_{X}\times\mathcal{N}_{Y}
.

More precisely, the remaining of this section is devoted to the proof of the following result

Proposition 29
.

Let
\displaystyle\left(H_{X},H_{Y}\right)\in\mathcal{N}_{X}\times\mathcal{N}_{Y}
. Then there exists a unique solution
\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}\right)\in\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{Y}
of (
9.55
). Moreover, there exists
C>0
independent of
\left(H_{X},H_{Y}\right)
such that

\left|\left|\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}\right)\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{Y}}\leq C\left|\left|\left(H_{X},H_{Y}\right)\right|\right|_{\mathcal{N}_{X}\times\mathcal{N}_{Y}}

9.6.1.1  Existence of weak solutions to the linear non homogeneous problem

Let
\left(H_{X},H_{Y}\right)\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
and consider the system (
9.55
). Set

\mathcal{L}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;\left(|\partial\overset{\circ}{X}+X^{-1}_{K}(\partial Y_{K})\overset{\circ}{Y}|^{2}+|\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}-X^{-1}_{K}(\partial Y_{K})\overset{\circ}{X}|^{2}+X^{-2}_{K}|\overset{\circ}{X}\partial Y_{K}-\overset{\circ}{Y}\partial X_{K}|^{2}+2\overset{\circ}{X}H_{X}+2\overset{\circ}{Y}H_{Y}\right).

(9.56)

We state the following lemmas from
[
17
]
. We recall the proofs in order to be self-contained.

Lemma 67
.

•

\mathcal{L}
is well-defined on
\displaystyle\dot{H}_{axi}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times\left(\dot{H}_{axi}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\cap L^{2}_{|\partial h|}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\right)
.

•

(
9.55
) are the Euler-Lagrange equations associated with the Lagrangian
\mathcal{L}
.

Before we prove the above result, we will need the following inequalities

Lemma 68
.

Let
f(\rho,z)\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
. Then

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;|\partial X_{K}|^{2}f^{2}\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;X^{2}_{K}|\partial f|^{2}

Proof.

By Lemma
55
, there exist
c,C>0
such that
\forall(\rho,z)\in\overline{\mathscr{B}}
, we have

ce^{h}\left|\partial h\right|\leq\left|\partial X_{K}\right|\leq Ce^{h}\left|\partial h\right|.

Therefore, it suffices to show that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;e^{2h}|\partial h|^{2}f^{2}\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;e^{2h}|\partial f|^{2}

Recall that
\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}h=0
and note that

\text{div}_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(e^{2h}f^{2}\partial h)=e^{2h}f^{2}\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}h+\nabla h\cdot\nabla(e^{2h}f^{2})=\nabla h\cdot\nabla(e^{2h}f^{2}).

Now, let
0<\epsilon<1
,
Z>0
large ,
R>0
large so that
f(\rho,z)=0\;\forall\rho\geq 0
, and consider the domain
\mathcal{U}(R,\epsilon,h)\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
defined by

{U}(R,\epsilon,Z):=\left\{(r,\phi,z):\;\epsilon<r<R\;,0<\phi<2\pi\;,\;|z|<Z\right\}.

We apply the divergence theorem to obtain

\int_{{U}(R,\epsilon,Z)}\text{div}_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(e^{2h}f^{2}\partial h)\rho d\rho d\phi dz=-2\pi\int^{Z}_{-Z}\;(e^{2h}f^{2}\partial_{\rho}h)(\epsilon,z)\,dz.

Therefore,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\text{div}_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(e^{2h}f^{2}\partial h)\rho d\rho d\phi dz=-2\pi\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\int^{\infty}_{-\infty}\;(e^{2h}f^{2}\partial_{\rho}h)(\epsilon,z)\,dz.

We have

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}h(e^{2h})(\rho,z)

\displaystyle=\left(\sqrt{\rho^{2}+(z-\gamma)^{2}}-(z-\gamma)\right)\frac{\rho}{\sqrt{\rho^{2}+(z-\gamma)^{2}}}\left(\sqrt{\rho^{2}+(z+\gamma)^{2}}+(z+\gamma)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+\left(\sqrt{\rho^{2}+(z-\gamma)^{2}}-(z-\gamma)\right)^{2}\frac{\rho}{\sqrt{\rho^{2}+(z+\gamma)^{2}}}\left(\sqrt{\rho^{2}+(z+\gamma)^{2}}+(z+\gamma)\right).

Therefore, there exists
\overline{f}(z,\gamma)>0
which is compactly supported in the z-variable such that
\rho\in[0,1]

\left|(e^{2h}f^{2}\partial h)(\rho,z)\right|\leq\overline{f}(z,\gamma).

Moreover,

\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\,\partial_{\rho}h(e^{2h})(\epsilon,z)=0.

Therefore, by the dominated convergence theorem, we obtain

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\nabla h\cdot\nabla(e^{2h}f^{2})\rho d\rho d\phi dz=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\text{div}_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(e^{2h}f^{2}\partial h)\rho d\rho d\phi dz=0.

Now, we compute:

\nabla h\cdot\nabla(e^{2h}f^{2})=2e^{2h}\left(f^{2}|\nabla h|^{2}+f\nabla h\cdot\nabla f\right).

Therefore

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,e^{2h}|\partial h|^{2}f^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,e^{2h}|f|\,|\partial f|\,|\partial h|

\displaystyle\leq q\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;e^{2h}|\partial f|^{2}+\frac{1}{4q}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;e^{2h}f^{2}|\partial h|^{2}\quad\forall q>0.

Finally, we choose
q
so that we obtain the desired ineaquality.
∎

Lemma 69
.

Let
f\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
. Then,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;\left<r\right>^{-2}f^{2}\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;|\partial f|^{2}.

where,
\left<r\right>
is given by

\left<r\right>:=(1+x^{2}+y^{2}+z^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Proof.

We apply Theorem
8
with
p=2
and
\delta
satisfying:

\displaystyle-2\delta-3=-2\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\delta=-\frac{1}{2}.

Thus,

\displaystyle||f_{r}||^{2}_{2,-\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;|\left<r\right>^{-1(x,y,z)^{t}\cdot\partial f}|^{2}\left<r\right>^{3-3},

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;|\partial f|^{2}.

Therefore,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;\left<r\right>^{-2}f^{2}\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;|\partial f|^{2}.

∎

Now, we state the following lemma

Lemma 70
.

There exists
C\geq 0
such that if

\overset{\circ}{X}\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{3}),\quad\overset{\circ}{Y}\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{3}),\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;\frac{|\partial X_{K}|^{2}}{X^{2}_{K}}\overset{\circ}{Y}^{2}<\infty,

then

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;\left(|\partial\overset{\circ}{X}|^{2}+|\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}|^{2}+|\partial h|^{2}\overset{\circ}{Y}^{2}\right)\leq C\left(\mathcal{L}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y})+2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;\left(|\overset{\circ}{X}H_{X}|+|\overset{\circ}{Y}H_{Y}|\right)\right).

(9.57)

Proof.

1.

Set

\mathcal{L}_{0}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}):=\mathcal{L}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y})-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;\left(|\overset{\circ}{X}H_{X}|+|\overset{\circ}{Y}H_{Y}|\right).

(9.58)

We have

\displaystyle\left|\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}-X^{-1}_{K}\partial X_{k}\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left|\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}-X^{-1}_{K}\partial Y_{k}\overset{\circ}{X}+X^{-1}_{K}\partial Y_{k}\overset{\circ}{X}-X^{-1}_{K}\partial X_{k}\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|^{2},

\displaystyle\leq\left|\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}-X^{-1}_{K}\partial Y_{k}\overset{\circ}{X}\right|^{2}+X^{-2}_{K}\left|\partial Y_{k}\overset{\circ}{X}-\partial X_{k}\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|^{2}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;\left|\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}-X^{-1}_{K}\partial X_{k}\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathcal{L}_{0}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}).

Now, we define
\tilde{Y}:\mathbb{R}^{3}\backslash\left\{0\right\}\to\mathbb{R}
by
\tilde{Y}:=X^{-1}_{K}\overset{\circ}{Y}
and compute

\displaystyle\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}-X^{-1}_{K}\partial X_{k}\overset{\circ}{Y}

\displaystyle=\partial X_{K}\tilde{Y}+X_{K}\partial\tilde{Y}-X^{-1}_{K}\partial X_{k}\tilde{Y},

\displaystyle=X_{K}\partial\tilde{Y}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;\left|X_{K}\partial\tilde{Y}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathcal{L}_{0}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}).

By Lemma
68
, we have

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;|\partial X_{K}|^{2}\tilde{Y}^{2}\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;\left|X_{K}\partial\tilde{Y}\right|^{2}\leq C\mathcal{L}_{0}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}),

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;|\partial h|^{2}\overset{\circ}{Y}^{2}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;X^{2}_{K}|\partial h|^{2}\tilde{Y}^{2}\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;|\partial X_{K}|^{2}\tilde{Y}^{2}\leq C\mathcal{L}_{0}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}),

and

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}X^{-2}_{K}\overset{\circ}{X}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;|\overset{\circ}{X}\partial Y_{K}|^{2}X^{-2}_{K},

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;X^{-2}_{K}|\overset{\circ}{X}\partial Y_{K}-\overset{\circ}{Y}\partial X_{K}+\overset{\circ}{Y}\partial X_{K}|^{2},

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;X^{-2}_{K}|\overset{\circ}{X}\partial Y_{K}-\overset{\circ}{Y}\partial X_{K}|^{2}++\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;\tilde{Y}^{2}|\partial X_{K}|^{2},

\displaystyle\leq C\mathcal{L}_{0}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}).

Similarly, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;|\partial\overset{\circ}{X}|^{2}\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;|\partial\overset{\circ}{X}+X^{-1}_{K}\partial Y_{K}\overset{\circ}{Y}|^{2}+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;X^{2}_{K}\overset{\circ}{Y}^{2}|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}.

Now, we show that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;X^{2}_{K}\overset{\circ}{Y}^{2}|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;\left<r\right>^{-2}\overset{\circ}{Y}^{2}.

By Lemma
54
,

\rho\frac{|\partial Y_{K}|}{X^{2}_{K}}\leq C\left<r\right>^{-4}\quad\text{on}\quad\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}}.

This implies

\rho\frac{|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}}{X^{2}_{K}}\leq C\left(\frac{X_{K}}{\rho}\right)^{2}\left<r\right>^{-8}.

Moreover,

\forall(\rho,z)\in\mathscr{B}\;:\;\frac{X_{K}}{\rho}=\rho\frac{\Pi}{\Sigma^{2}\Delta}=\rho^{2}\frac{(r(\rho,z)^{2}+a^{2})^{2}-a\rho^{2}}{(r(\rho,z)^{2}+\frac{z^{2}}{(r(\rho,z)-M)^{2}})(r(\rho,z)^{2}-2Mr(\rho,z)+a^{2})},

when
\langle r\rangle
goes to
\infty
,

\left|\frac{X_{K}}{\rho}\right|^{2}\leq C\left<r\right>^{2}

Thus,

\frac{|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}}{X^{2}_{K}}\leq\left<r\right>^{-6}\leq C\left<r\right>^{-2}.

Therefore, by Lemma
69
, we obtain
