\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;X^{2}_{K}\overset{\circ}{Y}^{2}|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;\left<r\right>^{-2}\overset{\circ}{Y}^{2}\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;|\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}|^{2}\leq C\mathcal{L}_{0}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}).

This finishes the proof.
∎

Now, we prove Lemma
67

Proof.

1.

Let
(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y})\in\dot{H}_{axi}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times\left(\dot{H}_{axi}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\cap L^{2}_{|\partial h|}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\right)
. We show that

X^{-1}_{K}(\partial Y_{K})\overset{\circ}{Y}\;,\;X^{-1}_{K}(\partial Y_{K})\overset{\circ}{X}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}).

By Lemma
54
, we have

\frac{|\partial Y_{K}|}{X_{K}}\leq Cr^{-4}\frac{X_{K}}{\rho}\quad\text{on}\quad\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}}

and

{|\underline{\partial}Y_{K}|}\leq Cs^{3}\quad\text{on}\quad\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}\quad\text{on}\quad{|\underline{\partial}Y_{K}|}\leq C(s^{\prime})^{3}\quad\text{on}\quad\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}}.

Moreover, by Proposition
4
, we have

X_{K}(\rho,z)=s^{2}X_{N}(s^{2},\chi^{2})\quad\quad X_{N}(0,\chi),X_{N}(s,0)>0.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}X^{-2}_{K}|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}\overset{\circ}{Y}^{2}

\displaystyle=2\pi\int_{\overline{\mathscr{B}}}\,X^{-2}_{K}|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}\overset{\circ}{Y}^{2}d\rho dz

\displaystyle=2\pi\int_{\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}}}\,(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})X^{-2}_{K}|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}\overset{\circ}{Y}^{2}\rho d\rho dz

\displaystyle+2\pi\int_{\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}}\xi_{N}\frac{1}{s^{4}X^{2}_{N}}|\underline{\partial}Y_{K}|^{2}\overset{\circ}{Y}^{2}{s\chi}\,dsd\chi

\displaystyle+2\pi\int_{\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}}}\xi_{S}\frac{1}{(s^{\prime})^{4}X^{2}_{S}}|\underline{\partial}Y_{K}|^{2}\overset{\circ}{Y}^{2}{s^{\prime}\chi^{\prime}}\,ds^{\prime}d\chi^{\prime}.

By the decay estimates for
Y_{K}
, the above integrals are all finite. Therefore,

X^{-1}_{K}(\partial Y_{K})\overset{\circ}{Y}\;,\;X^{-1}_{K}(\partial Y_{K})\overset{\circ}{X}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})

and
\mathcal{L}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y})
is finite.

2.

Let
(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y})\in\dot{H}^{1}_{axi}(\mathbb{R})^{3}\times\left(\dot{H}^{1}_{axi}(\mathbb{R})^{3}\cap L^{2}_{\partial h}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\right)
be minimiser for
\mathcal{L}
, let
(\phi,\psi)\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
and consider the real-valued function

L(\tau):=\mathcal{L}((\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y})+\tau(\phi,\psi)).

L
has a minimum at
\tau=0
. Therefore,

L^{\prime}(0)=0.

Hence,

\left.\frac{d}{d\tau}\right|_{\tau=0}\mathcal{L}((\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y})+\tau(\phi,\psi))=0.

Now, we compute

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d}{d\tau}\right|_{\tau=0}\mathcal{L}((\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y})+\tau(\phi,\psi))

\displaystyle=\left.\frac{d}{d\tau}\right|_{\tau=0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(|\partial(\overset{\circ}{X}+t\phi)+X^{-1}_{K}(\partial Y_{K})(\overset{\circ}{Y}+t\psi)|^{2}\right.

\displaystyle+|\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}-X^{-1}_{K}(\partial Y_{K})(\overset{\circ}{X}+t\phi)|^{2}+X^{-2}_{K}|(\overset{\circ}{X}+t\phi)\partial Y_{K}-(\overset{\circ}{Y}+t\psi)\partial X_{K}|^{2}

\displaystyle\left.+2(\overset{\circ}{X}+t\phi)H_{X}+2(\overset{\circ}{Y}+t\psi)H_{Y}\right)

\displaystyle=2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,\partial\overset{\circ}{X}\cdot\partial\phi-\frac{2\partial Y_{K}\cdot\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}}{X_{K}}\phi+\frac{2|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}\overset{\circ}{X}\phi-2\frac{\partial X_{K}\cdot\partial Y_{K}}{X_{K}^{2}}\overset{\circ}{Y}\phi+H_{X}\phi

\displaystyle+\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}\cdot\partial\psi+\frac{2\partial Y_{K}\cdot\partial\overset{\circ}{X}}{X_{K}}\psi+\frac{(|\partial X_{K}|^{2}+|\partial Y_{K}|^{2})}{X_{K}^{2}}\overset{\circ}{Y}\psi+H_{Y}\psi

Here, we applied the dominated convergence theorem in order to invert the derivative and the integral. Therefore, for
(\phi,0)
we obtain

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;\partial\overset{\circ}{X}\cdot\partial\phi-\frac{2\partial Y_{K}\cdot\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}}{X_{K}}\phi+\frac{2|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}\overset{\circ}{X}\phi-2\frac{\partial X_{K}\cdot\partial Y_{K}}{X_{K}^{2}}\overset{\circ}{Y}\phi+H_{X}\phi=0

and for
(0,\psi)
, we have

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}\cdot\partial\psi+\frac{2\partial Y_{K}\cdot\partial\overset{\circ}{X}}{X_{K}}\psi+\frac{(|\partial X_{K}|^{2}+|\partial Y_{K}|^{2})}{X_{K}^{2}}\overset{\circ}{Y}\psi+H_{Y}\psi=0.

Finally, we integrate by part

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;-\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\overset{\circ}{X}\phi-\frac{2\partial Y_{K}\cdot\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}}{X_{K}}\phi+\frac{2|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}\overset{\circ}{X}\phi-2\frac{\partial X_{K}\cdot\partial Y_{K}}{X_{K}^{2}}\overset{\circ}{Y}\phi+H_{X}\phi=0,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;-\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\overset{\circ}{Y}\psi+\frac{2\partial Y_{K}\cdot\partial\overset{\circ}{X}}{X_{K}}\psi+\frac{(|\partial X_{K}|^{2}+|\partial Y_{K}|^{2})}{X_{K}^{2}}\overset{\circ}{Y}\psi+H_{Y}\psi=0.

The latter holds for all
(\phi,\psi)\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
. Therefore, (
9.55
) are the Euler-Lagrange equations associated to
\mathcal{L}
.

∎

We apply classical variational methods in order to prove the existence and uniqueness of weak solutions to (
9.55
). More precisely, we state

Lemma 71
.

Let
H_{X},H_{Y}\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
. Then, the system (
9.55
) has a unique weak solution
(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y})\in\dot{H}^{1}_{axi}(\mathbb{R})^{3}\times\left(\dot{H}^{1}_{axi}(\mathbb{R})^{3}\cap L^{2}_{\partial h}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\right)
i.e
(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})\in\dot{H}^{1}_{axi}(\mathbb{R})^{3}\times\left(\dot{H}^{1}_{axi}(\mathbb{R})^{3}\cap L^{2}_{\partial h}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\right)
we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;\nabla\overset{\circ}{X}\cdot\nabla\phi_{1}-\frac{2\partial Y_{K}\cdot\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}}{X_{K}}\phi_{1}+\frac{2|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}\overset{\circ}{X}\phi_{1}-2\frac{\partial X_{K}\cdot\partial Y_{K}}{X_{K}^{2}}\overset{\circ}{Y}\phi_{1}+H_{X}\phi_{1}

(9.59)

\displaystyle+\nabla\overset{\circ}{Y}\cdot\nabla\phi_{2}+\frac{2\partial Y_{K}\cdot\partial\overset{\circ}{X}}{X_{K}}\phi_{2}+\frac{(|\partial X_{K}|^{2}+|\partial Y_{K}|^{2})}{X_{K}^{2}}\overset{\circ}{Y}\phi_{2}+H_{Y}\phi_{2}=0.

Finally, the solution is uniquely determined in the class
\displaystyle\dot{H}^{1}_{axi}(\mathbb{R})^{3}\times\left(\dot{H}^{1}_{axi}(\mathbb{R})^{3}\cap L^{2}_{\partial h}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\right)
.

Before we prove the above lemma, we state the following result on the properties of
\mathcal{L}

Lemma 72
.

1.

There exist
C,\tilde{C}>0
such that
\forall\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}\right)\in\mathcal{U}:=\dot{H}^{1}_{axi}(\mathbb{R})^{3}\times\left(\dot{H}^{1}_{axi}(\mathbb{R})^{3}\cap L^{2}_{\partial h}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\right)

\mathcal{L}\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}\right)\geq C\left(\left|\left|\partial\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left|\left|\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\partial h}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right)-\tilde{C}

2.

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}
is weakly lower semi-continuous on
\displaystyle\mathcal{U}
.

Proof.

1.

By Lemma
70
, there exists
C>0
such that
\forall(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y})\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
and
\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,\frac{|\partial X_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}{\overset{\circ}{Y}}^{2}<\infty
, we have

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;\left(|\partial\overset{\circ}{X}|^{2}+|\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}|^{2}+|\partial h|^{2}\overset{\circ}{Y}^{2}\right)\leq C\left(\mathcal{L}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y})+2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;\left(|\overset{\circ}{X}H_{X}|+|\overset{\circ}{Y}H_{Y}|\right)\right).

Thus,
\forall q>0
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;\left(|\partial\overset{\circ}{X}|^{2}+|\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}|^{2}+|\partial h|^{2}\overset{\circ}{Y}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\mathcal{L}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y})+2Cq||\overset{\circ}{X}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\frac{C}{2q}||H_{X}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle+2Cq||\overset{\circ}{Y}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\frac{C}{2q}||H_{X}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

By Poincaré inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;\left(|\partial\overset{\circ}{X}|^{2}+|\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}|^{2}+|\partial h|^{2}\overset{\circ}{Y}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\mathcal{L}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y})+2Cq||\partial\overset{\circ}{X}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\frac{C}{2q}||H_{X}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle+2Cq||\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\frac{C}{2q}||H_{Y}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

Finally, we choose
q>0
so that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\;\left(|\partial\overset{\circ}{X}|^{2}+|\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}|^{2}+|\partial h|^{2}\overset{\circ}{Y}^{2}\right)\leq C\left(\mathcal{L}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y})+||H_{X}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+||H_{Y}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right)

2.

Let
(f_{k},g_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence of
\mathcal{U}
which converges weakly in
\mathcal{U}
to
(f,g)
. We show that

\mathcal{L}(f,g)\leq\underline{\lim}_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}(f_{k},g_{k}).

We have,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{0}(f_{k},g_{k})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,|\partial f_{k}|^{2}+|\partial g_{k}|^{2}+\frac{|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}\left(g_{k}^{2}+2f_{k}^{2}\right)+\frac{|\partial X_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}g_{k}^{2}+2g_{k}\partial f_{k}\cdot\frac{\partial Y_{K}}{X_{K}}-2f_{k}\partial g_{k}\cdot\frac{\partial Y_{K}}{X_{K}}

\displaystyle-2f_{k}g_{k}\frac{\partial X_{K}\cdot\partial Y_{K}}{X_{K}^{2}},

where
\mathcal{L}_{0}
is defined by (
9.58
). Since
\displaystyle(f_{k},g_{k})\rightharpoonup(f,g)
in
\mathcal{U}
, there exists a subsequence of
(f_{k},g_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
which converges strongly in
L^{2}(\overline{B}_{R})
for all
R>0
. Now, by convexity of
x\to x^{2}
, we have

\int_{\overline{B}_{R}}\,|\partial f_{k}|^{2}+|\partial g_{k}|^{2}\geq 2\int_{\overline{B}_{R}}\partial f\cdot(\partial f_{k}-\partial f)+2\int_{\overline{B}_{R}}\partial g\cdot(\partial g_{k}-\partial g)+\int_{\overline{B}_{R}}\,|\partial f^{2}+|\partial g|^{2}.

Since
\displaystyle(\partial f_{k},\partial g_{k})\rightharpoonup(\partial f,\partial g)
in
L^{2}(\overline{B}_{R})
, we obtain

\lim_{k\to\infty}\,2\int_{\overline{B}_{R}}\partial f\cdot(\partial f_{k}-\partial f)+2\int_{\overline{B}_{R}}\partial g\cdot(\partial g_{k}-\partial g)=0.

Therefore,

\underline{\lim}_{k\to\infty}\int_{\overline{B}_{R}}\,|\partial f_{k}|^{2}+|\partial g_{k}|^{2}\geq\int_{\overline{B}_{R}}\,|\partial f^{2}+|\partial g|^{2}.

Moreover,

\int_{\overline{B}_{R}}\,\frac{|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}g_{k}^{2}\geq\int_{\overline{B}_{R}}\,\frac{|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}g_{k}(g_{k}-g)+\int_{\overline{B}_{R}}\,\frac{|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}g^{2}.

Since
(g_{k})_{k}
converges strongly in
L^{2}(\overline{B}_{R})
, we obtain

\lim_{k\to\infty}\,\int_{\overline{B}_{R}}\,\frac{|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}g(g_{k}-g)=0.

We have,

\int_{\overline{B}_{R}}\,\frac{|\partial X_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}g_{k}^{2}\geq\int_{\overline{B}_{R}}\,\frac{|\partial X_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}g(g_{k}-g)+\int_{\overline{B}_{R}}\,\frac{|\partial X_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}g^{2}.

Moreover,
\displaystyle\partial hg_{k}\rightharpoonup\partial hg
in
L^{2}(\overline{B}_{R})
and
g\partial h\in L^{2}(\overline{B}_{R})
. Hence,

\underline{\lim}_{k\to\infty}\int_{\overline{B}_{R}}\,\frac{|\partial X_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}g_{k}^{2}\geq\int_{\overline{B}_{R}}\,\frac{|\partial X_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}g^{2}.

The remaining terms are tackled in the same manner. Therefore,

\displaystyle l:=\underline{\lim}_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}_{0}(f_{k},g_{k})

\displaystyle\geq\int_{\overline{B}_{R}}\,|\partial f|^{2}+|\partial g|^{2}+\frac{|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}\left(g^{2}+2f^{2}\right)+\frac{|\partial X_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}g^{2}+2g\partial f\cdot\frac{\partial Y_{K}}{X_{K}}-2f\partial g\cdot\frac{\partial Y_{K}}{X_{K}}

(9.60)

\displaystyle-2fg\frac{\partial X_{K}\cdot\partial Y_{K}}{X_{K}^{2}},\quad\quad\forall R>0.

Now, we have,

\displaystyle\int_{\overline{B}_{R}}\,|\partial f|^{2}+|\partial g|^{2}+\frac{|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}\left(g^{2}+2f^{2}\right)+\frac{|\partial X_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}g^{2}+2g\partial f\cdot\frac{\partial Y_{K}}{X_{K}}-2f\partial g\cdot\frac{\partial Y_{K}}{X_{K}}-2fg\frac{\partial X_{K}\cdot\partial Y_{K}}{X_{K}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathds{1}_{\left\{x\,,\,|x|\leq R\right\}}\left(|\partial f|^{2}+|\partial g|^{2}+\frac{|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}\left(g^{2}+2f^{2}\right)+\frac{|\partial X_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}g^{2}+2g\partial f\cdot\frac{\partial Y_{K}}{X_{K}}-2f\partial g\cdot\frac{\partial Y_{K}}{X_{K}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-2fg\frac{\partial X_{K}\cdot\partial Y_{K}}{X_{K}^{2}}\right).

By positivity of the integrand, we can apply the monotone convergence theorem to obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{R\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathds{1}_{\left\{x\,,\,|x|\leq R\right\}}\left(|\partial f|^{2}+|\partial g|^{2}+\frac{|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}\left(g^{2}+2f^{2}\right)+\frac{|\partial X_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}g^{2}+2g\partial f\cdot\frac{\partial Y_{K}}{X_{K}}-2f\partial g\cdot\frac{\partial Y_{K}}{X_{K}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-2fg\frac{\partial X_{K}\cdot\partial Y_{K}}{X_{K}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(|\partial f|^{2}+|\partial g|^{2}+\frac{|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}\left(g^{2}+2f^{2}\right)+\frac{|\partial X_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}g^{2}+2g\partial f\cdot\frac{\partial Y_{K}}{X_{K}}-2f\partial g\cdot\frac{\partial Y_{K}}{X_{K}}-2fg\frac{\partial X_{K}\cdot\partial Y_{K}}{X_{K}^{2}}\right).

Finally, we take the limit of (
9.60
) when
R\to\infty
, we obtain the desired result.

∎

Now, we prove Lemma
71

Proof.

We begin by setting

m:=\inf_{(f,g)\in\mathcal{U}}\;\mathcal{L}(f,g).

(9.61)

1.

First, we claim that
m
exists and it is finite. Indeed, the set

\left\{\mathcal{L}(f,g)\;,\;(f,g)\in\mathcal{U}\right\}

is not empty. Moreover, by Lemma
72
,
\mathcal{L}
is bounded from below. Thus,
m
exists. The latter is finite by Lemma
67
.

2.

By the first point, there exists a minimising sequence
\displaystyle(f_{k},g_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\in\mathcal{U}^{\mathbb{N}}
such that

\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{L}(f_{k},g_{k})=m.

Therefore,
(\mathcal{L}(f_{k},g_{k}))_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded. Moreover, there exist
C,\tilde{C}>0
such that
\forall k\in\mathbb{N}
,

\left|\left|(f_{k},g_{k})\right|\right|_{\mathcal{U}}\leq C\mathcal{L}(f_{k},g_{k})+\tilde{C}.

Hence,
(f_{k},g_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded in
\mathcal{U}
and we can extract a subsequence that converges weakly in
\mathcal{U}
.

3.

By Lemma
72
,
\mathcal{L}
is weakly lower semi-continuous. Hence, after taking the limit, there exists a minimizer for the Lagrangian
\mathcal{L}
and we obtain (
9.59
)

4.

Finally, we show that the minimizer is unique. By the linearity of the problem (
9.55
), it suffices to show that if
(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y})
solves the latter with
H_{X}=0
and
H_{Y}=0
, then
\overset{\circ}{X}=0
and
\overset{\circ}{Y}=0
.

If
(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y})
solves weakly (
9.55
), then (
9.59
) holds with
(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})=(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y})
. Therefore,
\mathcal{L}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y})=0
.

By Lemma
70
, we obtain

||(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y})||_{\mathcal{U}}=0.

∎

9.6.1.2  Linear estimates

We have obtained so far:

•

\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}\right)
are smooth classical solutions in the region
\displaystyle\mathbb{R}^{3}\backslash\left\{(0,z)\;z\in\mathbb{R}\right\}.

•

\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}\right)
are compactly supported.

Moreover, the equations for
(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y})
have a singular behaviour on the the boundary because of the asymptotics for
(X_{K},Y_{K})
near the horizon and the axis:

•

the equation for
\overset{\circ}{X}
is singular only near
p_{N}
and
p_{S}
, provided
\overset{\circ}{Y}
in the right space.

•

the equation for
\overset{\circ}{Y}
is singular near
p_{N}
,
p_{S}
and
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}}
.

In order to overcome this difficulty, we introduce a change of variable on every region, work in higher dimensions in order to obtain the required estimates for the new quantities on each region, then deduce the estimates for the original unknowns.
Let
\displaystyle(H_{X},H_{Y})\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
and let
\displaystyle(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y})\in\mathcal{U}
be the unique weak solution of (
9.55
). We define
\tilde{Y}
on
\displaystyle\mathbb{R}^{3}\backslash\left\{(0,z)\;z\in\mathbb{R}\right\}
to be the function

\tilde{Y}:=X^{-1}_{K}{\overset{\circ}{Y}}.

(9.62)

Lemma 73
.

\tilde{Y}
is a weak solution of the equation

\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\tilde{Y}+2\frac{\partial X_{K}\cdot\partial\tilde{Y}}{X_{K}}-2\frac{\partial Y_{K}\cdot\partial\overset{\circ}{X}}{X^{2}_{K}}-2\frac{|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}}{X^{2}_{K}}\tilde{Y}=H_{Y}X^{-1}_{K}.

(9.63)

Proof.

We compute

\displaystyle\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}

\displaystyle=\tilde{Y}\partial X_{K}+X_{K}\partial\tilde{Y},

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\overset{\circ}{Y}

\displaystyle=\tilde{Y}\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}X_{K}+X_{K}\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\tilde{Y}+2\partial X_{K}\cdot\partial\tilde{Y}.

We plug the latter in the equation satisfied by
\overset{\circ}{Y}
in order to obtain

\tilde{Y}\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}X_{K}+X_{K}\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\tilde{Y}+2\partial X_{K}\cdot\partial\tilde{Y}-\frac{2\partial Y_{K}\cdot\partial\overset{\circ}{X}}{X_{K}}-\frac{|\partial X_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}}\tilde{Y}-\frac{|\partial X_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}}\tilde{Y}=H_{Y}.

Now, recall that the pair
(X_{K},Y_{K})
satisfies (
5.54
). Therefore, on
\mathbb{R}^{3}\backslash\left\{(0,z),\;z\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\right\}
, we have

\frac{|\partial X_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}}\tilde{Y}-\frac{|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}}\tilde{Y}+2\partial X_{K}\cdot\partial\tilde{Y}-2\frac{\partial Y_{K}\cdot\partial\overset{\circ}{X}}{X_{K}}-\frac{|\partial X_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}}\tilde{Y}+X_{K}\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\tilde{Y}-\frac{|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}}\tilde{Y}=H_{Y}.

Hence,
\tilde{Y}
satisfies (
9.63
) .∎

Lemma 74
.

1.

there exists a smooth vector field
\tilde{e}_{A}
defined on
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}
such that
\forall(\rho,z)\in\mathscr{B}:

\frac{\partial X_{K}}{X_{K}}=\frac{2}{\rho}\partial_{\rho}+\tilde{e}_{A},

2.

there exist a smooth vector field
\tilde{d}_{A}
and a smooth function
\overline{d}_{A}
defined on
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}
such that
\forall(\rho,z)\in\mathscr{B}:

\frac{\partial Y_{K}}{X^{2}_{K}}=\tilde{d}_{A}+\frac{\overline{d}_{A}}{\rho}\partial_{\rho},

3.

the function
\displaystyle\frac{\left|\partial Y_{K}\right|}{X_{K}}
extends smoothly to
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}
.

Proof.

This follows from Lemmas
54
,
55
and
56
.
∎

Therefore,
\tilde{Y}
satisfies the equation

\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\tilde{Y}+\frac{4}{\rho}\partial_{\rho}\tilde{Y}+\tilde{e}_{A}\cdot\partial\tilde{Y}-2\frac{|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}}{X^{2}_{K}}\tilde{Y}-2\tilde{d}_{A}\cdot\partial\overset{\circ}{X}-\frac{2\overline{d}_{A}}{\rho}\partial_{\rho}\overset{\circ}{X}=H_{Y}X^{-1}_{K}

(9.64)

Moreover, by Lemma
92
, we have

\forall z\in\mathbb{R}\quad\left.\frac{\partial_{\rho}\overset{\circ}{X}_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\cdot,\cdot,z)}{\rho}\right|_{(0,0)}=\left.\partial^{2}_{x}\overset{\circ}{X}_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\cdot,\cdot,z)\right|_{\left\{(x,y)=(0,0)\right\}}.

For any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{7}
, let
(\rho,\vartheta,\tilde{\vartheta},z)\in[0,\infty[\times(0,\pi)^{4}\times(0,2\pi)\times\mathbb{R}
be the coordinates defined in the following way

\displaystyle x_{1}

\displaystyle=\rho\cos\vartheta_{1}

\displaystyle x_{2}

\displaystyle=\rho\sin\vartheta_{1}\cos\vartheta_{2}

\displaystyle x_{3}

\displaystyle=\rho\sin\vartheta_{1}\sin\vartheta_{2}\cos\vartheta_{3}

\displaystyle x_{4}

\displaystyle=\rho\sin\vartheta_{1}\sin\vartheta_{2}\sin\vartheta_{3}\cos\vartheta_{4}

\displaystyle x_{5}

\displaystyle=\rho\sin\vartheta_{1}\sin\vartheta_{2}\sin\vartheta_{3}\sin\vartheta_{4}\cos\tilde{\vartheta}

\displaystyle x_{6}

\displaystyle=\rho\sin\vartheta_{1}\sin\vartheta_{2}\sin\vartheta_{3}\sin\vartheta_{4}\sin\tilde{\vartheta}

\displaystyle x_{7}

\displaystyle=z.

Hence,
\rho
is given by
\displaystyle\rho=\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{6}\,x_{i}^{2}}
. Moreover, to any function
f:\overline{\mathscr{B}}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
we associate an axisymmetric function
f_{\mathbb{R}^{7}}:{\mathbb{R}^{7}}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
by setting

f_{\mathbb{R}^{7}}(x):=f(\rho,z).

(9.65)

In particular, we associate to
\tilde{Y}
a function
\tilde{Y}_{\mathbb{R}^{7}}
defined on
\mathbb{R}^{7}
.

Now, note that

\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{7}}=\frac{4}{\rho}\partial_{\rho}+\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}.

Therefore,
\tilde{Y}_{\mathbb{R}^{7}}
satisfies the equation

\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{7}}\tilde{Y}_{\mathbb{R}^{7}}+\tilde{e}_{A}\cdot\partial\tilde{Y}-2\frac{|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}}{X^{2}_{K}}\tilde{Y}_{\mathbb{R}^{7}}-2\tilde{d}_{A}\cdot\partial\overset{\circ}{X}-\frac{2\overline{d}_{A}}{\rho}\partial_{\rho}\overset{\circ}{X}=H_{Y}X^{-1}_{K}

(9.66)

on
\displaystyle\mathbb{R}^{7}
.
Note that this identification with
\mathbb{R}^{7}
only makes sense within the set
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}}
. In this case, we denote by
\mathbb{R}_{A}^{7}
the set
(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}})\times\mathbb{S}^{5}\subset\mathbb{R}^{7}
and we introduce the following norms

||f||_{W^{k,p}(\mathbb{R}^{7}_{A})}:=\sum_{|i|\leq k}\left(\int\;\int_{\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}}}\;\left|\partial^{i}f\right|^{p}\,\rho^{5}d\rho dz\right)^{\frac{1}{p}},

||u||_{C^{k,\alpha}}(\mathbb{R}^{7}_{A}):=\sum_{|\alpha|\leq k}||D^{\alpha}u||_{C^{0}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}})}+\sum_{|\alpha|=k}[D^{\alpha}u]_{0,\alpha;\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}}}

and the semi norm

||f||_{\dot{W}^{k,p}(\mathbb{R}^{7}_{A})}:=\left(\int\;\int_{\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}}}\;\left|\partial^{k}f\right|^{p}\,\rho^{5}d\rho dz\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}.

of a function
f:\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}}\to\mathbb{R}
.

Now, we recall the coefficients behavior of the elliptic operator associated to (
9.55
) near
p_{N}
and
p_{S}

Lemma 75
.

1.

there exists a smooth vector field
\tilde{e}_{N}
defined on
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}
such that
\forall(s,\chi)\in\mathscr{B}_{N}:

\frac{\underline{\partial}X_{K}}{X_{K}}=\frac{2}{s}\underline{\partial}_{s}+\tilde{e}_{N},

2.

there exists a smooth vector field
\tilde{e}_{S}
defined on
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}}
such that
\forall(s^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})\in\mathscr{B}_{S}:

\frac{\underline{\partial}X_{K}}{X_{K}}=\frac{2}{s^{\prime}}\underline{\partial}_{s^{\prime}}+\tilde{e}_{S},

3.

the function
\displaystyle\frac{\left|\underline{\partial}Y_{K}\right|}{X_{K}}
extends smoothly to
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}
and to
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}}
.

Proof.

This follows from Lemmas
54
,
55
and
56
.
∎

Now, we consider the system (
9.55
) near
p_{N}
and
p_{S}
and we write the latter in terms of the coordinates
(s,\chi)

Lemma 76
.

(\overset{\circ}{X},\tilde{Y})
is a weak solution of the system

\displaystyle\underline{\Delta}_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\overset{\circ}{X}+2\frac{\underline{\partial}Y_{K}\cdot\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}}{X_{K}}-2\frac{|\underline{\partial}Y_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}\overset{\circ}{X}+2\frac{\underline{\partial}X_{K}\cdot\underline{\partial}Y_{K}}{X_{K}^{2}}\overset{\circ}{Y}=(s^{2}+\chi^{2})H_{X}

(9.67)

\displaystyle\underline{\Delta}_{\mathbb{R}^{8}}\tilde{Y}+\tilde{e}_{N}\cdot\underline{\partial}\tilde{Y}-2\frac{|\underline{\partial}Y_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}\tilde{Y}-2\frac{\underline{\partial}Y_{K}\cdot\underline{\partial}\overset{\circ}{X}}{X_{K}^{2}}=(s^{2}+\chi^{2})X_{K}^{-1}H_{Y},

on
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}
, where
\underline{\partial}
is the gradient with respect to
(s,\chi)
coordinates and
\tilde{e}_{N}
is given by Lemma
75
. The above equations hold on
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}}
provided that we replace
\tilde{e}_{N}
by
\tilde{e}_{S}
and we use
(s^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})
coordinates.

Proof.

The computations are straightforward.
∎

In order to prove Proposition
29
, we will prove the following estimates:

1.

First of all, we establish estimates for
\overset{\circ}{X}
away from
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}
and
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}}
using the first equation of (
9.55
).

2.

Next, we establish estimates for
\overset{\circ}{X}
on
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}
and
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}}
using the first equation of (
9.67
).

3.

We establish estimates for
\overset{\circ}{Y}
away from the region
\left\{(\rho,z)\in\overline{\mathscr{B}}\;,\;\rho=0\;;\;z\in\mathbb{R}\right\}
.

4.

In order to handle the estimates for
\overset{\circ}{Y}
near the boundary of
\overline{\mathscr{B}}
, we establish the estimates for
\tilde{Y}
near
\mathscr{A}
using equation (
9.66
). Then, we use the second equation of (
9.67
) so that we obtain estimates of
\tilde{Y}
on
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}
and
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}}
.

5.

Finally, we deduce the estimates for
\overset{\circ}{Y}
.

Lemma 77
.

There exists
C>0
such that

\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{axi}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{axi}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\cap L^{2}_{\nabla h}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq C\left(\left|\left|H_{X}\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left|\left|H_{Y}\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right).

Proof.

Since
(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y})
is a weak solution, it minimises the Lagrangian
\mathcal{L}
. Therefore,

\mathcal{L}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y})\leq\mathcal{L}(X,Y)\quad\forall(X,Y)\in\mathcal{U}.

In particular,

\mathcal{L}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y})\leq\mathcal{L}(0,0)=0.

Now, by Lemma
70
, there exists
C>0

\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{1}_{axi}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{1}_{axi}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\cap L^{2}_{\nabla h}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq C\left(\mathcal{L}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y})+2\left(\left|\left|H_{X}\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left|\left|H_{Y}\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right)\right).

Therefore, by convexity of
x\to x^{2}
, we obtain the desired estimate.
∎

Now, we write

\displaystyle\overset{\circ}{X}

\displaystyle=(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\overset{\circ}{X}+\xi_{N}\overset{\circ}{X}+\xi_{S}\overset{\circ}{X},

\displaystyle\overset{\circ}{Y}

\displaystyle=(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})(1-\xi_{A})\overset{\circ}{Y}+(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\xi_{A}\overset{\circ}{Y}+\xi_{N}\overset{\circ}{Y}+\xi_{S}\overset{\circ}{Y}.

We have

Lemma 78
.

There exists
C>0
such that

\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|_{\dot{H}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq C\left(\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})H_{X}\right|\right|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left|\left|H_{X}\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left|\left|H_{Y}\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right).

Proof.

1.

First of all, we set
\overset{\circ}{X}_{A}:=(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\overset{\circ}{X}
and we claim that
\overset{\circ}{X}_{A}
satisfies

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\overset{\circ}{X}_{A}

\displaystyle=2\partial\overset{\circ}{X}\cdot\partial(\xi_{N}+\xi_{S})+\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\xi_{N}+\xi_{S})\overset{\circ}{X}-2(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\frac{\partial Y_{K}\cdot\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}}{X_{K}}+2(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\overset{\circ}{Y}\frac{\partial X_{K}\cdot\partial Y_{K}}{X_{K}^{2}}

(9.68)

\displaystyle+2\frac{|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\overset{\circ}{X}+(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})H_{X}.

2.

We will apply Theorem
11
with
p=2
,
n=3
and
f
given by

\displaystyle f(x)

\displaystyle:=2\partial\overset{\circ}{X}\cdot\partial(\xi_{N}+\xi_{S})+\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\xi_{N}+\xi_{S})\overset{\circ}{X}-2(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\frac{\partial Y_{K}\cdot\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}}{X_{K}}+2(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\overset{\circ}{Y}\frac{\partial X_{K}\cdot\partial Y_{K}}{X_{K}^{2}}

(9.69)

\displaystyle+2\frac{|Y_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\overset{\circ}{X}+(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})H_{X}

We recall here the identification of
f
with
f_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}
. To this end, we show that
f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
.

•

First of all, we have
\partial(\xi_{N}+\xi_{S}),\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\xi_{N}+\xi_{S})
are compactly supported in
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}}
and they vanish near
p_{N}
and
p_{S}
. Denote by
\overline{Q}
the support of their identifications on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
.

•

\overset{\circ}{X}\in\dot{H}^{1}_{axi}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
. Therefore,
\displaystyle-2\partial\overset{\circ}{X}\cdot\partial(\xi_{N}+\xi_{S})\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
.

•

By Poincaré inequality, we have

\displaystyle||\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\xi_{N}+\xi_{S})\overset{\circ}{X}||_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle=||\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\xi_{N}+\xi_{S})\overset{\circ}{X}||_{L^{2}(\overline{Q})}

\displaystyle\leq C||\overset{\circ}{X}||_{L^{2}(\overline{Q})}

\displaystyle\leq C||\nabla\overset{\circ}{X}||_{L^{2}(\overline{Q})}

\displaystyle\leq C||\overset{\circ}{X}||_{\dot{H}^{1}_{axi}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

•

By Lemma
54
and Lemma
69
, we have

\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\overset{\circ}{Y}\frac{\partial X_{K}\cdot\partial Y_{K}}{X_{K}^{2}}\right|\right|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq C\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\nabla h}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

•

Moreover, by Lemma
55
and Lemma
54
, we have

\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\frac{\partial Y_{K}\cdot\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}}{X_{K}}\right|\right|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq C\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{axi}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

•

Since
H_{X}\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, the term
\displaystyle(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})H_{X}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
.

Thus,
f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
and we apply Theorem
11
to obtain

\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|_{\dot{H}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq C\left|\left|f\right|\right|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

3.

Now, we use the latter estimates to obtain

\left|\left|f\right|\right|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq C\left(\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})H_{X}\right|\right|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+||\overset{\circ}{X}||_{\dot{H}^{1}_{axi}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{axi}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\nabla h}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right).

4.

Finally, we use Lemma
77
to conclude.

∎

Lemma 79
.

There exists
C>0
such that

\left|\left|\xi_{N}\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|_{\dot{H}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4}_{N})}+\left|\left|\xi_{S}\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|_{\dot{H}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4}_{S})}\leq C\left(\left|\left|\xi_{N}(s^{2}+\chi^{2})H_{X}\right|\right|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4}_{N})}+\left|\left|H_{X}\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left|\left|H_{Y}\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right)

Proof.

We proceed as in Lemma
78
. We only mention the main steps.

1.

First of all, we multiply the first equation of (
9.67
) by
\xi_{N}
and we express
\underline{\Delta}_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\left(\xi_{N}\overset{\circ}{X}\right)
in terms of the remaining quantities:

\displaystyle\underline{\Delta}_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\left(\xi_{N}\overset{\circ}{X}\right)

\displaystyle=\underline{\Delta}_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}(\xi_{N})\overset{\circ}{X}+2\underline{\partial}(\xi_{N}+\xi_{S})\cdot\underline{\partial}\overset{\circ}{X}-2\xi_{N}\frac{\underline{\partial}Y_{K}\cdot\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}}{X_{K}}+2\xi_{N}\frac{|\underline{\partial}Y_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}\overset{\circ}{X}

\displaystyle-2\xi_{N}\frac{\underline{\partial}X_{K}\cdot\underline{\partial}Y_{K}}{X_{K}^{2}}\overset{\circ}{Y}+\xi_{N}(s^{2}+\chi^{2})H_{X}

2.

We apply Theorem
11
with
n=4
,
p=2
and
f
given by the right hand side of the above equation. To this end, we show that
\displaystyle f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4}_{N})
. We give details for the term
\displaystyle\xi_{N}\frac{|\underline{\partial}Y_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}\overset{\circ}{X}
. The other terms follow in the same manner.

We have

\left|\left|\xi_{N}\frac{|\underline{\partial}Y_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4}_{N})}=(2\pi)^{2}\int\int_{\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}}\,\xi_{N}^{2}\left(\frac{|\underline{\partial}Y_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}\overset{\circ}{X}\right)^{2}s\chi d\xi d\chi.

By Lemma
4
, Lemma
54
, we have

\forall(s,\chi)\in\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}\;:\;\frac{|\underline{\partial}Y_{K}|}{X_{K}}\leq CsX_{N}^{-1}(s^{2},\chi^{2}).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\left|\left|\xi_{N}\frac{|\underline{\partial}Y_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4}_{N})}

\displaystyle\leq 2\pi C\int\int_{\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}}\,\xi_{N}^{2}\overset{\circ}{X}^{2}s\chi d\xi d\chi

\displaystyle\leq 2\pi C\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\,\xi_{N}^{2}\overset{\circ}{X}^{2}\rho d\rho dz

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{axi}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

Here, we used Poincaré inequality to obtain the latter estimate.

3.

We use Lemma
77
to conclude.

∎

Lemma 80
.

There exists
C>0
such that

\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})(1-\xi_{A})\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|_{\dot{H}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq C\left(\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})(1-\xi_{A})H_{Y}\right|\right|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left|\left|H_{X}\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left|\left|H_{Y}\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right).

Proof.

The proof is similar to the previous lemmas. Here we apply Calderon-Zygmund theory to the second equation of (
9.55
).
∎

Lemma 81
.

There exists
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\xi_{A}\tilde{Y}\right|\right|_{\dot{H}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{7}_{A})}\leq C\left(\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\xi_{A}H_{Y}X_{K}^{-1}\right|\right|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{7}_{A})}+\left|\left|H_{X}\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left|\left|H_{Y}\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right).

Proof.

Although the proof is similar to the previous lemmas, we give details leading to
L^{2}
estimates of the different terms.

1.

We multiply (
9.66
) by
\tilde{\xi}:=(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\xi_{A}
and we write
\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{7}}\left(\tilde{\xi}\tilde{Y}\right)
in terms of the remaining quantities:

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{7}}\left(\tilde{\xi}\tilde{Y}\right)

\displaystyle=\tilde{\xi}H_{Y}X_{K}^{-1}-\left(\tilde{\xi}\tilde{e}_{A}-2\partial\tilde{\xi}\right)\cdot\tilde{Y}+\left(2\frac{|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}\tilde{\xi}+\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{7}}\tilde{\xi}\right)\tilde{Y}.

2.

In order to apply Calderon-Zygmund theory, we need to show that the function

g=\tilde{\xi}H_{Y}X_{K}^{-1}-\left(\tilde{\xi}\tilde{e}_{A}-2\partial\tilde{\xi}\right)\cdot\partial\tilde{Y}+\left(2\frac{|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}\tilde{\xi}+\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{7}}\tilde{\xi}\right)\tilde{Y}+2\tilde{\xi}\tilde{d}_{A}\partial\overset{\circ}{X}+2\overline{d}_{A}\tilde{\xi}\frac{\partial_{\rho}\overset{\circ}{X}}{\rho}

lies in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{A}^{7})
.

•

Denote by
\tilde{\mathscr{A}}
the support of
\tilde{\xi}
and recall from Lemma
56
the decay estimate for
\tilde{e}_{A}
:

\left|\tilde{e}_{A}\right|=O_{r\to+\infty}\left(\langle r\rangle^{-2}\right).

•

We have

\displaystyle\left|\left|\tilde{\xi}H_{Y}X_{K}^{-1}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{7}_{A})}

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{5}\int\int_{\tilde{\mathscr{A}}}\,\tilde{\xi}^{2}X^{-2}_{K}H_{Y}^{2}\rho^{5}d\rho dz

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{5}\int\int_{\tilde{\mathscr{A}}}\,\tilde{\xi}^{2}X^{-2}_{\mathscr{A}}(\rho^{2},z)H_{Y}\rho d\rho dz

where
X_{\mathscr{A}}
is given by Definition
4
. Moreover,
X_{\mathscr{A}}
are bounded on
\tilde{\mathscr{A}}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\left|\left|\tilde{\xi}H_{Y}X_{K}^{-1}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{7}_{A})}

\displaystyle\leq 2\pi C\int\int_{\tilde{\mathscr{A}}}\,\tilde{\xi}^{2}H^{2}_{Y}\rho d\rho dz

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\tilde{\xi}^{2}H^{2}_{Y}\rho d\rho dz

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\left|\tilde{\xi}H_{Y}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

•

We have

\displaystyle\left|\left|\left(\tilde{\xi}\tilde{e}_{A}-2\partial\tilde{\xi}\right)\cdot\partial\tilde{Y}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{7}_{A})}

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{5}\int\int_{\tilde{\mathscr{A}}}\,\left(\left(\tilde{\xi}\tilde{e}_{A}-2\partial\tilde{\xi}\right)\cdot\partial\tilde{Y}\right)^{2}\rho^{5}d\rho dz

\displaystyle\leq(2\pi)C\int\int_{\tilde{\mathscr{A}}}\,\left|\partial\tilde{Y}\right|^{2}\rho^{5}d\rho dz.

We have

\forall(\rho,z)\in\tilde{\mathscr{A}}\;:\>\tilde{Y}=\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}X_{\mathscr{A}}^{-1}(\rho^{2},z)\overset{\circ}{Y}.

Hence,

\partial\tilde{Y}=-\frac{1}{\rho^{3}}X_{\mathscr{A}}^{-1}(\rho^{2},z)\overset{\circ}{Y}+\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\left(\partial X_{\mathscr{A}}^{-1}(\rho^{2},z)\overset{\circ}{Y}+X_{\mathscr{A}}^{-1}(\rho^{2},z)\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}\right).

Therefore,

\forall(\rho,z)\in\tilde{A}\;:\;\left|\partial\tilde{Y}\right|^{2}\leq C\left(\frac{\overset{\circ}{Y}^{2}}{\rho^{6}}+\frac{\left|\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|^{2}}{\rho^{4}}\right)

and

\displaystyle\left|\left|\left(\tilde{\xi}\tilde{e}_{A}-2\partial\tilde{\xi}\right)\cdot\partial\tilde{Y}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{7}_{A})}

\displaystyle\leq(2\pi)C\left(\int\int_{\tilde{\mathscr{A}}}\,\frac{\overset{\circ}{Y}^{2}}{\rho^{2}}\rho d\rho dz+\int\int_{\tilde{\mathscr{A}}}\,\left|\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|^{2}\rho d\rho dz\right).

By Lemma
56
,

\forall(\rho,z)\in\tilde{\mathscr{A}}\;:\;\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\leq|\partial h|^{2}.

This yields

\displaystyle\int\int_{\tilde{\mathscr{A}}}\,\frac{\overset{\circ}{Y}^{2}}{\rho^{2}}\rho d\rho dz

\displaystyle\leq\int\int_{\tilde{\mathscr{A}}}\,{|\partial h|\overset{\circ}{Y}^{2}}\rho d\rho dz

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\nabla h}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

and the estimate

\displaystyle\left|\left|\left(\tilde{\xi}\tilde{e}_{A}-2\partial\tilde{\xi}\right)\cdot\partial\tilde{Y}\right|\right|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{7}_{A})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{axi}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\cap L^{2}_{\nabla h}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left|\left|H_{X}\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left|\left|H_{Y}\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right).

•

In order to estimate
\displaystyle\overline{d}_{A}\tilde{\xi}\frac{\partial_{\rho}\overset{\circ}{X}}{\rho}
, we write

\displaystyle\left|\left|\overline{d}_{A}\tilde{\xi}\frac{\partial_{\rho}\overset{\circ}{X}}{\rho}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{7}_{A})}

\displaystyle\leq C\int\int_{\tilde{\mathscr{A}}}\,\tilde{\xi}^{2}(\partial_{\rho}\overset{\circ}{X})^{2}\rho d\rho dz

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{1}_{axi}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

•

The other terms follow in the same way.

∎

Lemma 82
.

There exists
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\left|\left|\xi_{N}\tilde{Y}\right|\right|_{\dot{H}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{8}_{N})}\leq C\left(\left|\left|(s^{2}+\chi^{2})H_{Y}\right|\right|_{L^{2}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}})}+\left|\left|H_{X}\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left|\left|H_{Y}\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right),

\displaystyle\left|\left|\xi_{S}\tilde{Y}\right|\right|_{\dot{H}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{8}_{S})}\leq C\left(\left|\left|((s^{\prime})^{2}+(\chi^{\prime})^{2})H_{Y}\right|\right|_{L^{2}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}})}+\left|\left|H_{X}\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left|\left|H_{Y}\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right).

Proof.

The proof is similar to the previous lemmas.
∎

Now, we derive the estimates for
\overset{\circ}{Y}
.

Lemma 83
.

There exists
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\xi_{A}\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|_{\dot{H}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq C\left(\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\xi_{A}H_{Y}\right|\right|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left|\left|H_{X}\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left|\left|H_{Y}\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right),

\displaystyle\left|\left|\xi_{N}\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|_{\dot{H}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4}_{N})}\leq C\left(\left|\left|(s^{2}+\chi^{2})H_{Y}\right|\right|_{L^{2}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}})}+\left|\left|H_{X}\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left|\left|H_{Y}\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right),

\displaystyle\left|\left|\xi_{S}\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|_{\dot{H}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4}_{S})}\leq C\left(\left|\left|((s^{\prime})^{2}+(\chi^{\prime})^{2})H_{Y}\right|\right|_{L^{2}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}})}+\left|\left|H_{X}\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left|\left|H_{Y}\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right).

Proof.

1.

First of all, we claim that
\forall f\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, such that

\forall z\in\mathbb{R}\;:\;\lim_{\rho\to\infty}f(\rho,z)=0,

we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\,f^{2}\rho^{3}d\rho dz\leq C\int_{0}^{\infty}\,(\partial_{\rho}f)^{2}\rho^{5}d\rho dz

(9.70)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\,f^{2}\rho d\rho dz\leq C\int_{0}^{\infty}\,(\partial_{\rho}f)^{2}\rho^{3}d\rho dz

Indeed, we apply the second estimate of Theorem
8
to the function
\rho\to f(\rho,z)
with
n=1
,
p=2
,
\delta=-1
in order to obtain the second inequality and
\delta=-2
in order to obtain the first inequality.

2.

We estimate the term
\displaystyle\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\xi_{A}\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|_{\dot{H}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}
. The other terms follow using similar arguments. We have

\displaystyle\left|\left|\tilde{\xi}\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle=2\pi\int\int_{\tilde{\mathscr{A}}}\,\left|\partial^{2}(\tilde{\xi}\overset{\circ}{Y})\right|^{2}\rho d\rho dz

\displaystyle=2\pi\int\int_{\tilde{\mathscr{A}}}\,\left|\partial^{2}(X_{K}\tilde{\xi}\tilde{Y})\right|^{2}\rho d\rho dz

\displaystyle\leq C\int\int_{\tilde{\mathscr{A}}}\,\left(X^{2}_{K}\left|\partial^{2}(\tilde{\xi}\tilde{Y})\right|^{2}+(\tilde{\xi}\tilde{Y})^{2}\left|\partial^{2}X_{K}\right|^{2}+\left|\partial X_{K}\right|^{2}\left|\partial(\tilde{\xi}\tilde{Y})\right|^{2}\right)\rho d\rho dz

\displaystyle\leq C\int\int_{\tilde{\mathscr{A}}}\,\left(\rho^{4}\left|\partial^{2}(\tilde{\xi}\tilde{Y})\right|^{2}+(\tilde{\xi}\tilde{Y})^{2}+\rho^{2}\left|\partial(\tilde{\xi}\tilde{Y})\right|^{2}\right)\rho d\rho dz

\displaystyle\leq C\int\int_{\tilde{\mathscr{A}}}\,\left|\partial^{2}(\tilde{\xi}\tilde{Y})\right|^{2}\rho^{5}\,d\rho dz+\int\int_{\tilde{\mathscr{A}}}\,\left|\partial(\tilde{\xi}\tilde{Y})\right|^{2}\rho^{3}\,d\rho dz+\int\int_{\tilde{\mathscr{A}}}\,(\tilde{\xi}\tilde{Y})^{2}\rho d\rho dz.

Now, we use (
9.70
) to obtain

\displaystyle\int\int_{\tilde{\mathscr{A}}}\,\left|\partial(\tilde{\xi}\tilde{Y})\right|^{2}\rho^{3}\,d\rho dz\leq C\int\int_{\tilde{\mathscr{A}}}\,\left|\partial^{2}(\tilde{\xi}\tilde{Y})\right|^{2}\rho^{5}\,d\rho dz

and

\displaystyle\int\int_{\tilde{\mathscr{A}}}\,(\tilde{\xi}\tilde{Y})^{2}\rho d\rho dz\leq C\int\int_{\tilde{\mathscr{A}}}\,\left|\partial(\tilde{\xi}\tilde{Y})\right|^{2}\rho^{3}\,d\rho dz

\displaystyle\leq C\int\int_{\tilde{\mathscr{A}}}\,\left|\partial^{2}(\tilde{\xi}\tilde{Y})\right|^{2}\rho^{5}\,d\rho dz.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\left|\left|\tilde{\xi}\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\xi_{A}\tilde{Y}\right|\right|_{\dot{H}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{7}_{A})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\xi_{A}H_{Y}X_{K}^{-1}\right|\right|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{7}_{A})}+\left|\left|H_{X}\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left|\left|H_{Y}\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right).

Here, we used Lemma
81
to obtain the latter estimate. Finally, by similar arguments, we show that

\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\xi_{A}H_{Y}\right|\right|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{7}_{A})}\leq C\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\xi_{A}H_{Y}\right|\right|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

This ends the proof.

∎

Lemma 84
.

There exists
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|_{\hat{C}^{1}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}

\displaystyle+\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|_{\hat{C}^{1}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}+\left|\left|\tilde{Y}\right|\right|_{\hat{C}^{1}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left|\left|H_{X}\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left|\left|H_{Y}\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})H_{X}\right|\right|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\cap L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\right.

\displaystyle\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})(1-\xi_{A})H_{Y}\right|\right|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\cap L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\xi_{A}X^{-1}_{K}H_{Y}\right|\right|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{7}_{A})\cap L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{7}_{A})}+

\displaystyle\left|\left|(s^{2}+\chi^{2})\xi_{N}X_{K}^{-1}H_{Y}\right|\right|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{8}_{N})}+\left|\left|((s^{\prime})^{2}+(\chi^{\prime})^{2})\xi_{S}X_{K}^{-1}H_{Y}\right|\right|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{8}_{S})}+

\displaystyle\left.\left|\left|(s^{2}+\chi^{2})\xi_{N}H_{X}\right|\right|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{4}_{N})}+\left|\left|((s^{\prime})^{2}+(\chi^{\prime})^{2})\xi_{S}H_{X}\right|\right|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{4}_{S})}\right).

Proof.

We will detail the
\hat{C}^{1}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})
estimates for
\overset{\circ}{X}
.
\hat{C}^{1}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})
estimates for
\overset{\circ}{Y}
and
\tilde{Y}
follow using similar argument.

To this end, we need to show that

\xi_{N}\overset{\circ}{X},\xi_{S}\overset{\circ}{X}\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{4})\quad\text{and}\quad(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\overset{\circ}{X}\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}).

1.

By Lemma
78
,
\overset{\circ}{X}_{A}=(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\overset{\circ}{X},\partial\overset{\circ}{X}_{A}\in\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
.

2.

Now, we recall the continuous embedding

\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\subset L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{3}).

(9.71)

3.

Recall that
(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\overset{\circ}{X}
satisfies (
9.68
).

4.

Now, we claim that
\partial^{2}\overset{\circ}{X}_{A}\in L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
. To prove the latter, we use equation (

) and we apply Theorem
11
with

),

and

. We need to show that

Recall that

is given by

\displaystyle f(x)

\displaystyle=2\partial\overset{\circ}{X}\cdot\partial(\xi_{N}+\xi_{S})+\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\xi_{N}+\xi_{S})\overset{\circ}{X}-2(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\frac{\partial Y_{K}\cdot\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}}{X_{K}}+2(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\overset{\circ}{Y}\frac{\partial X_{K}\cdot\partial Y_{K}}{X_{K}^{2}}

\displaystyle+2\frac{|Y_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\overset{\circ}{X}+(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})H_{X}

•

It is easy to see that the terms
\partial\overset{\circ}{X}\cdot\partial(\xi_{N}+\xi_{S}),\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\xi_{N}+\xi_{S})\overset{\circ}{X}
and
(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})H_{X}
lie in
L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{3})

•

By the decay estimates of

, we obtain

\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\frac{\partial Y_{K}\cdot\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}}{X_{K}}\right|\right|_{L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq C\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|_{\dot{W}^{1,6}_{axi}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

•

We have

\displaystyle\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\overset{\circ}{Y}\frac{\partial X_{K}\cdot\partial Y_{K}}{X_{K}^{2}}\right|\right|_{L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\overset{\circ}{Y}\frac{\partial X_{K}}{X_{K}}\right|\right|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\left|\left|\frac{\partial Y_{K}}{X_{K}}\right|\right|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\nabla h}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

•

Moreover, by Theorem
8
with

,
p=6
and
\displaystyle\delta=\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\overset{\circ}{X}\frac{|Y_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}\right|\right|_{L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\overset{\circ}{X}\langle r\rangle^{-8}\right|\right|_{L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|_{6,\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\left|\partial\overset{\circ}{X}_{A}\right|\right|_{6,-\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=C\left|\left|\partial\overset{\circ}{X}_{A}\right|\right|_{L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

5.

Finally, we use the Sobolev embedding

in order to obtain

\overset{\circ}{X}_{A}\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}).

Furthermore,

\displaystyle\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{X}_{A}\right|\right|_{C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq C||f||_{L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|_{\dot{H}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|_{\dot{H}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\nabla h}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left|\left|H_{X}\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left|\left|H_{Y}\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})H_{X}\right|\right|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\cap L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})(1-\xi_{A})H_{Y}\right|\right|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\cap L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\xi_{A}X^{-1}_{K}H_{Y}\right|\right|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{7}_{A})\cap L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{7}_{A})}\right).

6.

Using similar arguments and the previous lemmas, we estimate the

Finally, we prove Proposition
29

Proof.

We prove that there exists
C=C(\alpha_{0})>0
such that

\left|\left|(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y})\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{Y}}\leq C\left|\left|(H_{X},H_{Y})\right|\right|_{\mathcal{N}_{X}\times\mathcal{N}_{Y}}.

Recall the norms:

\displaystyle||\overset{\circ}{X}||_{\mathcal{L}_{X}}

\displaystyle=||\overset{\circ}{X}||_{\dot{W}^{1,2}_{axi}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}+||\overset{\circ}{X}||_{\hat{C}^{2,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}+||r\overset{\circ}{X}||_{L^{\infty}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}+||r^{2}\hat{\partial}\overset{\circ}{X}||_{L^{\infty}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}+||r^{3}\log^{-1}(4r)\hat{\partial}^{2}\overset{\circ}{X}||_{C^{0,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})},

\displaystyle||\overset{\circ}{Y}||_{\mathcal{L}_{Y}}

\displaystyle=||\overset{\circ}{Y}||_{\dot{W}^{1,2}_{axi}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}+|||\partial h|\overset{\circ}{Y}||_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+||\overset{\circ}{Y}||_{\hat{C}^{2,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}+||X_{K}^{-1}\overset{\circ}{Y}||_{\hat{C}^{2,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}+||r^{3}X_{K}^{-1}\overset{\circ}{Y}||_{L^{\infty}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}

\displaystyle+||r^{4}\hat{\partial}(X^{-1}_{K}\overset{\circ}{Y})||_{L^{\infty}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}+||r^{5}\log^{-1}(4r)\hat{\partial}^{2}(X^{-1}_{K}\overset{\circ}{Y})||_{C^{0,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})},

\displaystyle||H_{X}||_{\mathcal{N}_{X}}

\displaystyle=||r^{3}(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})H_{X}||_{C^{0,\alpha_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+||(\chi^{2}+s^{2})\xi_{N}H_{X}||_{C^{0,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}})}+||((\chi^{\prime})^{2}+(s^{\prime})^{2})\xi_{S}H_{Y}||_{C^{0,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}})},

\displaystyle||H_{Y}||_{\mathcal{N}_{Y}}

\displaystyle=||H_{Y}r^{5}X^{-1}_{K}||_{\hat{C}^{0,\alpha_{0}}\left(\left(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\right)\cap\left\{\rho\leq 1\right\}\right)}+||H_{Y}r^{4}||_{\hat{C}^{0,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}}\cap\left\{\rho\geq 1\right\})}+||(\chi^{2}+s^{2})\xi_{N}H_{X}||_{C^{0,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}})}

\displaystyle+||((\chi^{\prime})^{2}+(s^{\prime})^{2})\xi_{S}H_{Y}||_{C^{0,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}})}

1.

First of all, by
we have

\displaystyle\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})H_{X}\right|\right|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\cap L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq C\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})H_{X}\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\left|\left|(s^{2}+\chi^{2})\xi_{N}H_{X}\right|\right|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{4}_{N})}\leq C\left|\left|(s^{2}+\chi^{2})\xi_{N}H_{X}\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}})}

\displaystyle\left|\left|((s^{\prime})^{2}+(\chi^{\prime})^{2})\xi_{S}H_{X}\right|\right|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{4}_{S})}\leq C\left|\left|((s^{\prime})^{2}+(\chi^{\prime})^{2})\xi_{S}H_{X}\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}})}

2.

Similarly, we show that

\displaystyle\left|\left|(s^{2}+\chi^{2})\xi_{N}X_{K}^{-1}H_{Y}\right|\right|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{8}_{N})}\leq C\left|\left|(s^{2}+\chi^{2})\xi_{N}X_{K}^{-1}H_{Y}\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}})}

\displaystyle\left|\left|((s^{\prime})^{2}+(\chi^{\prime})^{2})\xi_{S}X_{K}^{-1}H_{Y}\right|\right|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{8}_{S})}\leq C\left|\left|((s^{\prime})^{2}+(\chi^{\prime})^{2})\xi_{S}X_{K}^{-1}H_{Y}\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}})}

By similar arguments, we have

\displaystyle\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})(1-\xi_{A})H_{Y}\right|\right|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\cap L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\xi_{A}X^{-1}_{K}H_{Y}\right|\right|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{7}_{A})\cap L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{7}_{A})}\leq C

4.

We estimate the term

:

\displaystyle\left|\left|H_{X}\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\left|\left|H_{X}\right|\right|_{L^{\frac{6}{5}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|_{L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left|H_{X}\right|\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\frac{6}{5}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\left(\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|_{L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left|\left|\xi_{N}\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|_{L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left|\left|\xi_{S}\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|_{L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

•

\xi_{N}\overset{\circ}{X}
and
\xi_{S}\overset{\circ}{X}
are compactly supported on

and

),

•

Thus,
\forall q>0
, we have

\displaystyle\left.+q\left|\left|\xi_{S}\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(q^{-1}\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})H_{X}\right|\right|_{L^{\frac{6}{5}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+q\left|\left|(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|_{\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+q^{-1}\left|\left|\xi_{N}H_{X}\right|\right|_{L^{\frac{6}{5}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+q\left|\left|\xi_{N}\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+q^{-1}\left|\left|\xi_{S}H_{X}\right|\right|_{L^{\frac{6}{5}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+q\left|\left|\xi_{S}\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right),

•

\xi_{N}H_{X}
and
\xi_{S}H_{X}
are compactly supported on

and

respectively. Hence,

and

•

Furthermore,

\displaystyle\left|\left|\xi_{N}\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left|\left|\xi_{S}\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|_{\hat{C}^{1}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}

•

We obtain similar estimates for
H_{Y}\overset{\circ}{Y}
.

5.

We choose
q>0
so that by Lemma
84
, we obtain

\displaystyle||\overset{\circ}{X}||_{\dot{W}^{1,2}_{axi}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}+||\overset{\circ}{Y}||_{\dot{W}^{1,2}_{axi}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}+\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|_{\hat{C}^{1}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}

\displaystyle+\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|_{\hat{C}^{1}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}\leq C(\alpha_{0})\left|\left|(H_{X},H_{Y})\right|\right|_{\mathcal{N}_{X}\times\mathcal{N}_{Y}}.

Now, we estimate the
C^{2,\alpha_{0}}
part of
(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y})
in terms of

•

First, since

.

. Now, we have

(a)

if

, the second order operator
(\overset{\circ}{X},\tilde{Y})\to L(\overset{\circ}{X},\tilde{Y})
defined by

&\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{7}}\tilde{Y}_{\mathbb{R}^{7}}+\tilde{e}_{A}\cdot\partial\tilde{Y}-2\frac{|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}}{X^{2}_{K}}\tilde{Y}_{\mathbb{R}^{7}}-2\tilde{d}_{A}\cdot\partial\overset{\circ}{X}-\frac{2\overline{d}_{A}}{\rho}\partial_{\rho}\overset{\circ}{X}.\end{aligned}\right)

is uniformly elliptic on

and

(b)

The remaining estimates in

and

7.

Now, we show that

\displaystyle||r\overset{\circ}{X}||_{L^{\infty}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}+||r^{2}\hat{\partial}\overset{\circ}{X}||_{L^{\infty}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}+||r^{3}\log^{-1}(4r)\hat{\partial}^{2}\overset{\circ}{X}||_{C^{0,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}\leq C\left|\left|(H_{X},H_{Y})\right|\right|_{\mathcal{N}_{X}\times\mathcal{N}_{Y}}.

9.6.2
Non-linear estimates

We apply Theorem

in order to obtain

Proposition 30
.

Let
\alpha_{0}\in(0,1)
and let
\overline{\delta}_{0}>0
. Then, there exists
