\displaystyle\forall\left(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\delta\right)\in B_{\delta_{0}}\left(\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\times[0,\infty[\right)
there exists a unique one parameter family
\left(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},\left(0,0,B_{z}^{(A)}\right),\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}\right)\right)
depending on
\left(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\delta\right)\in B_{\delta_{0}}\left(\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}\times\mathcal{L}_{B}\times\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{Y}\right)
which solves (
5.49
), (
9.53
) and (
5.51
) and which satisfies

\left|\left|\left(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},\left(0,0,B_{z}^{(A)}\right),\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}\right)\right)\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\delta\right)\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}\times\mathcal{L}_{B}}\leq C(\alpha_{0})\left(||\overset{\circ}{\Theta}||^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}}+||\overset{\circ}{\lambda}||^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}+\delta\right)

and
\displaystyle\forall\left(\overset{\circ}{\Theta}_{i},\overset{\circ}{\lambda}_{i},\delta_{i}\right)\in B_{\delta_{0}}\left(\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\times[0,\infty[\right)
,

\displaystyle\left|\left|\left(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},\left(0,0,B_{z}^{(A)}\right),\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}\right)\right)\left(\overset{\circ}{\Theta}_{1},\overset{\circ}{\lambda}_{1},\delta_{1}\right)-\left(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},\left(0,0,B_{z}^{(A)}\right),\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}\right)\right)\left(\overset{\circ}{\Theta}_{2},\overset{\circ}{\lambda}_{2},\delta_{2}\right)\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}\times\mathcal{L}_{B}\times\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{Y}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\alpha_{0})\left(\left(\left|\left|\left(\overset{\circ}{X}_{1},\overset{\circ}{\Theta}_{1},\overset{\circ}{\lambda}_{1}\right)\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}+\left|\left|\left(\overset{\circ}{X}_{2},\overset{\circ}{\Theta}_{2},\overset{\circ}{\lambda}_{2}\right)\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}\right)\left|\left|\left(\overset{\circ}{\Theta}_{1},\overset{\circ}{\lambda}_{1}\right)-\left(\overset{\circ}{\Theta}_{2},\overset{\circ}{\lambda}_{2}\right)\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\left|\delta_{1}-\delta_{2}\right|\right).

Before we prove the above proposition, we introduce the following notations

1.

Define
H_{X}
and
H_{Y}
on
\overline{\mathscr{B}}
to be the mappings

\displaystyle H_{X}(\rho,z)

\displaystyle=N_{X}^{(1)}(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},B,(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}),\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta)+N_{X}^{(2)}(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},B,(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}),\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta),

\displaystyle H_{Y}(\rho,z)

\displaystyle=N_{Y}^{(1)}(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},B,(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}),\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta)+N_{Y}^{(2)}(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},B,(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}),\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta),

where

N^{(1)}_{X}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y})(\rho,z):=\frac{X_{K}^{2}(|\partial\overset{\circ}{X}|^{2}-|\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}|^{2})+(\overset{\circ}{X}\partial Y_{K}-\overset{\circ}{Y}\partial X_{K})\cdot(2X_{K}\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}-\overset{\circ}{X}\partial Y_{K}+\overset{\circ}{Y}\partial X_{K})}{X_{K}^{2}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})},

N^{(1)}_{Y}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y})(\rho,z):=\frac{\partial\overset{\circ}{X}\cdot\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}+2X_{K}(\overset{\circ}{Y}\partial X_{K}-\overset{\circ}{X}\partial Y_{K})\cdot\partial\overset{\circ}{X}}{X_{K}^{2}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})},

\displaystyle N^{(2)}_{X}(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},B,(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}),\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta)(\rho,z)

\displaystyle:=(\rho^{-1}-\sigma^{-1}\partial_{\rho}\sigma)\frac{\partial_{\rho}(X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X}))}{X_{K}}-\sigma^{-1}\partial_{z}\sigma\frac{\partial_{z}(X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X}))}{X_{K}}

\displaystyle-\frac{2\partial_{\rho}(Y_{K}+X_{K}\overset{\circ}{Y})B_{\rho}}{X_{K}^{2}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})}-\frac{2\partial_{z}(Y_{K}+X_{K}\overset{\circ}{Y})B_{z}}{X_{K}^{2}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})}

\displaystyle-\frac{B_{\rho}^{2}+B_{z}^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})}+X^{-1}_{K}F_{1}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma})(\rho,z),

\displaystyle N^{(2)}_{Y}(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},B,(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}),\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta)(\rho,z)

\displaystyle:=(\rho^{-1}-\sigma^{-1}\partial_{\rho}\sigma)\partial_{\rho}(Y_{K}+X_{K}\overset{\circ}{Y})X_{K}^{-1}-\sigma^{-1}\partial_{\rho}\sigma B_{\rho}X_{K}^{-1}

\displaystyle-\sigma^{-1}\partial_{z}\sigma(\partial_{z}(Y_{K}+X_{K}\overset{\circ}{Y})+B_{z})X_{K}^{-1}

\displaystyle+\frac{B_{\rho}\partial_{\rho}(X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X}))+B_{z}\partial_{z}(X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X}))}{X_{K}^{2}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})},

2.

Let
\overline{\delta}_{0}>0
be obtained by Proposition (
28
). Define the nonlinear operator
N_{(X,Y)}
on
B_{\overline{\delta}_{0}}(\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{Y})\times B_{\overline{\delta}_{0}}\left(\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\times[0,\infty[\right)
by

N_{(X,Y)}\left(\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}\right),(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta)\right)(\rho,z):=\left(\begin{aligned} &(N^{(1)}_{X}+N^{(2)}_{X})(\overset{\circ}{\sigma}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta),B(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta),(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}),\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta)\\
&(N^{(1)}_{Y}+N^{(2)}_{Y})(\overset{\circ}{\sigma}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta),B(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta),(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}),\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta)\end{aligned}\right).

where
\overset{\circ}{\sigma}
and
B
are the mappings obtained by Proposition
28
.

Now, we state the following lemma

Lemma 85
.

1.

\displaystyle N_{(X,Y)}\left(B_{\overline{\delta}_{0}}(\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{Y})\times B_{\overline{\delta}_{0}}(\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\times[0,\infty[)\right)\subset\mathcal{N}_{X}\times\mathcal{N}_{Y}
.

2.

There exist
0<\delta_{0}\leq\overline{\delta}_{0}
and
C(\alpha_{0})>0
such that
\forall\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta\right)\in B_{\overline{\delta}_{0}}(\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\times[0,\infty[)

||(H_{X},H_{Y})||_{\mathcal{N}_{X}\times\mathcal{N}_{Y}}\leq C(\alpha_{0})\left(\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{X}}+\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{Y}\right|\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{Y}}+\left|\left|(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda})\right|\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}+\delta\right).

(9.72)

Proof.

1.

We use the same arguments used to prove Lemma
86
and Lemma
85
: the compact support of
F_{1}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma};\delta)
and the differentiability of
F_{1}
with respect to each variable.

In order to obtain (
9.72
), we write

\displaystyle F_{1}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta);\delta)

\displaystyle=D_{h}F_{1}(0,0,0;0)[(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\sigma}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta))]+D_{\delta}F_{1}(0,0,0;0)\delta

\displaystyle+O(||(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\sigma}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta))||^{2}+\delta^{2})

\displaystyle=D_{\delta}F_{1}(0,0,0;0)\delta+O(||(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\sigma}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta))||^{2}+\delta^{2}).

By Proposition
26
, we have

\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{\sigma}\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\delta\right)\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}}\leq C(\alpha_{0})\left(||\overset{\circ}{X}||^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{X}}+||\overset{\circ}{\Theta}||^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}}+||\overset{\circ}{\lambda}||^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}+\delta\right).

Thus,

||F_{1}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta);\delta)||_{\hat{C}^{1,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}\leq C(\alpha_{0})\left(||\overset{\circ}{X}||^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{X}}+||\overset{\circ}{\Theta}||^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}}+||\overset{\circ}{\lambda}||^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}+\delta\right).

Now, we write

X_{K}^{-1}F_{1}=(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})X_{K}^{-1}F_{1}+\xi_{N}X_{K}^{-1}F_{1}+\xi_{S}X_{K}^{-1}F_{1}.

By Proposition
24
,
F_{1}
is compactly supported in
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle||X_{K}^{-1}F_{1}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta);\delta)||_{\mathcal{N}_{X}}

\displaystyle=||r^{3}(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})X_{K}^{-1}F_{1}||_{C^{0,\alpha_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq C||F_{1}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\sigma}(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta);\delta)||_{\hat{C}^{1,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})}

\displaystyle\leq C(\alpha_{0})\left(||\overset{\circ}{X}||^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{X}}+||\overset{\circ}{\Theta}||^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}}+||\overset{\circ}{\lambda}||^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}+\delta\right)

2.

We refer to the proof of Proposition
9.2.1
in
[
17
]
in order to estimate
N_{X}^{(1)}
,
N_{Y}^{(1)}
,
N_{Y}^{(2)}
and the remaining terms of
N_{X}^{(2)}
.

∎

Now, we prove Proposition
28
.

Proof.

1.

First of all, by Proposition
26
and Proposition
28
, there exists a solution map
(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},B)(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta)
defined on
B_{\delta_{0}}
which solves (
5.49
) and (
5.50
).

2.

We apply Theorem
6
with
\displaystyle L
defined by

L(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}):=\left(\begin{aligned} &\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\overset{\circ}{X}+\frac{2\partial Y_{K}\cdot\partial\overset{\circ}{Y}}{X_{K}}-\frac{2|\partial Y_{K}|^{2}}{X_{K}^{2}}\overset{\circ}{X}+2\frac{\partial X_{K}\cdot\partial Y_{K}}{X_{K}^{2}}\overset{\circ}{Y}\\
&\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\overset{\circ}{Y}-\frac{2\partial Y_{K}\cdot\partial\overset{\circ}{X}}{X_{K}}-\frac{(|\partial X_{K}|^{2}+|\partial Y_{K}|^{2})}{X_{K}^{2}}\overset{\circ}{Y}\end{aligned}\right),

\displaystyle N=N_{(X,Y)}\;,\;\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{Y}\;,\;\mathcal{Q}=\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\times\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}
and
\displaystyle\mathcal{P}=[0,\delta_{0}[
. By the previous lemma, all the assumptions are satisfied and we obtain the desired result.

∎

9.7
Solving for
\Theta

We recall that
\overset{\circ}{\Theta}
satisfies

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}\overset{\circ}{\Theta}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\sigma}{X^{2}}(\partial_{z}Y+B_{z})+\frac{\rho}{X_{K}^{2}}\partial_{z}Y_{K},

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\overset{\circ}{\Theta}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma}{X^{2}}(\partial_{\rho}Y+B_{\rho})-\frac{\rho}{X_{K}^{2}}\partial_{\rho}Y_{K}.

9.7.1
Linear problem

We prove the following result

Proposition 31
.

Let
H_{\Theta}\in\mathcal{N}_{\Theta}
. Then, there exists
\overset{\circ}{\Theta}\in\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}
which solves the equation

d\overset{\circ}{\Theta}=H_{\Theta}.

(9.73)

It is given by

\overset{\circ}{\Theta}(\rho,z):=-\int_{\rho}^{\infty}\,\left(H_{\Theta}\right)_{\rho}(\tilde{\rho},z)\,d\tilde{\rho}.

(9.74)

Moreover, there exists
C(\alpha_{0})>0
such that

||\overset{\circ}{\sigma}||_{\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}}\leq C(\alpha_{0})||H_{\sigma}||_{\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}}.

Recall that
\mathcal{N}_{\Theta}
is the completion of smooth compactly supported closed
1-
forms under the norm

\displaystyle||F||_{\mathcal{N}_{\Theta}}

\displaystyle:=||r^{3}(1+\rho^{-1})F_{\rho}||_{\hat{C}^{1,\alpha_{0}}\left(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\right)}+||r^{3}F_{z}||_{\hat{C}^{1,\alpha_{0}}\left(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\right)}+||s^{-1}F_{s}||_{\hat{C}^{1,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}})}

\displaystyle+||F_{\chi}||_{\hat{C}^{1,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}})}+||(s^{\prime})^{-1}F_{s^{\prime}}||_{\hat{C}^{1,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}})}+||F_{\chi^{\prime}}||_{\hat{C}^{1,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}})}.

Proof.

1.

First of all, we show that
\overset{\circ}{\Theta}
given by (
9.74
) is well-defined .

When
\rho\to\infty
, we have

\left(H_{\Theta}\right)_{\rho}=O_{\rho\to\infty}(\rho^{-3}).

Therefore,
\overset{\circ}{\Theta}
is well defined.

2.

Now, we show that
\overset{\circ}{\Theta}
solves (
9.73
).

Since
H_{\theta}
is closed, we have

d(H_{\rho}d\rho+H_{z}dz)=dH_{\Theta}=0.

Therefore,

\partial_{z}\left(H_{\Theta}\right)_{\rho}=\partial_{\rho}\left(H_{\Theta}\right)_{z}.

Now, we compute

\partial_{\rho}\overset{\circ}{\Theta}=\left(H_{\Theta}\right)_{\rho}(\rho,z).

We apply the dominated convergence theorem to obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\overset{\circ}{\Theta}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\rho}^{\infty}\,\partial_{z}\left(H_{\Theta}\right)_{\rho}(\tilde{\rho},z)\,d\tilde{\rho}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\rho}^{\infty}\,\partial_{\rho}\left(H_{\Theta}\right)_{z}(\tilde{\rho},z)\,d\tilde{\rho}

\displaystyle=\left(H_{\Theta}\right)_{z}.

3.

Finally, we prove that
\overset{\circ}{\Theta}
lies in
\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}
, that is

\left((1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})r^{2}\overset{\circ}{\Theta}\right)_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\in C^{2,\alpha_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\;,\;\left(\xi_{N}r^{2}\overset{\circ}{\Theta}\right)_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\in C^{2,\alpha_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{4})\;\text{and}\;\left(\xi_{N}r^{2}\overset{\circ}{\Theta}\right)_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\in C^{2,\alpha_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{4}).

To this end, we prove the estimates on the different region: away from the boundary of
\overline{\mathscr{B}}
, near the axis, the horizon and near the poles.

First of all, recall the following change of variables

\displaystyle x

\displaystyle=\rho\cos\vartheta

\displaystyle y

\displaystyle=\rho\sin\vartheta.

We have

\partial_{x}=\frac{x}{\sqrt{x^{2}+y^{2}}}\partial_{\rho}\;,\;\partial_{y}=\frac{y}{\sqrt{x^{2}+y^{2}}}\partial_{\rho}.

(a)

We control the
L^{\infty}
norm of
r^{2}\overset{\circ}{\Theta}
:

i.

Away from the region
\partial\overline{\mathscr{B}}
, say
[\rho_{0},\infty[\times\mathbb{R}
for some
\rho_{0}>0
, there exists
C>0
such that

\forall(\rho,z)\in[\rho_{0},\infty[\times\mathbb{R}\;:\;\left|\overset{\circ}{\Theta}(\rho,z)\right|\leq Cr^{-2}

Indeed,

•

if
(\rho,z)\in[\rho_{0},\infty[\times\mathbb{R}\cap B((0,0),1)
, then the estimates are straightforward.

•

Otherwise, we have
\rho^{2}+z^{2}>1.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\left|\overset{\circ}{\Theta}(\rho,z)\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\rho}^{\infty}\;r^{-3}(\tilde{\rho},z)\,d\tilde{\rho}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\rho}^{\infty}\;\frac{1}{(\tilde{\rho}^{2}+z^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}\,d\tilde{\rho}

\displaystyle=C\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{((\tau+\rho)^{2}+z^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}\,d\tau

\displaystyle=C\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(\rho^{2}+z^{2}+2\rho\tau+\tau^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}\,d\tau

\displaystyle\leq C(\rho^{2}+z^{2})^{-\frac{3}{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\left(1+\left(\frac{\tau}{\sqrt{\rho^{2}+z^{2}}}\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}}\,d\tau

\displaystyle=C(\rho^{2}+z^{2})\int_{0}^{\infty}\,\frac{1}{(1+u^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}\,du

\displaystyle\leq Cr^{-2}(\rho,z).

Therefore, we obtain the estimate for
r^{2}\overset{\circ}{\Theta}
.

ii.

In
\tilde{\mathscr{A}}\subset\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}
, a neighbourhood of the axis or in
\tilde{\mathscr{H}}\subset\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}}
, a neighbourhood of the horizon, we have

\displaystyle\left|\overset{\circ}{\Theta}(\rho,z)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|-\int_{\rho}^{\infty}\,\left(H_{\Theta}\right)_{\rho}(\tilde{\rho},z)\,d\tilde{\rho}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\rho}^{\infty}\;\frac{\tilde{\rho}}{1+\tilde{\rho}}r^{-3}(\tilde{\rho},z)\,d\tilde{\rho}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\rho}^{\infty}\;2\tilde{\rho}\left(1+\tilde{\rho}^{2}+z^{2}\right)^{-\frac{3}{2}}\,d\tilde{\rho}

iii.

In
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}
, we write (
9.73
) in the
(s,\chi)
coordinate system. We have

\partial_{s}\overset{\circ}{\Theta}=(H_{\Theta})_{s}\quad\text{;}\quad\partial_{\chi}\overset{\circ}{\Theta}=(H_{\Theta})_{\chi}.

and

\overset{\circ}{\Theta}(s,\chi)=\int_{0}^{s}\;(H_{\Theta})_{s}(\tilde{s},\chi)\,d\tilde{s}.

We have

\displaystyle\left|(r^{2}\xi_{N}\overset{\circ}{\Theta})_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\right|\leq C\int_{0}^{s}\tilde{s}d\tilde{s}\leq C.

Here, we used the estimate of
s^{-1}(H_{\Theta})_{s}
in the region
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}
.

(b)

We control the
L^{\infty}
norm of
\nabla_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(r^{2}(1-\xi_{N}-\xi_{S})\overset{\circ}{\Theta})_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}
,
\nabla_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}(r^{2}\xi_{N}\overset{\circ}{\Theta})_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}
and
\nabla_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}(r^{2}\xi_{S}\overset{\circ}{\Theta})_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}

We have

\displaystyle\partial_{x}(r^{2}\overset{\circ}{\Theta})_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}

\displaystyle=\frac{x}{\sqrt{x^{2}+y^{2}}}\partial_{\rho}\left(r^{2}(\rho,z)\overset{\circ}{\Theta}(\rho,z)\right),

\displaystyle\partial_{y}(r^{2}\overset{\circ}{\Theta})_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}

\displaystyle=\frac{y}{\sqrt{x^{2}+y^{2}}}\partial_{\rho}\left(r^{2}(\rho,z)\overset{\circ}{\Theta}(\rho,z)\right),

\displaystyle\partial_{z}(r^{2}\overset{\circ}{\Theta})_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}

\displaystyle=\partial_{z}\left(r^{2}(\rho,z)\overset{\circ}{\Theta}(\rho,z)\right).

i.

Away from the boundary
\left\{0\right\}\times\mathbb{R}
, the estimates are straightforward since
H_{\Theta}\in\mathcal{N}_{\Theta}
.

ii.

In
\tilde{\mathscr{A}}\cup\tilde{\mathscr{H}},
we have

\displaystyle\partial_{x}(r^{2}\overset{\circ}{\Theta})_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}

\displaystyle=\frac{x}{\sqrt{x^{2}+y^{2}}}\partial_{\rho}\left(r^{2}(\rho,z)\overset{\circ}{\Theta}(\rho,z)\right)

\displaystyle=x\overset{\circ}{\Theta}(\rho,z)+r^{2}(\rho,z)\frac{x}{\sqrt{x^{2}+y^{2}}}\partial_{\rho}\overset{\circ}{\Theta}.

The first term is easily bounded by
r^{-2}
thanks to (a). As for the second term, we have

\displaystyle\left|r^{2}(\rho,z)\frac{x}{\sqrt{x^{2}+y^{2}}}\partial_{\rho}\overset{\circ}{\Theta}\right|

\displaystyle=r^{2}(\rho,z)\frac{x}{\sqrt{x^{2}+y^{2}}}\left|(H_{\Theta})_{\rho}\right|

\displaystyle\leq Cr^{-1}\frac{\rho}{1+\rho}\frac{x}{\sqrt{x^{2}+y^{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq C.

We control
\partial_{y}(r^{2}\overset{\circ}{\Theta})_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}
in a similar way. As for
\partial_{z}(r^{2}\overset{\circ}{\Theta})_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}
, it is straightforward.

iii.

In
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}
, we have

\partial_{s}(r^{2}\overset{\circ}{\Theta})_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}=\partial_{s}r^{2}\overset{\circ}{\Theta}(s,\chi)+r^{2}(H_{\Theta})_{s}

The previous point and the estimate for
H_{\Theta}
yield the result.

(c)

Now, we control the
L^{\infty}
norm of
\left(\nabla^{2}_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}r^{2}\overset{\circ}{\Theta}\right)
.

i.

Away from the boundary
\left\{0\right\}\times\mathbb{R}
, the estimates are straightforward since
H_{\Theta}\in\mathcal{N}_{\Theta}
.

ii.

In
\tilde{\mathscr{A}}\cup\tilde{\mathscr{H}},
we have

\displaystyle\partial_{xx}\left(r^{2}\overset{\circ}{\Theta}\right)_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}

\displaystyle=\overset{\circ}{\Theta}+\frac{x^{2}}{\sqrt{x^{2}+y^{2}}}\partial_{\rho}\overset{\circ}{\Theta}+x\partial_{x}\left(\frac{r^{2}}{\rho}(H_{\Theta})_{\rho}\right)+\frac{r^{2}}{\rho}(H_{\Theta})_{\rho}.

The above terms are controlled using the previous estimates and the
L^{\infty}
estimate of
\displaystyle\nabla_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(r^{3}\left(1+\frac{1}{\rho}\right)H_{\Theta}\right)
.

iii.

In
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}
, we have

\partial_{ss}(r^{2}\overset{\circ}{\Theta})_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}=\partial_{ss}r^{2}\overset{\circ}{\Theta}(s,\chi)+r^{2}\partial_{s}(H_{\Theta})+2\partial_{s}r^{2}(H_{\Theta})_{s}.

The estimate is straigthforward.

(d)

Finally, we control

\sup_{X_{1}\neq X_{2}}\frac{\left|\left|\left(\nabla^{2}_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}r^{2}\overset{\circ}{\Theta}\right)(X_{1})-\left(\nabla^{2}_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}r^{2}\overset{\circ}{\Theta}\right)(X_{2})\right|\right|}{\left|X_{1}-X_{2}\right|^{\alpha_{0}}}\leq Cr^{-2}.

The estimates are straightforward thanks to the control of
H_{\Theta}
and the regularity of the different terms.

∎

9.7.2
Non-linear estimates

We apply Theorem
6
in order to obtain

Proposition 32
.

Let
\alpha_{0}\in(0,1)
and let
\overline{\delta}_{0}>0
. Then, there exists
0<\delta_{0}\leq\overline{\delta}_{0}
such that
\displaystyle\forall\left(\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\delta\right)\in B_{\delta_{0}}\left(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\times[0,\infty[\right)
there exists a unique one parameter family
\left(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},\left(0,0,B_{z}^{(A)}\right),\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}\right),\overset{\circ}{\Theta}\right)\left(\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\delta\right)\in B_{\delta_{0}}

\left(\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}\times\mathcal{L}_{B}\times\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{Y}\times\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\right)
which solves (
5.49
), (
9.53
), (
5.51
) and (
5.52
) and which satisfies

\left|\left|\left(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},\left(0,0,B_{z}^{(A)}\right),\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}\right),\overset{\circ}{\Theta}\right)\left(\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\delta\right)\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}\times\mathcal{L}_{B}\times\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{Y}\times\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}}\leq C(\alpha_{0})\left(||\overset{\circ}{\lambda}||^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}+\delta\right)

and
\displaystyle\forall\left(\overset{\circ}{\lambda}_{i},\delta_{i}\right)\in B_{\delta_{0}}\left(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\times[0,\infty[\right)
,

\displaystyle\left|\left|\left(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},\left(0,0,B_{z}^{(A)}\right),\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}\right),\overset{\circ}{\Theta}\right)\left(\overset{\circ}{\lambda}_{1},\delta_{1}\right)-\left(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},\left(0,0,B_{z}^{(A)}\right),\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}\right),\overset{\circ}{\Theta}\right)\left(\overset{\circ}{\lambda}_{2},\delta_{2}\right)\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}\times\mathcal{L}_{B}\times\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{Y}\times\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\alpha_{0})\left(\left(\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{\lambda}_{1}\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}+\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{\lambda}_{2}\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}\right)\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{\lambda}_{1}-\overset{\circ}{\lambda}_{2}\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}+\left|\delta_{1}-\delta_{2}\right|\right).

Before we prove the above proposition, we introduce the following notations

1.

Define
H_{\Theta}
on
\overline{\mathscr{B}}
to be the one form which expression in the
(\rho,z)
coordinates is

\displaystyle(H_{\Theta})_{\rho}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\rho}{X_{K}^{2}}\frac{(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma})}{(1+\overset{\circ}{X})^{2}}(\partial_{z}(Y_{K}+X_{K}\overset{\circ}{Y})+B_{z})+\frac{\rho}{X_{K}^{2}}\partial_{z}Y_{K},

\displaystyle(H_{\Theta})_{z}

\displaystyle=\frac{\rho}{X_{K}^{2}}\frac{(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma})}{(1+\overset{\circ}{X})^{2}}(\partial_{\rho}(Y_{K}+X_{K}\overset{\circ}{Y})+B_{\rho})-\frac{\rho}{X_{K}^{2}}\partial_{\rho}Y_{K}.

2.

Let
\overline{\delta}_{0}>0
be obtained by Proposition
30
. Define the nonlinear operator
N_{\Theta}
on
B_{\overline{\delta}_{0}}(\mathcal{L}_{\Theta})\times B_{\overline{\delta}_{0}}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\times[0,\infty[)
by

\displaystyle\left[N_{\Theta}\left(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta)\right)\right]_{\rho}(\rho,z):=-\frac{\rho}{X_{K}^{2}}\frac{(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta))}{(1+\overset{\circ}{X}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta))^{2}}(\partial_{z}(Y_{K}+X_{K}\overset{\circ}{Y}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta))+B_{z}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta))+\frac{\rho}{X_{K}^{2}}\partial_{z}Y_{K}

\displaystyle\left[N_{\Theta}\left(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta)\right)\right]_{z}(\rho,z):=\frac{\rho}{X_{K}^{2}}\frac{(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta))}{(1+\overset{\circ}{X}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta))^{2}}(\partial_{\rho}(Y_{K}+X_{K}\overset{\circ}{Y}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta))+B_{\rho}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta))-\frac{\rho}{X_{K}^{2}}\partial_{\rho}Y_{K}

and set

\displaystyle\left[N_{\Theta}\left(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\left[N_{\Theta}\left(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta)\right)\right]_{\rho}d\rho+\left[N_{\Theta}\left(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta)\right)\right]_{z}dz,

where
(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},B,\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y})
are the solution mappings obtained by Proposition
30
.

In order to prove the above proposition, we will need the following lemma

Lemma 86
.

1.

There exists
\overline{\delta}_{0}>0
such that
\displaystyle N_{\Theta}\left(B_{\overline{\delta}_{0}}(\mathcal{L}_{\Theta})\times B_{\overline{\delta}_{0}}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\times[0,\infty[)\right)\subset\mathcal{N}_{\Theta}
.

2.

There exist
0<\delta_{0}\leq\overline{\delta}_{0}
and
C(\alpha_{0})>0
such that
\forall\left(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta\right)\in B_{\overline{\delta}_{0}}(\mathcal{L}_{\Theta})\times B_{\overline{\delta}_{0}}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\times[0,\infty[)

||H_{\Theta}||_{\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}}\leq C(\alpha_{0})\left(\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{\Theta}\right|\right|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}}+||\overset{\circ}{\lambda}||^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}+\delta\right).

Proof.

1.

Let
\overline{\delta}_{0}>0
be obtained by Proposition
30
and let
(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta)\in(B_{\overline{\delta}_{0}}(\mathcal{L}_{\Theta})\times B_{\overline{\delta}_{0}}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\times[0,\infty[).
First of all, we show that
\displaystyle N_{\Theta}\left(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta\right)
is a differentiable closed one-form on
\overline{\mathscr{B}}
.

•

For the differentiability, the only terms that we need to analyse are

\frac{\rho}{X_{K}^{2}}\partial_{z}Y_{K}\quad\text{,}\quad\frac{\rho}{X_{K}^{2}}\partial_{\rho}Y_{K}\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{\rho}{X_{K}^{2}}.

The differentiability of the remaining terms as well as the differentiability away from the
\partial\overline{\mathscr{B}}
follow because
(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},B,\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y})(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\lambda)
is differentiable.

•

Now, we check that
\displaystyle N_{\Theta}\left(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta\right)
is closed.

–

(\overset{\circ}{Y},B)(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta)
solve (
5.51
) and (
5.50
). We set

\theta:=dY+B\quad\text{and}\quad\theta_{K}:=dY_{K}.

–

Therefore,

N_{\Theta}\left(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta)\right)=-\frac{\sigma}{X^{2}}\left(\theta_{z}d\rho-\theta_{\rho}dz\right)+\frac{\rho}{X_{K}^{2}}\left(\theta_{z}^{K}-\theta_{\rho}^{K}dz\right).

–

Moreover,
\theta
verifies

\sigma^{-1}\partial_{\rho}(\sigma\theta_{\rho})+\sigma^{-1}\partial_{z}(\sigma\theta_{z})=\frac{2\theta_{\rho}\partial_{\rho}X+2\theta_{z}\partial_{z}X}{X}

–

Straightforward computations and the used of the above equation imply that the one forms
\displaystyle\frac{\sigma}{X^{2}}\left(\theta_{z}d\rho-\theta_{\rho}dz\right)
and
\displaystyle\frac{\rho}{X_{K}^{2}}\left(\theta_{z}^{K}-\theta_{\rho}^{K}dz\right)
are closed.

–

Hence,
\displaystyle N_{\Theta}\left(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta)\right)
is closed.

2.

Let
\displaystyle(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta)\in(B_{\overline{\delta}_{0}}(\mathcal{L}_{\Theta})\times B_{\overline{\delta}_{0}}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\times[0,\infty[).
We show that
N_{\Theta}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta)
provided
\delta
sufficiently small. To this end, we show the estimates in the region
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}}
, then in
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}
and
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}}
. In order to lighten the expressions, we omit the dependence of
\overset{\circ}{\sigma},\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y},B
on the quantities
\displaystyle(\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta)
.
We re-write
\left(N_{\Theta}\right)_{\rho}
and
\left(N_{\Theta}\right)_{z}
on the form

\displaystyle\left(N_{\Theta}\right)_{\rho}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\rho}{X_{K}^{2}}\frac{(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma})}{(1+\overset{\circ}{X})^{2}}(\partial_{z}(X_{K}\overset{\circ}{Y}+Y_{K}))+B_{z})+\frac{\rho}{X_{K}^{2}}\partial_{z}Y_{K}

\displaystyle=\frac{\rho}{X_{K}^{2}}\frac{-1-\overset{\circ}{\sigma}+(1+\overset{\circ}{X})^{2}}{(1+\overset{\circ}{X}^{2})^{2}}\partial_{z}Y_{K}-\frac{\rho}{X_{K}}\frac{(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma})}{(1+\overset{\circ}{X})^{2}}B_{z}-\frac{\sigma}{X_{K}^{2}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})^{2}}\partial_{z}(X_{K}^{2}(X_{K}^{-1}\overset{\circ}{Y}))

\displaystyle=\frac{\rho}{X_{K}^{2}}\frac{-\overset{\circ}{\sigma}+\overset{\circ}{X}^{2}+2\overset{\circ}{X}}{(1+\overset{\circ}{X}^{2})^{2}}\partial_{z}Y_{K}-\frac{\rho}{X_{K}}\frac{(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma})}{(1+\overset{\circ}{X})^{2}}B_{z}-\frac{\rho(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma})}{(1+\overset{\circ}{X})^{2}}\partial_{z}(X_{K}^{-1}\overset{\circ}{Y})

\displaystyle-2\frac{\rho(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma})}{(1+\overset{\circ}{X})^{2}}X_{K}^{-1}\overset{\circ}{Y}\partial_{z}\log X_{K}.

\displaystyle\left(N_{\Theta}\right)_{z}

\displaystyle=\frac{\rho}{X_{K}^{2}}\frac{-\overset{\circ}{\sigma}+\overset{\circ}{X}^{2}+2\overset{\circ}{X}}{(1+\overset{\circ}{X}^{2})^{2}}\partial_{\rho}Y_{K}-\frac{\rho}{X_{K}}\frac{(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma})}{(1+\overset{\circ}{X})^{2}}B_{\rho}-\frac{\rho(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma})}{(1+\overset{\circ}{X})^{2}}\partial_{\rho}(X_{K}^{-1}\overset{\circ}{Y})

\displaystyle-2\frac{\rho(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma})}{(1+\overset{\circ}{X})^{2}}X_{K}^{-1}\overset{\circ}{Y}\partial_{\rho}\log X_{K}.

•

In
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}}
, we prove that
\displaystyle r^{3}(1+\rho^{-1})\left(N_{\Theta}\right)_{\rho}
and
r^{3}(N_{\Theta})_{z}
are bounded in
\hat{C}^{1,\alpha_{0}}
. We have

–

By Lemma
54
,

\frac{1}{X^{2}_{K}}|\partial_{z}Y_{K}|\leq Cr^{-5},\quad|\partial\left(X^{-2}_{K}\partial Y_{K}\right)|\leq Cr^{-6},\quad|\partial^{2}\left(X^{-2}_{K}\partial Y_{K}\right)|\leq Cr^{-7}

and

\frac{\rho}{X^{2}_{K}}|\partial Y_{K}|\leq Cr^{-4},\quad|\partial\left(X^{-1}_{K}\partial Y_{K}\right)|\leq Cr^{-4},\quad|\partial^{2}\left(X^{-1}_{K}\partial Y_{K}\right)|\leq Cr^{-5}.

Thus, the term
\displaystyle\frac{\rho}{X_{K}^{2}}\partial_{z}Y_{K}
is bounded by
\displaystyle r^{3}(1+\rho^{-1})\frac{\rho}{X_{K}^{2}}\partial_{z}Y_{K}
is bounded in
\hat{C}^{1,\alpha_{0}}
.

–

By Lemma
56
, the term
\partial_{z}(\log X_{K})\in C^{\infty}(\overline{\mathscr{B}})
and satisfies

\left|\partial_{z}\log X_{K}\right|=O_{r\to\infty}(r^{-2}).

–

Moreover, the quantities
\displaystyle r^{3}X_{K}^{-1}\overset{\circ}{Y}
,
\displaystyle r^{4}X_{K}^{-1}\overset{\circ}{Y}
and
\displaystyle r^{5}X_{K}^{-1}\overset{\circ}{Y}
are bounded in

, which implies that the term
\displaystyle r^{3}(1+\rho^{-1})X_{K}^{-1}\overset{\circ}{Y}
is bounded in
\hat{C}^{1,\alpha_{0}}

–

Finally, recall that we control
\displaystyle\frac{(1+\rho^{10})(1+r^{10})}{\rho^{10}}B_{z}
in
\hat{C}^{1,\alpha_{0}}
.

–

This yields to estimates in the region
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}}
.

•

In the region
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}
, we write
N_{\Theta}
in
(s,\chi)
coordinates

–

We have

\displaystyle N_{\Theta}

\displaystyle=(N_{\Theta})_{\rho}d\rho+(N_{\Theta})_{z}dz

\displaystyle=(\chi(N_{\Theta})_{\rho}(s,\chi)-s(N_{\Theta})_{z}(s,\chi))ds+(s(N_{\Theta})_{\rho}(s,\chi)+\chi(N_{\Theta})_{z}(s,\chi))d\chi.

–

Moreover,

\partial_{\rho}=\frac{\chi}{\chi^{2}+s^{2}}\partial_{s}+\frac{s}{\chi^{2}+s^{2}}\partial_{\chi},\quad\quad\partial_{z}=\frac{-s}{\chi^{2}+s^{2}}\partial_{s}+\frac{\chi}{\chi^{2}+s^{2}}\partial_{\chi}.

–

Therefore,

\displaystyle(N_{\Theta})_{s}

\displaystyle=\chi\left(-\frac{\sigma}{X^{2}}\left(\frac{-s}{\chi^{2}+s^{2}}\partial_{s}Y+\frac{\chi}{\chi^{2}+s^{2}}\partial_{\chi}Y\right)\right)-s\left(\frac{\sigma}{X^{2}}\left(\frac{\chi}{\chi^{2}+s^{2}}\partial_{s}Y+\frac{s}{\chi^{2}+s^{2}}\partial_{\chi}Y\right)\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{\sigma}{X^{2}}B_{\chi}

\displaystyle=\frac{s\chi}{X_{K}^{2}}\frac{-\overset{\circ}{\sigma}+\overset{\circ}{X}^{2}+2\overset{\circ}{X}}{(1+\overset{\circ}{X}^{2})^{2}}\partial_{\chi}Y_{K}-\frac{s\chi(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma})}{(1+\overset{\circ}{X})^{2}}\partial_{\chi}(X_{K}^{-1}\overset{\circ}{Y})-2\frac{s\chi(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma})}{(1+\overset{\circ}{X})^{2}}X_{K}^{-1}\overset{\circ}{Y}\partial_{\chi}\log X_{K}

\displaystyle-\frac{\sigma}{X^{2}}B_{\chi}.

In the same manner, we obtain

(N_{\Theta})_{\chi}=\frac{s\chi}{X_{K}^{2}}\frac{-\overset{\circ}{\sigma}+\overset{\circ}{X}^{2}+2\overset{\circ}{X}}{(1+\overset{\circ}{X}^{2})^{2}}\partial_{s}Y_{K}-\frac{s\chi(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma})}{(1+\overset{\circ}{X})^{2}}\partial_{s}(X_{K}^{-1}\overset{\circ}{Y})-2\frac{s\chi(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma})}{(1+\overset{\circ}{X})^{2}}X_{K}^{-1}\overset{\circ}{Y}\partial_{s}\log X_{K}-\frac{\sigma}{X^{2}}B_{s}.

•

Now, we estimate
s^{-1}(N_{\Theta})_{s}
in
\displaystyle\hat{C}^{1,\alpha_{0}}
:

–

We start with
\displaystyle\frac{s\chi\partial_{\chi}Y_{K}}{X_{K}^{2}}
. We recall that
Y_{K}

dY_{K}=\theta_{K}

which takes the following form in the
(s,\chi)
coordinates

\partial_{s}(X_{K}^{-1}W_{K})ds+\partial_{\chi}(X_{K}^{-1}W_{K})d\chi=\frac{s\chi}{X_{K}^{2}}\left(\partial_{\chi}Y_{K}-\partial_{s}Y_{K}\right).

Moreover, we recall the
(x,y,u,v)
coordinates defined by (
5.59
) so that any function
f(s,\chi)
defined on
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}
can be see as a function
f_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}(x,y,u,v)
defined on
\mathbb{R}^{4}

Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{s\chi}{X_{K}^{2}}\partial_{\chi}Y_{K}

\displaystyle=\partial_{s}(X_{K}^{-1}W_{K})

\displaystyle=s\left.\partial_{xx}\left(\frac{W_{K}}{X_{K}}\right)\right|_{(0,\sqrt{x^{2}+y^{2}},u,v)}.

Now, we recall that
\displaystyle\frac{W_{K}}{X_{K}}
is smooth on
\overline{\mathscr{B}}
. This yields the
\hat{C}^{1,\alpha_{0}}
estimates of
\displaystyle\frac{\chi}{X_{K}^{2}}\partial_{\chi}Y_{K}
.

–

The remaining terms are easily controlled in
\hat{C}^{1,\alpha_{0}}
due to the estimates for
X_{K}^{-1}\overset{\circ}{Y}
and
B
.

–

Finally, the estimates for
(N_{\Theta})_{\chi}
follow similarly.

•

In the region
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}}
, the estimates are obtained as in
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}
.

∎

.

9.8.1
Solving the modified equations (
9.81
)

We begin by introducing some notations: let
\overline{\delta}_{0}>0
and let
\alpha_{0}\in(0,1)
. Let
0<\delta_{0}\leq\overline{\delta}_{0}
be obtained by Proposition
32
. Let
\displaystyle\mathscr{L}:B_{\delta_{0}}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda})\times[0,\delta_{0}[\to\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}
be the mapping defined by

\mathscr{L}(\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\delta)(\rho,z):=-\int_{\rho}^{\infty}\left(\alpha_{\rho}-(\alpha_{K})_{\rho}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\rho}\log(1+\overset{\circ}{X})\right)(\tau,z)\,d\tau.

(9.82)

Here,
(\sigma,\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta})=(\sigma,\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta})(\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\delta)
. We omit the dependence on the
(\overset{\circ}{\lambda};\delta)
in order to lighten the expressions.

Now, we state the following lemma

Lemma 87
.

Let
(\sigma,\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{\Theta})
be the solution map obtained by Proposition
32
. Then,
\forall(\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\delta)\in B_{\delta_{0}}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda})\times[0,\delta_{0}[
, the one-form
\alpha
defined by (
9.78
) and (
9.79
) satisfies

d\alpha=\beta_{2}\wedge\left(d\lambda-\alpha-\frac{1}{2}d\log X\right).

where
\beta_{2}
is defined by (
9.76
).

Proof.

We check the assumptions of Theorem
7

1.

The regularity assumptions are satisfied since the quantities
\displaystyle\left(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},B,\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y},\overset{\circ}{\Theta}\right)(\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\delta)
as well as
\overset{\circ}{\lambda}
lie in the right spaces.

2.

\displaystyle\left(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},B,\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y},\overset{\circ}{\Theta}\right)(\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\delta)
solve their respective equations on
\overline{\mathscr{B}}
. Therefore, the original metric data
(X,W,\theta,\sigma)(\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\delta)
solve their respective equations on
\mathscr{B}
.

3.

Since
\sigma_{K}=\rho,

d\sigma_{K}=d\rho
on
\mathscr{B}
. Therefore
|d\sigma_{K}|\neq 0
. Now, we choose
\delta_{0}>0
sufficiently small so that
|d\sigma|\neq 0
. Indeed,

\displaystyle\left|d\sigma\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left|d\left(\rho(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma})\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma}+\rho\partial_{\rho}\overset{\circ}{\sigma})^{2}+(\rho\partial_{z}\overset{\circ}{\sigma})^{2}.

On
\mathscr{B}
, we have

\displaystyle\left|d\sigma\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma})^{2}+2\rho(\partial_{\rho}\overset{\circ}{\sigma})(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma})

\displaystyle\geq 1-4\delta_{0}.

The latter is obtained by assuming that
\delta_{0}<1
and by the control of
L^{\infty}
norm of
\rho\partial_{\rho}\overset{\circ}{\sigma}
. Therefore, we choose
\delta_{0}
so that the latter is positive.

4.

\mathchoice{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha\beta}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha\beta}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha\beta}}}}{{T}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha\beta}}}}
is divergence free and satisfies (
5.16
) (See Section
5.3
) with respect to
g
given by (
5.1
).

Therefore, we apply Theorem
7
to obtain that

•

\alpha
satisfies (
9.75
),

•

if
\lambda
satisfies (
9.77
), then
\lambda
also satisfies (
9.80
).

∎

The remaining of this section is to prove the following result

Lemma 88
.

Let
\overline{\delta}_{0}>0
and let
\alpha_{0}\in(0,1)
. There exists
0<\delta_{0}\leq\overline{\delta}_{0}
such that
\displaystyle\forall\overset{\circ}{\lambda}\in B_{\delta_{0}}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda})\,;\,\forall\delta\in[0,\delta_{0}[
,

•

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}(\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\delta)
is well-defined and lies in
\displaystyle B_{\delta_{0}}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda})
,

•

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}(\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\delta)
solves (
9.81
) and verifies

\displaystyle\left|\left|\mathscr{L}(\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\delta)\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}\leq C\left(\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{\lambda}\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}^{2}+\delta\right),

\displaystyle\left|\left|\mathscr{L}(\overset{\circ}{\lambda}_{1},\delta_{1})-\mathscr{L}(\overset{\circ}{\lambda}_{2},\delta_{2})\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}\leq C\left(\left(\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{\lambda}_{1}\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}+\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{\lambda}_{2}\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}\right)\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{\lambda}_{1}-\overset{\circ}{\lambda}_{2}\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}+|\delta_{1}-\delta_{2}|\right),

Proof.

The proof is similar to what has been done before: the estimates are proven in each region
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}},\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}},\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}}
.

1.

First of all, we claim that there exists
C(\alpha_{0})>0
such that

\left|\left|r^{2}(\alpha-\alpha_{K})\right|\right|_{\hat{C}^{1,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}})}+\left|\left|r^{2}d\log(1+\overset{\circ}{X})\right|\right|_{\hat{C}^{1,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}})}\leq C\delta+C\left|\left|\left(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},B,(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}),\overset{\circ}{\Theta}\right)\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}\times\cdots\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}}

•

We start with the term
\alpha-\alpha_{K}
. We write

\displaystyle(\alpha-\alpha_{K})_{\rho}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\left(|\partial\sigma|^{2}(\partial_{\rho}\sigma)\sigma X^{-2}(\partial_{\rho}X)^{2}-\rho X_{K}^{-2}(\partial_{\rho}X_{K})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}(\partial_{\rho}\sigma)|\partial\sigma|^{-2}\sigma\frac{-(\partial_{z}X)^{2}+(\theta_{\rho})^{2}-(\theta_{z})^{2}}{X^{2}}+|\partial\sigma|^{-2}(\partial_{\rho}\sigma)(\partial^{2}_{\rho}\sigma-\partial^{2}_{z}\sigma)+|\partial\sigma|^{-2}(\partial_{z}\sigma)((\partial^{2}_{\rho,z}\sigma))

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}|\partial\sigma|^{-2}X^{-2}((\partial_{\rho}X)(\partial_{z}X)+(\theta_{\rho})(\theta_{z})))-\frac{1}{4}\rho X_{K}^{-2}(-(\partial_{z}X_{K})^{2}+(\partial_{\rho}Y_{K})^{2}-(\partial_{z}Y_{K})^{2})

Recall from Lemma
55
and Lemma
56
that
\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}\log X_{K}
and thus
\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}\log X
behave like
\displaystyle\frac{1}{\rho}
near the axis.

Furthermore, since the renormalised unknowns lie in the right space, the
\hat{C}^{1,\alpha_{0}}
estimates is straightforward for all the terms except the following:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\left(|\partial\sigma|^{-2}(\partial_{\rho}\sigma)\sigma X^{-2}(\partial_{\rho}X)^{2}-\rho X_{K}^{-2}(\partial_{\rho}X_{K})^{2}\right)

In order to estimate the latter, we write

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\left(|\partial\sigma|^{-2}(\partial_{\rho}\sigma)\sigma X^{-2}(\partial_{\rho}X)^{2}-\rho X_{K}^{-2}(\partial_{\rho}X_{K})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\left(|\partial\sigma|^{-2}(\partial_{\rho}\sigma)\sigma(\partial_{\rho}\log X)^{2}-\rho(\partial_{\rho}\log X_{K})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\left(1-\rho|\partial\sigma|^{-2}\left(\partial_{\rho}\overset{\circ}{\sigma}(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma})+\rho|\partial\overset{\circ}{\sigma}|^{2}\right)\right)\rho\left(\partial_{\rho}\log(1+\overset{\circ}{X})\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\rho\left(\partial_{\rho}\log X_{K}\right)\left(\partial_{\rho}\log(1+\overset{\circ}{X})\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}|\partial\sigma|^{-2}\left((1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma})\partial_{\rho}\overset{\circ}{\sigma}+\rho|\partial\overset{\circ}{\sigma}|^{2}\right)\rho^{2}\left(\partial_{\rho}\log X_{K}\right)^{2}.

Since
\rho\partial_{\rho}\log X_{K}
is bounded and smooth near the axis and all the remaining terms are
C^{1,\alpha}
controlled. We obtain the desired estimate.

•

The term
\displaystyle r^{2}d\log(1+\overset{\circ}{X})
is easily controlled by
\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{X}\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{X}}
in
\hat{C}^{1,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}})
.

•

We apply the dominated convergence theorem and obtain that
\mathscr{L}(\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\delta)
is well defined and lies in
\hat{C}^{1,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}})
. Moreover, we have the bounds

\left|\left|\mathscr{L}(\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\delta)\right|\right|_{\hat{C}^{1,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}})}\leq C\left|\left|\left(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},B,(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}),\overset{\circ}{\Theta}\right)\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}\times\cdots\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}}.

2.

Now, we show that
\mathscr{L}(\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\delta)
solves (
9.81
). We compute,

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}\mathscr{L}(\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\delta)

\displaystyle=\left(\alpha_{\rho}-(\alpha_{K})_{\rho}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\rho}\log(1+\overset{\circ}{X})\right)(\rho,z)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\mathscr{L}(\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\delta)

\displaystyle=-\int_{\rho}^{\infty}\partial_{z}\left(\alpha_{\rho}-(\alpha_{K})_{\rho}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\rho}\log(1+\overset{\circ}{X})\right)(\tau,z)\,d\tau

By Lemma
87
,
\alpha
verifies

\displaystyle d(\alpha-\alpha_{K})

\displaystyle=d\alpha

\displaystyle=\beta_{2}\wedge\left(d\lambda-\alpha-\frac{1}{2}d\log X\right)

\displaystyle=\beta_{2}\wedge\left(d\overset{\circ}{\lambda}-d\lambda_{K}-\alpha-\frac{1}{2}d\log X_{K}-\frac{1}{2}d\log(1+\overset{\circ}{X})\right).

We recall that

d\lambda_{K}=\alpha_{K}-\frac{1}{2}d\log X_{K}.

Hence,

\displaystyle d(\alpha-\alpha_{K})

\displaystyle=\beta_{2}\wedge\left(d\overset{\circ}{\lambda}-(\alpha-\alpha_{K})-\frac{1}{2}d\log(1+\overset{\circ}{X})\right)

Hence,

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\mathscr{L}(\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\delta)

\displaystyle=\alpha_{z}(\rho,z)-(\alpha_{z})_{K}(\rho,z)-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{z}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})-\int_{\rho}^{\infty}(d\alpha)_{\rho z}(\tau,z)\,d\tau

\displaystyle=\alpha_{z}(\rho,z)-(\alpha_{z})_{K}(\rho,z)-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{z}(1+\overset{\circ}{X})

\displaystyle-\int_{\rho}^{\infty}\left(\beta_{2}\wedge\left(d\overset{\circ}{\lambda}-(\alpha-\alpha_{K})-\frac{1}{2}d\log(1+\overset{\circ}{X})\right)_{\rho z}(\tau,z)\right)\,d\tau.

3.

The estimates in the region
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}
follow in the same manner up to computations for the change of coordinates from
(\rho,z)
to
(s,\chi)
. We refer to the proof of Lemma 12.2.2 in
[
17
]
for details.

\left|\left|\mathscr{(}\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\delta)\right|\right|_{\hat{C}^{1,\alpha_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}})}\leq C\left|\left|\left(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},B,(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}),\overset{\circ}{\Theta}\right)\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}\times\cdots\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}}.

4.

The remaining estimates follow in the same manner.

5.

Finally, we choose
\delta_{0}>0
sufficiently small that
\displaystyle\mathscr{L}(\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\delta)\in B_{\delta_{0}}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda})
.

∎

9.8.2
Solving the original equations (
5.53
)

Now, let
\delta_{0}>0
and
(\overset{\circ}{\lambda}(\delta))_{\delta\in[0,\delta_{0}[}
be the one-parameter family of solutions to (
9.81
). In this section, we will show that the latter actually satisfies (
9.77
), that is

d\overset{\circ}{\lambda}=\alpha-\alpha_{K}-\frac{1}{2}d\log(1+\overset{\circ}{X}).

To this end, let
z\in\mathbb{R}
be fixed and set

f_{z}(\rho):=\left(\partial_{z}\overset{\circ}{\lambda}-(\alpha_{z}-(\alpha_{K})_{z})-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{z}\log(1+\overset{\circ}{X})\right)(\rho,z)

We state the following lemma

Lemma 89
.

\forall z\in\mathbb{R}
we have

\forall\rho\geq 0\;,\;f_{z}(\rho)=0.

Proof.

•

First of all, since
\overset{\circ}{\lambda}(\delta)
solves (
9.81
), we have

\displaystyle f_{z}(\rho)

\displaystyle=\left(\partial_{z}\overset{\circ}{\lambda}-(\alpha_{z}-(\alpha_{K})_{z})-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{z}\log(1+\overset{\circ}{X})\right)(\rho,z)

\displaystyle=\int_{\rho}^{\infty}\,\left(\beta_{2}\wedge\left(d\overset{\circ}{\lambda}-(\alpha-\alpha_{K})-\frac{1}{2}d\log(1+\overset{\circ}{X})\right)\right)_{\rho,z}(\tau,z)\,d\tau

\displaystyle=\int_{\rho}^{\infty}\,\left(((\beta_{2})_{\rho}d\rho+(\beta_{2})_{z}dz)\wedge\left(\partial_{z}\overset{\circ}{\lambda}-(\alpha-\alpha_{K})_{z}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{z}\log(1+\overset{\circ}{X})\right)\right)_{\rho,z}(\tau,z)\,d\tau

\displaystyle=\int_{\rho}^{\infty}\,(\beta_{2})_{\rho}(\tau,z)f_{z}(\tau)\,d\tau.

•

Now, since
\overset{\circ}{\sigma}\in\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}
, we have

\left|(\beta_{2})_{\rho}\right|\leq Cr^{-3}.

Therefore
\forall\rho_{0}>0
, there exists
c_{0}=c_{0}(\rho_{0})>0
such that

\forall(\rho,z)\in[\rho_{0},\infty[\times\mathbb{R}\;,\;\left|(\beta_{2})_{\rho}\right|\leq C\rho^{-3}.

Moreover, there exists
c_{1}>0
such that

\forall(\rho,z)\in\overline{\mathscr{B}}\;\;,\;\;\left|f_{z}(\rho)\right|\leq c_{1}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\left|f_{z}(\rho)\right|

\displaystyle\leq c_{0}\int_{\rho}^{\infty}\,\left|f_{z}(\tau)\right|\tau^{-3}\,d\tau

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c_{0}c_{1}}{2}\rho^{-2}.

•

Therefore

\forall n\in\mathbb{N}\;,\;\forall(\rho,z)\in[\rho_{0},\infty[\times\mathbb{R}\;\left|f_{z}(\rho)\right|\leq c_{1}\left(\frac{c_{0}}{2\rho^{2}}\right)^{n}\frac{1}{n!}

•

Hence, when
n\to\infty
, the right hand side goes to
0
and thus,
\forall\rho_{0}>0\;,\;\forall(\rho,z)\in[\rho_{0},\infty[\times\mathbb{R}\;:\;f_{z}(\rho)=0.

Finally, by continuity of
f_{z}
,
f_{z}
vanishes on
[0,\infty[
.
∎

Therefore, by the previous Lemma, we have

\partial_{z}\overset{\circ}{\lambda}=\alpha_{z}-(\alpha_{K})_{z}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{z}\log(1+\overset{\circ}{X}).

9.8.3
Conclusion

Finally, we combine the previous results in order to prove Proposition
33
:

Proof.

1.

First of all, we apply Proposition
32
to find a one-parameter family of solutions
\displaystyle\left(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},\left(0,0,B_{z}^{(A)}\right),\right.

\left.\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}\right),\overset{\circ}{\Theta}\right)\left(\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\delta\right)
to their respective equations which depends continuously on
(\overset{\circ}{\lambda},\delta)\in B_{\delta_{0}}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda})\times[0,\delta_{0}[
.

2.

We apply Lemma
88
in order to show that
\mathscr{L}
satisfies the assumptions of Theorem
5
.

3.

We apply Theorem
5
with:

\mathcal{T}=\mathscr{L}\;,\;\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\;,\;\mathcal{P}=[0,\overline{\delta}_{0}[

Therefore, after choosing
0<\delta_{0}\leq\overline{\delta}_{0}
, we obtain a one-parameter family of solutions
(\overset{\circ}{\lambda}(\delta))_{\delta\in[0,\delta_{0}[}
satisfying

\displaystyle\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{\lambda}(\delta)\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}\leq C\delta,

\displaystyle\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{\lambda}(\delta_{1})-\overset{\circ}{\lambda}(\delta_{2})\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}\leq C|\delta_{1}-\delta_{2}|,

4.

Henceforth, the solution map
\left(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},\left(0,0,B_{z}^{(A)}\right),\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}\right),\overset{\circ}{\Theta}\right)
can be seen as a one-parameter family depending on
\delta
in the following way

\displaystyle\left|\left|\left(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},\left(0,0,B_{z}^{(A)}\right),\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}\right),\overset{\circ}{\Theta}\right)\left(\delta\right)\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}\times\mathcal{L}_{B}\times\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{Y}\times\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}}\leq C(\alpha_{0})\left(||\overset{\circ}{\lambda}(\delta)||^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}+\delta\right)

\displaystyle\leq C(\delta^{2}+\delta)\leq C\delta.

and
\displaystyle\forall\delta_{i}\in[0,\delta_{0}[
,

\displaystyle\left|\left|\left(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},\left(0,0,B_{z}^{(A)}\right),\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}\right),\overset{\circ}{\Theta}\right)\left(\delta_{1}\right)-\left(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},\left(0,0,B_{z}^{(A)}\right),\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}\right),\overset{\circ}{\Theta}\right)\left(\delta_{2}\right)\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}\times\mathcal{L}_{B}\times\mathcal{L}_{X}\times\mathcal{L}_{Y}\times\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\alpha_{0})\left(\left(\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{\lambda}(\delta_{1})\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}+\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{\lambda}(\delta_{2})\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}\right)\left|\left|\overset{\circ}{\lambda}(\delta_{1})-\overset{\circ}{\lambda}(\delta_{2})\right|\right|_{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}}+\left|\delta_{1}-\delta_{2}\right|\right)

\displaystyle\leq C(\alpha_{0})\delta.

∎

10
Proof of the Main Result

By the previous section, we obtain the following result

Proposition 34
.

Let
\delta_{0}>0
and let
\displaystyle\delta\ni[0,\delta_{0}[\to\left(\overset{\circ}{\sigma},B,\left(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{Y}\right),\overset{\circ}{\Theta},\overset{\circ}{\lambda}\right)(\delta)
be the one parameter family family of solutions to the reduced EV-system obtained by Proposition
33
. Then,

•

the metric
g_{\delta}
given by

g_{\delta}:=-V_{\delta}dt^{2}+2W_{\delta}dtd\phi+X_{\delta}d\phi^{2}+e^{2\lambda_{\delta}}\left(d\rho^{2}+dz^{2}\right)

where

\displaystyle\lambda_{\delta}

\displaystyle:=\overset{\circ}{\lambda}(\delta)+\lambda_{K}

(10.1)

\displaystyle X_{\delta}:

\displaystyle=X_{K}(1+\overset{\circ}{X}(\delta))

\displaystyle W_{\delta}:

\displaystyle=X_{\delta}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta}(\delta)+X_{K}^{-1}W_{K})

\displaystyle V_{\delta}

\displaystyle:=\frac{\sigma^{2}_{\delta}-W^{2}_{\delta}}{X_{\delta}}\quad\text{where}\quad\sigma_{\delta}:=\rho(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma}(\delta)),

•

and the distribution function
f^{\delta}
given by

f^{\delta}(t,\phi,\rho,z,p^{\rho},p^{\phi},p^{z}):=\Phi(\varepsilon_{\delta},\ell_{z})\Psi_{\eta}(\rho-\rho_{1}((\varepsilon_{\delta},(\ell_{z})_{\delta}),(X_{\delta},W_{\delta},\sigma_{\delta})))

where

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\delta}

\displaystyle:=\frac{\sigma_{\delta}}{\sqrt{X_{\delta}}}(1+|p|^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}-\frac{W_{\delta}}{X_{\delta}}(\ell_{z})_{\delta},

\displaystyle(\ell_{z})_{\delta}

\displaystyle:=\sqrt{X_{\delta}}p^{\phi},

and
\rho_{1}
is the second largest solution of the equation

E_{(\ell_{z})_{\delta}}(X_{\delta},W_{\delta},\sigma_{\delta},\rho,0)=\varepsilon_{\delta}.

solve the Einstein-Vlasov system on
\mathcal{M}=\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{S}^{1}\times\mathscr{B}
.

It remains to prove the following

1.

The spacetime
(\mathcal{M},g_{\delta})
is
C^{2,\alpha}-
extendable to a black hole spacetime in the sense of Definition
4
,

2.

The extended solution
(\tilde{\mathcal{M}},\tilde{g}_{\delta})
is asymptotically flat.

First of all, we claim that

Lemma 90
.

there exists
\Omega_{\delta}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\left.\frac{W_{\delta}}{X_{\delta}}\right|_{\mathscr{H}}=-\Omega.

Proof.

We recall that
\forall\delta\geq 0
, we have

\partial_{z}\left(\frac{W_{\delta}}{X_{\delta}}\right)=\frac{\sigma_{\delta}}{X_{\delta}}\left(\partial_{\rho}Y_{\delta}+(B_{\rho})_{\delta}\right).

On the horizon, the right hand side vanishes, since
\sigma_{\delta}
vanishes. Therefore, there exists
\Omega_{\delta}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\forall z\in]-\beta,\beta[\quad,\quad\frac{W_{\delta}}{X_{\delta}}(0,z)=-\Omega_{\delta}

∎

We recall the following results from
[
17
, Section 13]

Proposition 35
.

Let
\delta\in[0,\delta_{0}[
. Then,

1.

\displaystyle\left.\left(e^{2\lambda}-\rho^{-2}X\right)\right|_{\mathscr{A}}=0
.

2.

there exists
\kappa(\delta)>0
such that
\displaystyle\left.\left(e^{2\lambda}-\kappa_{\delta}^{-2}\rho^{-2}\left(V-2\Omega_{\delta}W-\Omega_{\delta}^{2}X\right)\right)\right|_{\mathscr{H}}=0.

3.

On the set
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}
, we have

\left.\left((\chi^{2}+s^{2})e^{2\lambda}-s^{-2}X\right)\right|_{\left\{s=0\right\}\cup\left\{\chi=0\right\}}=0.

4.

On the set
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}
, we have

\left.\left((\chi^{2}+s^{2})e^{2\lambda}-\kappa_{\delta}^{-2}\chi^{-2}\left(V-2\Omega_{\delta}W-\Omega_{\delta}^{2}X\right)\right)\right|_{\left\{s=0\right\}\cup\left\{\chi=0\right\}}=0.

5.

On the set
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}}
, we have

\left.\left(((\chi^{\prime})^{2}+(s^{\prime})^{2})e^{2\lambda}-(s^{\prime})^{-2}X\right)\right|_{\left\{s^{\prime}=0\right\}\cup\left\{\chi^{\prime}=0\right\}}=0.

6.

On the set
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}}
, we have

\left.\left(((\chi^{\prime})^{2}+(s^{\prime})^{2})e^{2\lambda}-\kappa_{\delta}^{-2}(\chi^{\prime})^{-2}\left(V-2\Omega_{\delta}W-\Omega_{\delta}^{2}X\right)\right)\right|_{\left\{s^{\prime}=0\right\}\cup\left\{\chi^{\prime}=0\right\}}=0.

Lemma 91
.

Assume that
f(\rho,z)
is a smooth function on
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}
, resp.
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}}
, that is smooth when considered as a function on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
with the metric
d\rho^{2}+\rho^{2}d\mathbb{S}^{n-2}+dz^{2}
for
n>2
. Then,
f(\rho,z)=g(\rho^{2},z)
for some smooth function on
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}
, resp
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}}
.

If
f(s,\chi)
is a smooth function on
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}
, that is smooth when considered as a function on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
with the metric
ds^{2}+s^{2}d\phi_{1}^{2}+d\chi^{2}+\chi^{2}d\phi_{2}^{2}
for
n>2
. Then,
f(s,\chi)=g(s^{2},\chi)
for some smooth function on
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}
.

The above proposition and lemma, together with Proposition
4
yield the extendability of
(\mathcal{M},g_{\delta})
, for all
\delta\geq 0
. More precisely, we obtain the following result

Proposition 36
.

The spacetime
(\mathcal{M},g_{\delta})
is
C^{2,\alpha}-
extendable to a Lorentzian manifold with corners
(\tilde{\mathcal{M}},\tilde{g}_{\delta})
which is stationary and axisymmetric, and whose boundary corresponds to a bifurcate Killing event horizon.

Proof.

1.

We will only write the details for the extendability near the axis. The other extensions are obtained in the same way.

2.

By construction, all the metric coefficients are
C^{2,\alpha}
on
\mathscr{B}
.

3.

By Proposition
4
, the Kerr metric is
C^{2,\alpha}
(it is in fact
C^{\infty}
) extendable in the sense of Definition
4
.

4.

By Lemma
91
, in a neighbourhood of the axis of symmetry, we have

\displaystyle\overset{\circ}{X}(\delta)(\rho,z)

\displaystyle=\overset{\circ}{X}_{\delta,\mathscr{A}}(\rho^{2},z),

\displaystyle\overset{\circ}{\Theta}(\delta)(\rho,z)

\displaystyle=\overset{\circ}{\Theta}_{\delta,\mathscr{A}}(\rho^{2},z),

\displaystyle\overset{\circ}{\sigma}(\delta)(\rho,z)

\displaystyle=\overset{\circ}{\sigma}_{\delta,\mathscr{A}}(\rho^{2},z),

for
C^{2,\alpha}
functions,
\overset{\circ}{X}_{\delta,\mathscr{A}},\overset{\circ}{\Theta}_{\delta,\mathscr{A}},\overset{\circ}{\sigma}_{\delta,\mathscr{A}}
defined on
\tilde{\mathscr{A}}
.

5.

In
\tilde{\mathscr{A}}
, we have

\left.X_{\delta}\right|_{\tilde{\mathscr{A}}}=\rho^{2}X_{\mathscr{A}}(\rho^{2},z)(1+\overset{\circ}{X}(\delta)(\rho,z)),

where
X_{\mathscr{A}}(0,z)>0
. Since
\delta
is small so that
||\overset{\circ}{X}(\delta)||_{\mathcal{L}_{X}}<1
, then there exists a
C^{2,\alpha}
function
X_{\delta,\mathscr{A}}:\tilde{\mathscr{A}}\to\mathbb{R}
such that
X_{\delta,\mathscr{A}}(0,z)>0
and

\left.X_{\delta}\right|_{\tilde{\mathscr{A}}}(\rho,z)=\rho^{2}X_{\delta,\mathscr{A}}(\rho^{2},z).

Hence,
X_{\delta}
verifies the third point of Definition
4
.

6.

By (
10.1
), we have

W_{\delta}=X_{\delta}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta}(\delta)+X_{K}^{-1}W_{K})

and

V_{\delta}=\frac{\sigma^{2}_{\delta}-W^{2}_{\delta}}{X_{\delta}}\quad\text{where}\quad\sigma_{\delta}:=\rho(1+\overset{\circ}{\sigma}(\delta)),

Again, we use the extendability properties of
X_{K}
and
W_{K}
in order to obtain

\displaystyle\left.W_{\delta}\right|_{\tilde{\mathscr{A}}}

\displaystyle=\left.X_{\delta}\right|_{\tilde{\mathscr{A}}}(\overset{\circ}{\Theta}(\delta)(\rho,z)+X_{\mathscr{A}}(\rho^{2},z)^{-1}W_{\mathscr{A}}(\rho^{2},z))

\displaystyle=\rho^{2}X_{\delta,\mathscr{A}}(\rho^{2},z)(\overset{\circ}{\Theta}_{\delta,\mathscr{A}}(\rho^{2},z)+X_{\mathscr{A}}(\rho^{2},z)^{-1}W_{\mathscr{A}}(\rho^{2},z))

\displaystyle=\rho^{2}W_{\delta,\mathscr{A}}(\rho^{2},z).

Hence,
W_{\delta}
verifies the second point of Definition
4
.

7.

The first point follows from the definition of
V_{\delta}
.

8.

As for the fourth point, we apply the first point of Proposition
35
:

\displaystyle\left.\left(e^{2\lambda}-\rho^{-2}X\right)\right|_{\mathscr{A}}=0.

Finally, we apply Proposition
3
to conclude.
∎

Remark 30
.

The regularity of the metric coefficients depend on the regularity of the distribution function, which depends on the regularity of the profile
\Phi
. Moreover, the way one extends the resulting spacetimes to a larger spacetimes with event horizon depends on the regularity of the profile
\Phi
. Hence, if
\Phi
is
C^{k}
, then one can
C^{k+2,\alpha}
-extend.

Finally, we state the following result

Proposition 37
.

\forall\delta\in[0,\delta_{0}[
, the spacetime obtained by Proposition
36
,
(\tilde{\mathcal{M}},\tilde{g}_{\delta})
, is asymptotically flat in the sense of Definition
9
.

Appendix A
Coordinate Systems for asymptotic flatness

In this appendix, we recall from
[
16
, Appendix A]
the different change of coordinates used in order to define "spatial infinity". This allows us to define a notion of asymptotic flatness adapted to our work.

Assume that
(\mathcal{M},g)
is extendable to a regular black hole spacetime with corners
(\tilde{\mathcal{M}},\tilde{g})
.
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}
is given by

\tilde{\mathcal{M}}=\mathbb{R}_{t}\times(0,2\pi)_{\phi}\times\overline{\mathscr{B}}_{\rho,z},

where
\overline{\mathscr{B}}
is covered by
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}},\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}},\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}
and
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}}
. Let
K\subset\subset\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}
be a compact subset of
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{A}}
such that

\overline{\mathscr{B}_{H}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{N}}\cup\overline{\mathscr{B}_{S}}\nsubseteq\overline{\mathscr{B}}\backslash K.

We introduce the coordinates
(t,x,y,z)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
in the region
\mathbb{R}\times(0,2\pi)\times\overline{\mathscr{B}}\backslash K
defined by

\displaystyle x:=\rho\cos\phi,

(A.1)

\displaystyle y:=\rho\sin\phi.

Therefore, a stationary axisymmetric spacetime
(\mathcal{M},g)
is asymptotically flat if the
(t,x,y,z)
coordinates defined above, the metric
\tilde{g}
has the following expansion in the region
\mathbb{R}\times(0,2\pi)\times\overline{\mathscr{B}}\backslash K

\tilde{g}=\left(1+O\left(r^{-1}\right)\right)\left(-dt^{2}+dx^{2}+dy^{2}+dz^{2}\right)+O\left(r^{-2}\right)\left(dtdx+dtdy+dxdy\right).

Appendix B
Classical inequalities and estimates

Theorem 8

(
[
9
]
)
.

If
\delta<0
, then there exists
C>0
such that
\forall u\in W^{1,p}_{\delta}(\mathbb{R}^{3})

||u||_{p,\delta}\leq C||u_{r}||_{p,\delta-1},

where
u_{r}
is defined by

u_{r}:=\left<r\right>^{-1}(x,y,z)^{t}\cdot\partial u.

Lemma 92
.

Let
f\;:\;\mathbb{R}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}
be a
C^{2}
spherically symmetric function and
(\rho,\phi)
denote polar coordinates on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Then, for any point
(x_{0},y_{0})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
we have

\left(x_{0}^{2}+y_{0}^{2}\right)^{-1/2}\left.\partial_{\rho}f\right|_{(x,y)=(x_{0},y_{0})}=\left.\partial_{xx}f\right|_{(x,y)=\left(0,\left(x_{0}^{2}+y_{0}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\right)}

We recall Theorem
6.6
of
[
27
]
concerning Schauder estimates:

Theorem 9
.

Let
\Omega
be a
C^{2,\alpha}
domain in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and let
u\in C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega})
be a solution of
\displaystyle Lu=f\;\text{in}\;\Omega
, where
f\in C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega})
,
L
is an elliptic operator and the coefficients of
L
satisfy, for positive constants
\lambda
,
\Lambda
,

a^{ij}\xi_{i}\xi_{j}\geq\lambda|\xi|^{2}\quad\forall x\in\Omega,\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n},

and

|a^{ij}|_{0,\alpha;\Omega},|b^{i}|_{0,\alpha;\Omega},|c|_{0,\alpha;\Omega}\leq\Lambda.

Let
\phi(x)\in C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega})
and suppose
u=\phi
on
\partial\Omega
. Then

|u|_{2;\alpha;\Omega}\leq C(n,\alpha,\lambda,\Lambda,\Omega)\left(|u|_{0;\alpha;\Omega}+|\phi|_{0;\alpha;\Omega}+|f|_{0;\alpha;\Omega}\right).

We recall Theorem
10.3
of
[
34
]
concerning Schauder estimates for the Laplacian in
\mathbb{R}^{n}

Theorem 10

(Newtonian estimates)
.

Let
f\in C_{c}^{k,\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
with
k\in\mathbb{N},\;\alpha\in(0,1)
and let
K_{f}
be given by

K_{f}(x):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\,G(x,y)f(y)\,dy\quad\text{where }\quad G(x,y):=-\frac{1}{|x-y|^{n-2}}

Then,

K_{f}\in C^{k+2,\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{n})

and there exists
C=C(k,\alpha,n)>0

||K_{f}||_{C^{k+2,\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq C||f||_{C^{k,\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

Theorem 11

(Calderon-Zygmund estimates)
.

Let
f\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and let
K_{f}
be as defined in Theorem
10
. Then,
K_{f}
has weak second derivative in
L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and we have

||K_{f}||_{\dot{W}^{2,p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq C||f||_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

Appendix C
Classical Carter-Robinson theory

In the absence of a matter field, the metric quantities
X,W,\sigma,\theta,\lambda
satisfy the following equations on
\mathscr{B}
:

•

\sigma=\rho
. Hence,

\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\sigma=0.

•

\theta
is given by

\theta=\frac{X^{2}}{\sigma}(\partial_{z}(X^{-1}W)d\rho-\partial_{\rho}(X^{-1}W)dz).

•

W
satisfies the equation

\partial_{\rho}(X^{-1}W)d\rho+\partial_{z}(X^{-1}W)dz=\frac{\rho}{X^{2}}((\partial_{\rho}Y)dz-(\partial_{z}Y)d\rho).

•

(X,Y)
satisfies the following system on
\mathbb{R}^{3}

\left\{\begin{aligned} \rho^{-1}\partial_{\rho}(\rho\partial_{\rho}X)+\rho^{-1}\partial_{z}(\rho\partial_{z}X)&=\frac{(\partial_{\rho}X)^{2}+(\partial_{z}X)^{2}-(\partial_{\rho}Y)^{2}-(\partial_{z}Y)^{2}}{X},\\
\rho^{-1}\partial_{\rho}(\rho\partial_{\rho}Y)+\rho^{-1}\partial_{z}(\rho\partial_{z}Y)&=\frac{2(\partial_{\rho}Y)(\partial_{\rho}X)+2(\partial_{z}Y)(\partial_{z}X)}{X}.\end{aligned}\right.

(C.1)

•

\lambda
satisfies the equation

\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{\rho}\lambda&=\frac{1}{4}\rho{X}^{-2}((\partial_{\rho}X)^{2}-(\partial_{z}X)^{2}+(\partial_{\rho}Y)^{2}-(\partial_{z}Y)^{2})-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\rho}\log{X},\\
\partial_{\rho}\lambda&=\frac{1}{4}\rho{X}^{-2}((\partial_{\rho}X)(\partial_{z}X)+(\partial_{\rho}Y)(\partial_{z}Y))-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{z}\log{X}.\end{aligned}\right.

(C.2)

In particular
(X_{K},Y_{K},\sigma_{K},\theta_{K},\lambda_{K})
satisfies the above equations.
